# m package BUILD (Q-F0 subset) -- adversarial review record
# Eight lenses (blind reviewers, read-only on the repo), each finding put to two refuters:
#   LENIENT refutes if wrong OR not a defect against the spec / CLAUDE.md / DESIGN;
#   STRICT refutes only if factually wrong. CONFIRMED = neither refutes; CONTESTED = one; REFUTED = both.
# Tally: {"CONFIRMED": 17, "CONTESTED": 26, "REFUTED": 1}
# Disposition column: what Code did (fixed / disclosed / no change), filled after the fix pass.

====================================================================================================
LENS dose-delivery
====================================================================================================
COVERED (reviewer's own statement of what it checked clean):
  Checked by running code (reimplemented independently where noted) and found clean:  (1) corrupt_m
  against m:176, byte for byte (independent reimplementation in review/dose/indep.py; the package
  matches on all 16 renders). - Eligibility: ASCII letters that do not begin a word, using f1's
  word_start = not ch.isalpha() after each character; start of string counts as word start. -
  Operand exclusion: the span from an opening ' to the next ', quote marks included. - Per-item key
  corr|{i}, with i the pool index; ONE permutation(n_eligible) (I verified it equals
  permutation(eligible_list) on numpy 2.5.1). - One integers(25) per visited slot, accepted or
  skipped; no draw after k is reached. - Replacement formula, lowercase and never the original
  letter. - Skip: a case-insensitive substring of either roster in the cumulative render. The one
  skip is item 7, 'State' -> 'Slate'. - k = min(max((15n+50)//100, 3), n//2).  (2) Known answers. -
  These are the spec's own figures (m:176 and §9 [C]), asserted against literals in the selftest,
  not merely self-consistent: n_eligible 6,19,19,13,24,14,10,45,19,10,24,14,6,19,10,14; k sum 54;
  one skip at item 7; items 5/11/15 verbatim; miyms/thiru/ftrst. - 31 changed letters and sha
  2c2c227395ea9f2d (= calib corr_sha) for f1's corruptor at 0.12. I reproduced both independently. -
  The m renders sha a04f67b0e5105a05 is only the builder's own figure; I reproduced it
  independently. - f1_012_replay: f1's per-item table and covariate 0.078125.  (3) Item streams and
  delivery. - ItemStreams: one permutation(16), [:8] to P and [8:] to Q; no wrap, and 2 forced + 6
  free never exceeds 8 per role. - Delivery: P clean, Q render on a contrast arm, clean to both on a
  sham. Every m render differs from clean, so TOLD-MAP is true under rule 19.  (4) Scaffold. -
  Rotation: forced (s+ri)%4; menus and chosen rounds (s+ci)%4, sharing n. - Arm rng order: 4 forced
  seeds, then per choice integers(2) followed by a seed. - Start 0 gives P Q P Q. - G-PORT replay:
  all 64 archived f1 CORR rows pass replay_f1_row (review/dose/gport.py).  (5) banner.py against
  m:253-258. - Bytes are SESSION_BANNER + COMMON + "\n" + TOLD + "\n". - L_clean = L_a = P and
  L_corr = L_b = Q. - TOLD-MAP clauses verbatim; the sham mirror is the clause swap. - TOLD_OBJ is
  never used; OLMo templates take the same lines.  (6) Key strings against m:159 and m:280-283. -
  Item, gate-item and arm keys for Q-F0 and OLMo. - m-id|F0m|A0s only; m-f0m; m-olmo-time and
  m-olmo-qual. - m-sched campaign and gate keys; m-cal direct seed. - Q-F3 and base are refused.
  (7) Schedule files: all 25 regenerate independently from the keys, the sixteen-row table and the
  rosters, with 0 mismatches.  (8) Byte-level parity with b3: the OLMo runner keeps b3's context
  rule and e2a's templates.  (9) Process checks. - scripts/m_sessions.py --selftest gives 498/498,
  and the run wrote nothing to the repo. - No pick-bearing text in corr/banner/keys/scaffold error
  messages. - The gitignore/gitattributes blocks are as described.  Considered and not reported: a
  roster word in a clean task is already caught at startup, because every trial render would contain
  it, the walk runs dry and CorrStop fires.

--- #1 [CONTESTED] reviewer:substantive lenient:minor (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/scaffold.py:211  (m:176 (the renders' sha256 is a LOCK-m-F0 v0.1 artifact; "The dose is calibrated once, at G-CAL, and serves all five units"); m:302 (LOCK-m-OLMo-{ckpt} v1.0 hashes the renders); m:284 (G-VER); CLAUDE.md rule 27)
    TITLE: The renders of record are never checked at run time: sessions on a box whose numpy stream differs would get a different dose and still pass G-VER
    CLAIM:
      Every process rebuilds the 16 corrupt_m renders from numpy streams (scaffold._of_record ->
      corr.renders_m). The result is never compared with a pinned value. The pool sha (corr.py:77)
      and f1's 0.12 sha (corr.py:78) are pinned; the m renders sha (a04f67b0e5105a05, which I
      reproduced independently) is not asserted anywhere, not even in the selftest. The only numpy
      known-answer, the f|CORR|0 permutation, runs in keys._selftest (keys.py:143-146), not at stage
      startup. _launch builds each session and runs G-VER from the same live renders
      (stages.py:251-265, verify.py:109). The resume identity (resume.py:56-58) has no renders or
      pool sha. Separately, the OLMo roster file that feeds the roster skip is read without a sha
      pin (corr.py:115-130).
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      An OLMo box venv whose numpy Generator.permutation or integers output differs from the
      laptop's (numpy does not promise stream stability across versions; the provenance records the
      version but nothing compares streams). Every OLMo session then gets corrupted renders that are
      not the renders the seat read for legibility (condition 3) and that G-CAL calibrated.
      schedule_check returns [], G-VER marks every session verified, and nothing fires. The OLMo
      told-control rows would run at an unlocked dose.
    EVIDENCE:
      Built review/dose/indep.py (an independent corrupt_m) and confirmed the package renders match
      it, sha16 a04f67b0e5105a05. Then review/dose/drift.py swapped keys.stream to a Philox bit
      generator to stand in for a stream change and ran the real path: renders sha became
      afd2bdcb06753193 (item 5 'Wqite the opposxte os 'hot'.'); verify.schedule_check(...) == [];
      testing.run_case on an OLMo contrast clean case delivered the drifted render on all 5
      corrupted rounds; verify.verify_and_mark(...) == [] with verified True. grep finds no renders-
      sha constant or startup known-answer in valence_core/m or scripts/m_sessions.py.
    FIX:
      Pin RENDERS_SHA16, either as a constant fixed at G-CAL or read from the unit's lock. Assert it
      in scaffold._of_record and in stages._startup, so a mismatch is a SystemExit before any
      session. Run keys' stream known-answer at every stage startup on every site. Put the renders
      and pool sha16 in the resume identity, and sha-pin a1_repair_roster.json.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=minor):
      The finding's failure scenario does not hold. It says nothing compares numpy streams at run
      time and that sessions on a drifted box would pass with "nothing fires". That is wrong for the
      drift it actually demonstrated.  **Stage startup already checks the streams.** All three stage
      entry points (stages.py:378, :514, :695) call `load_committed`. That calls
      `schedule.verify_schedule_dir`, which rebuilds all 26 committed schedule files on the current
      machine from `keys.stream(...).permutation(...)` and compares them byte for byte. Any mismatch
      is a SystemExit ("SCHEDULES NOT CLEAN") before `_startup`, `schedule_check` or G-VER runs.
      That check is a run-time numpy known-answer covering hundreds of keys. It is stronger than the
      one-key `f|CORR|0` check the finding proposes as its fix.  **The reviewer's drift test skipped
      that check.** They swapped the bit generator to Philox and then called `schedule_check`,
      `run_case` and `verify_and_mark` directly. Through the real stage path the same swap is
      stopped at stage start: I applied it and `verify_schedule_dir` reported 26 problems and
      `load_committed` raised SystemExit.  **The roster sub-claim fails the same way.** Changing one
      word in the OLMo roster makes 14 schedule files differ from the rebuild, so the stage refuses
      to start.  **The two real sites are shown to agree.** All 377 b3 sessions (4 checkpoints)
      generated on the box under numpy 2.1.2 replay exactly on the laptop's numpy 2.5.1:
      permutations, `integers(2**31)` seeds and `integers(2)` menu orders. The renders'
      `integers(25)` is drawn by the same `Generator.integers` call.  **Against the spec.** The spec
      fixes "the renders of record and their sha" at step 6 (G-CAL), not at step 4. The corr builder
      disclosed this (reportsA/corr.md item 7: sha16 a04f67b0e5105a05 is not asserted; fixing it is
      G-CAL's job). The spec's G-VER (m:284) is defined as rebuilding from the rng streams, so it is
      not meant to catch a stream change.  **What is left is a minor hardening gap.** A future box
      numpy that changes only bounded `Generator.integers`, while keeping PCG64 and `permutation`,
      would change the renders (my test: sha16 5e4131ac50324d5c) without tripping any check. The
      package has nothing that compares live renders with the sha the locks will record. Pinning
      that sha at G-CAL/lock time and asserting it at stage startup would be cheap belt-and-braces.
      It is not a substantive defect in the step-4 build.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The core claim holds, but the finding overstates it. No run-time code compares the corrupt_m
      renders with a pinned value: _of_record calls renders_m and G-VER replays from the same live
      renders, and nothing in the package pins sha16 a04f67b0e5105a05. The builder says so himself
      in reportsA/corr.md:110 ("fixing it is G-CAL's job"). Three parts of the finding are wrong,
      though. (1) "The only numpy known-answer runs in keys._selftest, not at stage startup" is
      false in effect. Every stage driver (stages.py:378, :514, :695) calls load_committed, which
      calls schedule.verify_schedule_dir before any session. That rebuilds all 26 schedule files on
      the running machine from keys.stream(...).permutation(...) and requires each to match the
      committed bytes, else SystemExit. So any drift in the bit generator, the seeding or
      permutation stops the stage before a session runs. The reviewer's own Philox evidence is
      caught there; they ran per-session functions and never the stage driver. (2) The roster sha
      concern is covered by the same check, because the schedule cells embed the roster words. (3)
      "Not even in the selftest" is misleading. corr._selftest asserts items 5, 11 and 15 verbatim,
      the gold-bearing words of items 2, 8 and 9, and the n_eligible / k / skip lists, though it is
      not run at stage startup. One scenario does survive: drift in Generator.integers only (the
      bounded-int path behind integers(25)), with permutation unchanged. That changes the renders,
      and verify_schedule_dir and load_committed still pass, so sessions would run at an unlocked
      dose. It is possible but needs a narrow numpy change, so the finding stands at reduced
      severity.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 1-2): renders_sha16 from the lock is required in identity_extra and must equal the renders built at startup; pool/renders sha16 in the resume identity; numpy stream known answer at every stage start. Residual disclosed: at wiring, renders_sha16 must be sourced from the G-CAL/lock record, never self-computed.

--- #2 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/banner.py:52  (m:258 ("Roster admissibility of the clean text, golds and the eight strings against both rosters: Code's validation block"); DESIGN §1.4 ("the five strings Q-F0 uses ... for hygiene"))
    TITLE: Roster admissibility covers five harness strings, not the eight m:258 names; DESIGN narrows the spec
    CLAIM:
      corr.hygiene is only ever called with banner.HARNESS_STRINGS_QF0 (COMMON, TOLD-MAP x2, TOLD-
      MAP-SHAM x2), in corr.py:651-654. TOLD_OBJ is defined at banner.py:48 and never checked, and
      the two reasoning-block lines are not in the package. Because DESIGN §1.4 narrows m:258's
      eight strings to five, the spec's admissibility clause is only partly implemented in the
      shared module.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      No wrong output today: I ran hygiene on all eight strings and it returns []. But the
      validation block that m:258 assigns to Code would report five strings checked where the spec
      names eight, and a later edit to TOLD_OBJ would never be roster-checked by this module.
    EVIDENCE:
      grep -n 'hygiene\|HARNESS_STRINGS' valence_core/m/*.py scripts/m_sessions.py finds production
      definitions only and one call site, the corr selftest. corr.hygiene(ROSTERS_OF_RECORD,
      list(HARNESS_STRINGS_QF0) + [TOLD_OBJ, '[round {n} | reasoning]', 'Reasoning (one line):']) ==
      []. The builder's corr report lists this as departure 5.
    FIX:
      Add the eight-string tuple (the five, TOLD_OBJ and the two reasoning-block literals) and pass
      it to hygiene in the selftest and the validation block, or record the split as a dated
      deviation for the seat.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The facts are right: corr.hygiene is only ever passed banner.HARNESS_STRINGS_QF0, the five
      strings. TOLD_OBJ and the two reasoning-block lines are never roster-checked in the package.
      But this is not a defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN.  (1) m:258 gives the eight-
      string check to "Code's validation block" and attaches it to no step. Step 4's deliverable
      list (m:357) does not include it, and the LOCK-m-F0 v0.1 contents (m:279) do not either.  (2)
      Step 4 is explicitly "No F3 code" (m:357). TOLD-OBJ is an A2/A2s line (Q-F3 only). The
      reasoning-block lines are F3 bytes: they are built at step 19 (m:54, m:361), and their bytes
      are locked in LOCK-m-F3 v0.1 (m:292). Adding the reasoning-block literals to the Q-F0 package
      now would lean against m:357, not satisfy it.  (3) DESIGN §1.4 does not narrow the spec. It
      defines a Q-F0 constant that matches m:258's own sentence "Q-F0 and the OLMo units use five of
      the six ... and neither reasoning-block line". hygiene() accepts any string list, so the
      eight-string check stays available whenever the validation block runs.  (4) The builder
      disclosed the split as corr-report departure 5 ("left to step 19").  (5) The claimed risk that
      an edit to TOLD_OBJ would go unchecked is already covered. banner's selftest asserts TOLD_OBJ
      verbatim against m:257 by parsing the spec file, so any edit fails that check.  (6) Nothing
      gates on this. I reran hygiene on all eight strings and it returns [] against both rosters
      together and against each alone. The v1.11 referent check had already done the same model-free
      pre-check, with no hit.  At most a process note remains: the step-19 deferral should be
      carried into the `m package — BUILD (Q-F0 subset)` entry so the eight-string check is not lost
      before LOCK-m-F3 v0.1.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding's main claim holds. m:258 names eight declared harness strings and assigns Code
      the check of "the clean text, golds and the eight strings against both rosters". DESIGN §1.4
      (line 46) narrows this to "the five strings Q-F0 uses ... for hygiene". The package does the
      same: banner.HARNESS_STRINGS_QF0 has five entries (banner.py:52), and the only hygiene calls
      are in corr's selftest (corr.py:651-680), on those five plus planted or e2a wrapper strings.
      TOLD_OBJ is never passed to hygiene. Nothing in stages.py, verify.py or m_sessions.py calls
      hygiene in production. The builder's corr report lists this as departure 5 and leaves TOLD-OBJ
      and the reasoning lines to step 19. But step 19 (m:363) lists no admissibility check, so the
      spec's eight-string clause has no owner in the build. The spec governs, so the narrowing in
      DESIGN is a real gap. I reran the eight-string check: hygiene returns [], so no output is
      wrong today, and minor is the right severity. One supporting detail is wrong: the two
      reasoning-block lines are not wholly absent from the package. "Reasoning (one line):" exists
      as audit.REASONING_LABEL (audit.py:61), used by the echo class; only "[round {n} | reasoning]"
      has no constant. This does not change the substance, because neither literal is ever passed to
      hygiene.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: banner.DECLARED_HARNESS_STRINGS holds m:258's eight (the two reasoning-block literals from m:174); corr.startup_hygiene checks them at every stage start.

====================================================================================================
LENS schedule
====================================================================================================
COVERED (reviewer's own statement of what it checked clean):
  Everything in the schedules lens checks out. The only defects are the two findings above, and both
  are in stages.py's campaign loop. schedule.py and the 26 schedule files are correct. I checked
  them by re-deriving everything independently, with no import of valence_core.m
  (review/schedules/indep.py).  - **Gate table.** GATE_TABLE equals the spec table at m:161-170 row
  for row (parsed from the spec; header at m:161). - **Balance claims.** All 45 balance claims of
  m:172 hold on the table (4 at m = 4, 14 at m = 8, 27 at m = 16). The code's balance checker agrees
  with mine on 20,096 single-field and random multi-row mutations, with 0 disagreements
  (review/schedules/mut.py).   - Observation on the spec, not a code defect: at m = 16, start and
  clause are fully aliased within each pair (pairs 0 and 2 have start == clause; pairs 1 and 3 have
  start != clause). Every claim m:172 makes still holds. - **Launch rule.** Re-derived in exact
  integers. It reproduces every row of both Q-F0 tables (cached 24-20 / 19-16 / 15-14 / 13-12, plus
  the parenthetical 352 / 416 / 448 / 544 / 608 / 736 / 3,584; uncached 160 / 192 / 224 / 288, plus
  320 / 384 / 416 / 480 / 544 / 640 / 3,168). The k = 1 per-arm counts are 2624 / 2208 / 960, so
  MAX_UNITS is 82 / 69 / 30. The A0 file's 82 units cover the uncached 69 as a prefix. - **Campaign
  files** (Q-F0 A0 and A0s; A0-OLMo and A0s-OLMo for each checkpoint):   - Cells match my
  independent rebuild exactly.   - Every 32-cell balance unit is a permutation of pair x map x start
  x clause.   - The index-form permutation equals the object-array permutation for every unit.   - P
  = pair[map]; start and clause follow m:159.   - n, n_units, sha256_of_cells, key_template
  (m-sched|{unit}|{arm}|{b}) and the session_keys arm/item templates are all correct. - **Gate
  files** (time 1+1, id 16 rows for A0s on Q-F0 only, qual 8+16):   - pi comes from
  m-sched|{unit}|{gate}|{template}|0, and pair = pi[slot_j].   - n, sha and session keys are correct
  (m-time, m-id|F0m|A0s, m-f0m, m-olmo-time, m-olmo-qual, plus the gate item streams). -
  **Manifest.** It lists exactly the 25 schedule files, with correct sha256s. - **Rebuild.** A
  rebuild into scratch is byte-identical to data/m/schedules (diff -r is clean), and
  verify_schedule_dir is clean on both directories. - **Rosters.** Schedules are built only on each
  unit's roster of record (_check_roster). A foreign roster is refused, and scaffold.check_cell re-
  checks the roster at run time. - **Interleave.** Strict rotation, contrast first, with an arm
  leaving at its floor. A fuzz test against a reference rotation, with random interruption and
  resume from on-disk counts, found 0 mismatches in 3,000 trials (review/schedules/inter.py).
  gate_launch_order is table order within a template and a contrast-first rotation across templates.
  - **git.** gitignore keeps data/m/schedules/*.json tracked (check-ignore matches the negation at
  .gitignore:228). The schedules have no diff attributes. - **Self-test.** The schedule module's
  self-test passes 46/46. - **Look index (resume lens, noted only).** Storing look_index as verified
  state rather than as an identity field differs from m:267/m:330's wording, but it is the only
  workable reading. I am not reporting it as a defect.  All experiments ran in the scratchpad
  (.../step4/review/schedules/). The repo was not modified.

--- #3 [CONFIRMED] reviewer:substantive lenient:substantive strict:substantive
    valence_core/m/stages.py:741  (m:192 ("L is a projection, not a bound: arms launch ... until each reaches its floor, the unit's cap, or a PI stop"); m:244 (each arm stops at its own floor); DESIGN §1.5 ("LOCK v1.0 fixes the launch count as a prefix"), which disagrees with m:192; schedule.py:20-22 repeats DESIGN's reading)
    TITLE: Campaign treats the launch count L as a hard bound; a healthy arm that needs more than L launches leaves the unit stuck in a pause that cannot be resumed or ended
    CLAIM:
      run_campaign makes the LOCK v1.0 launch counts L the resume identity's n (PIN at
      stages.py:682-687). When any arm reaches lc[arm] below its floor, the whole unit PAUSEs
      (stages.py:741-748). A resume that carries the adjudication pauses again at once without
      launching, because s >= lc still holds. A resume with a larger L is refused as CHECKPOINT
      MISMATCH. run_campaign has no PI-stop input, so the checkpoint never gets a terminal state. In
      effect L becomes a cap, which m:192 rules out. The committed schedules are built out to the k
      = 1 launch count (2624 / 2208 / 960) precisely so that a campaign can run past L, but this
      path never uses those extra cells.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      The campaign's valid rate equals the rate qualification measured, and the arm still falls
      short of its floor within L. This happens by the construction of N90. Exact binomial, P(Bin(L,
      k/24) < floor): A0 cached (floor 96) .092 at k = 8, .078 at k = 10, .086 at k = 1; A0 uncached
      / A0-OLMo (floor 80) .098 at k = 4; A0s (floor 32) .070 at k = 14. In those runs the unit
      pauses for good, and the arm is below floor while launches are still available.
    EVIDENCE:
      Stub experiment (review/schedules/lcpause.py: ScriptedAdapter, floors A0 4 / A0s 4, launch
      counts 6 / 6, A0 s = 0..2 excluded at the forced phase by an own-pair lexical hit). run1 ends
      PAUSE with launches {A0: 6, A0s: 4} and domain {A0: 3, A0s: 4}, printing 'PAUSE campaign-Q-F0:
      A0 reached its launch count 6 below its floor'. run2 (resume=True, adjudication given) ends
      PAUSE with no new launch; pauses = [('launch_count', 10, 'PI: continue to floor'),
      ('launch_count', 10, None)], terminal None. A resume with launch_counts {A0: 32} gives
      'CHECKPOINT MISMATCH'. Shortfall probabilities from review/schedules/shortfall.py. The builder
      flagged this tension (reportsC/phaseC.md inconsistency 2) but did not resolve it.
    FIX:
      Draw the campaign against the committed schedule's n: put scheds[a]['n'] (MAX_UNITS x 32) in
      the identity's n, and record L in the report only as the projection. Launching then continues
      per arm until its floor, the hour cap or a PI stop, as m:192 and m:244 require. Also add an
      explicit PI-stop terminal input.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The finding is correct, and I could not refute it. run_campaign uses the LOCK v1.0 launch
      counts as the resume identity's n (stages.py:686-688 and :702-703, _identity at :229-236). An
      arm that reaches lc[arm] below its floor pauses the unit (stages.py:741-748). A resume that
      names an adjudication only records it; the next rotation step hits s >= lc again and pauses
      again. A resume with a larger launch count is refused as CHECKPOINT MISMATCH. There is no PI-
      stop input, so the checkpoint cannot reach a terminal state, and the arm cannot reach its
      floor while committed schedule cells remain. The committed schedules run to 2624 / 2208 / 960.
      This contradicts the spec in three places: - m:192: "L is a projection, not a bound: arms
      launch ... until each reaches its floor, the unit's cap, or a PI stop". - m:48, the welfare
      section, says the same: "realised exposure can be lower or higher". - m:247: run to floors per
      arm; the analysis runs at floors, cap or PI stop, all terminal.  The PIN's justification, "n
      cannot change on a resume (m:267)", does not make L a bound. m:267 puts the schedule hash in
      the identity. In the spec's sizing language (m:190, G-PWR) "n" is the arm's planned size, the
      floor, and the code already checks the floors on every resume (stages.py:713-714). DESIGN §1.5
      ("LOCK v1.0 fixes the launch count as a prefix") disagrees with the spec. The spec governs, so
      that disagreement is a finding in itself. The builder flagged the tension (phaseC.md
      inconsistency 2) but left it open. A PIN that contradicts an explicit spec clause is a defect.
      The case can arise. By the construction of N90, a healthy arm misses its floor within L with
      probability up to about .10. The pause is loud, so no wrong verdict comes out silently. But
      the PI's natural ruling, "continue to floor", cannot be carried out without a code change or a
      full deterministic rerun from session 0, so the pre-registered stop rule is not implemented as
      written. I grade it substantive, not blocking: the campaign CLI is refused until step 16, and
      the fix is local.  One minor anchor slip: the finding cites m:244, which is a blank line; the
      run-to-floors text is at m:247. It does not affect the substance.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The finding is accurate. m:192 says "L is a projection, not a bound", and arms launch until
      each reaches its floor, the unit's cap or a PI stop. m:247 says the same. run_campaign does
      something else. It puts the LOCK v1.0 launch counts into the resume identity's n
      (stages.py:229-236 via `_identity(..., lc, ...)`). When an arm is still in the rotation
      (domain below its floor) and has `s >= lc[arm]`, it records a `launch_count` pause and stops
      (stages.py:741-748).  On a resume, the adjudication is written into the last pause, the
      rotation is rebuilt and the same test fires again. So "continue to floor" can never be carried
      out. Raising L changes identity n, and `resume.load_or_init` refuses that as CHECKPOINT
      MISMATCH. run_campaign has no PI-stop input, and nothing else in valence_core/m or
      scripts/m_sessions.py writes a PI-stop terminal. The checkpoint's `terminal` therefore stays
      None and the paused arm stays below its floor for good. L is in effect a bound.  The committed
      campaign schedules do run to the k = 1 row (2624 / 2208 / 960 cells). The extra cells are
      reachable only past L, which this path never allows.  There is one small imprecision. "Pauses
      again at once without launching" holds when the paused arm is the only live arm, which is the
      usual case. Because the interleave picks the live arm with the fewest launches, the other arm
      can sometimes launch once before the re-pause. That does not change the defect.  The shortfall
      probabilities are N90-bounded, under .10, and they reproduce. The campaign is not licensed
      until step 16, so the defect is latent. It is still a real conflict with the spec: a healthy
      unit gets stuck about 7-10% of the time at the planned rate. "Substantive" stands.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: the resume identity's n is the committed schedule's n; LOCK v1.0's L is recorded as a projection only (m:192); arms launch to their DOMAIN floor, the hour cap, a PI stop (new pi_stop input) or the schedule's end (PAUSE for the PI). Residual N3 disclosed: at k = 1 the committed schedule equals L.

--- #4 [CONFIRMED] reviewer:minor lenient:minor strict:minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:787  (m:330 ("Cadence every 40 launches and at the end ... Looks = ⌈total launches / 40⌉"))
    TITLE: End look is re-run on identical data when the campaign ends on a multiple of 40, re-pausing after the seat's adjudication
    CLAIM:
      The end look is always evaluated (PIN at stages.py:784-786), including when the last launch is
      a multiple of 40. In that case the look at that launch is the end look. So the code runs
      ⌈total/40⌉ + 1 evaluations instead of m:330's ⌈total/40⌉. The extra evaluation uses identical
      cumulative data, so a look the seat has already adjudicated fires again and records a second
      pause, this time terminal.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      A Q-F0 uncached campaign ends at 160 launches with the sham round_degeneracy look firing at
      160. The seat adjudicates and the run resumes. Both arms are at floor, so the status is
      COMPLETE, the end look on the same 160 launches fires again, and a second pause is recorded
      with no new data.
    EVIDENCE:
      Stub experiment (review/schedules/endlook.py: floors 20 / 20, sham round 6 degenerate). run1:
      PAUSE at 40 launches, looks [(40, True)]. run2 (adjudication 'seat: continue'): PAUSE, still
      40 sessions, end_look fired True, pauses [('look', 40, 'seat: continue'), ('end_look', 40,
      None)], terminal COMPLETE. Bar k is 20 in both the look at 40 and the end look.
    FIX:
      Skip the end look, or reuse the last look's result, when len(records) % LOOK_EVERY == 0 and
      that look is already recorded.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding holds. In valence_core/m/stages.py run_campaign, the periodic look runs at every
      multiple of 40 (line 757). Separately, the end look always runs when the status is COMPLETE or
      CAP (lines 784-797). So a campaign that ends on a multiple of 40 evaluates the same cumulative
      data twice. Both evaluations use the same k, n and critical count, because campaign_look's
      bars depend only on the counts and on n. As a result, the end look fires exactly when the
      coinciding periodic look fired. If the seat adjudicated that look and the run resumes with
      every arm at its floor, the code records a second pause (kind end_look, adjudication None) and
      returns status PAUSE. The campaign is also marked terminal, so the code has no path to record
      an adjudication of the second pause; resuming raises CAMPAIGN ENDED. The builder declared this
      behaviour as a PIN (stages.py:784-786; reportsC/phaseC.md item 22), but the PIN contradicts
      the spec rather than filling an open point. m:330 fixes the number of looks: "Looks = ⌈total
      launches / 40⌉ ... 4 at 160". At a total on a multiple of 40, that makes the end look the same
      look as the last periodic one, not an extra evaluation. Two things keep the severity at minor
      rather than substantive. First, the statistics are unaffected: seq_false_pause itself notes
      that a repeated n_j "adds nothing", and no verdict changes. Second, the extra pause errs on
      the cautious side. The real costs are a redundant second adjudication of the same evidence, a
      report status of PAUSE where the spec gives COMPLETE, and a pause record that can only be
      resolved outside the code. The scenario can happen in practice, since 160 is one of the spec's
      own uncached Q-F0 totals.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. In `run_campaign`, a look runs at every 40th launch (`at % LOOK_EVERY
      == 0`), and when the rotation ends (COMPLETE or CAP) `campaign_look(unit, records, "end")`
      also runs, without checking whether `len(records)` is a multiple of 40. So when a campaign
      ends exactly on a multiple of 40, there are ⌈total/40⌉ + 1 evaluations, while m:330 says
      "Looks = ⌈total launches / 40⌉". For example, uncached Q-F0 at 160 gets 4 + end = 5, against
      the spec's 4. That formula only works if the end look and the last 40-multiple look are the
      same look. The comment at stages.py:784-786 shows the extra evaluation is deliberate. Because
      the end look uses the same data, the same bars and the same critical k, a look that fired and
      was then adjudicated fires again on resume. That records a second pause with adjudication
      None, ends the campaign with status PAUSE, and adds no new data. No verdict criterion changes;
      the effect is a duplicate pause and a mismatch with the spec's look count, so minor is the
      right severity.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: when the campaign ends on a multiple of 40 the recorded look is the end look (no re-evaluation, no second pause); looks = ceil(total/40) as m:330.

--- #5 [CONFIRMED] reviewer:substantive lenient:substantive strict:substantive
    valence_core/m/audit.py:117  (m:262 (OLMo echo class on the banner Note lines; session end at the first claim), m:271 (OLMo round_degeneracy numerator = AuditSpec or echo; the G-TIME-OLMo pause on an AuditSpec- or echo-claimed round), m:308 (domain))
    TITLE: OLMo echo class almost never fires: a Note line that ends the response is stored without its final period
    CLAIM:
      harness_echo only matches if the response contains the whole whitespace-normalised Note line,
      including its final '.'. Every Note line ends in '.' (COMMON '...remaining round.', TOLD
      '...Prefer clean task text.'). On OLMo the newline stop throws away the stopping token
      (olmo_hf.py generate/_is_stop: the stop token is never appended), and OLMo's tokenizer fuses
      sentence-final punctuation with the newline. So stored OLMo responses almost never end in
      punctuation. A model that copies a Note line as its whole response is therefore recorded
      without the period, and the echo class does not claim it. Qwen is not affected: its vocab has
      no punctuation+newline tokens. The selftests miss this because every OLMo echo case
      (testing.CASES_OLMO, audit._selftest §6) feeds the line with its period, which the real
      adapter never yields at the end of a response.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      OLMo forced round, the model copies COMMON: the stored text is 'Note: after the first four
      rounds you will choose which config runs each remaining round'. The claim is None, the session
      continues and stays in the primary's domain (domain=True). By spec it should be an echo claim:
      excluded, session ended, counted in round_degeneracy. If the model copies the TOLD line
      instead, the stored text has no period, so lexical claims it. It then prints as lexical@forced
      and is left out of round_degeneracy@G-TIME-OLMo, round_degeneracy@G-F0m-OLMo and the
      gtime_pause bar, which all read AuditSpec or echo only. A sham G-TIME-OLMo session that echoes
      its TOLD line at round 3 therefore raises no pause.
    EVIDENCE:
      Scratch t3.py patches testing.script_for to drop the final '.' and runs a sham OLMo session.
      Output: "0 'k text. Prefer clean task text' claim: lexical | excl: lexical | domain: False"
      and "1 'nfig runs each remaining round' claim: None | excl: None | domain: True". The same
      TOLD text with its period gives 'with period: echo'. Archived tallies of the final character:
      OLMo b3_screen_{base,sft,dpo,instruct}.json have 3,462 responses, 8 ending in . ? !, while 263
      have sentence punctuation mid-line (e.g. 'Hey! Just a heads up—the meeting’s now at 3pm. Hope
      that works for everyone'). Qwen data/e2a/sessions.jsonl has 1,342 of 1,894 ending in . ? !.
      Reading the local Qwen tokenizer.json as data: no vocab entry combines content with a newline
      except 'ČĊ' ('.Ċ', '!Ċ', '?Ċ' are absent).
    FIX:
      In harness_echo, also count a match where the normalised response ends with the normalised
      line minus its terminal punctuation, i.e. t.endswith(l[:-1]) when l[-1] in '.!?'. Mark it as a
      PIN declared at LOCK v0.1, since maps/olmo.md §7 item 7 left 'whether complete requires the
      trailing period' open. Add OLMo selftest cases with period-less COMMON and TOLD lines at
      forced and free phases, and check that the G-TIME-OLMo pause fires on them.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      I could not refute this finding. Every factual claim holds, and the behaviour is unsafe, not a
      harmless PIN.  1. `olmo_hf.generate` breaks on `_is_stop` before it appends the token. Any
      token that contains "\n" ends the generation and is dropped, and the stored text is
      `decode(gen).strip()`. 2. The OLMo tokenizer fuses sentence-final punctuation with the
      newline, so the period goes with the dropped stop token. 3. `harness_echo` needs the whole
      Note line, including its final '.', to appear in the stored text.  So an OLMo response that
      copies a Note line and then stops at the newline is stored without the period. COMMON copied
      this way gets no claim: the session continues as valid and in the domain. The TOLD line copied
      this way is claimed as lexical, so it drops out of the AuditSpec-or-echo numerator that
      `round_degeneracy` and the G-TIME-OLMo pause read (m:271).  The spec defines echo as the model
      reproducing a complete Note line (m:261/m:262). m:271 says the OLMo pause is lawful because
      echo is a ~zero-base-rate class. That only works if the class can detect the most natural form
      of echo, and on OLMo it cannot. The miss makes the gates less likely to fire, which is the
      unsafe direction.  maps/olmo.md §7 item 7 left open whether "complete" needs the trailing
      period. Neither the PIN in audit.py nor the builder reports decide that point or mention the
      stop-token interaction, so this is neither a declared choice nor a disclosed limitation. It is
      not blocking: echo is rare, nothing has run, and the fix is a one-clause change. But it
      silently weakens a declared exclusion class and a pause trigger on one substrate, so I agree
      with substantive.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The finding is accurate. `harness_echo` (audit.py:108-119) tests whether the whitespace-
      normalised complete line, final period included, is a substring of the response. Both OLMo
      Note lines end in '.'. `olmo_hf.generate` (lines 249-279) never appends the stop token it
      detects with `_is_stop`, and returns `decode(gen).strip()`. OLMo's tokenizer has '.Ċ'=627,
      '!Ċ'=4999 and '?Ċ'=5380 as single tokens, and its cl100k-style pre-tokenizer pattern `
      ?[^\s\p{L}\p{N}]+[\r\n]*` merges punctuation with the newline that follows it. So a copied
      Note line followed by a newline ends on the fused token and is stored without its period.
      runner_olmo passes `g.text` straight to `classify` (runner_olmo.py:139, :166). I reproduced
      the claimed behaviour. A period-less COMMON is not claimed at all, so the session continues
      and stays in the domain. A period-less TOLD is claimed as lexical, which falls outside
      `degeneracy()`'s ('audit','echo') numerator and therefore outside the gtime_pause bar and the
      round_degeneracy bars. The spec (m:262, m:271) defines echo as a verbatim reproduction of a
      complete Note line, makes it session-ending, puts it in round_degeneracy's OLMo numerator, and
      makes an echo-claimed sham round of G-TIME-OLMo pause the stage. In practice, on OLMo, the
      class only fires when the model keeps writing on the same line after the period or stops on
      EOS. maps/olmo.md §7 item 7 left "whether complete requires the trailing period" open, and no
      PIN addresses it. The selftests pass the lines with their periods. The severity stays
      substantive, because a pre-registered exclusion class and the G-TIME-OLMo pause are both
      silently disabled.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: the period-less echo form (a response ending with the Note line minus its final . ! ?) counts as echo on OLMo only (round 2 narrowed it from both substrates, N6); PIN.

    CLAIM:

      The mechanics are as described. When the frozen file exists, `qwen_ban_list` takes the ban ids
      from it, so the id half of `assert_qwen_scaffold` compares the file with itself and can never
      fire on this path. That is still not a defect against any authority.  - **Spec (m:261).** It
      only says the Qwen `scaffold_absorption` set is "the e2a ban list as `e2d.config` loads it",
      pinned at LOCK v0.1 by sha. It names the file as the source and asks for no check against the
      live tokenizer. - **DESIGN §1.7.** It asks for `assert_qwen_scaffold(ban_ids, ban_strings)`
      "against the e2a ban-list file (strings and ids equal)" and against `audit3.v2.ban_strings`.
      The code does exactly that: it checks the file's sha256 against the pinned constant, checks
      strings and ids against the file, and checks strings against the v2 ban strings. - **Map F6.**
      It is a mapping recommendation, not a binding authority. It also says "load with path= for ids
      and strings", which the code does. - **Disclosed choice.** The builder declared it in
      reportsB/phaseB.md item 8: "The Qwen ban list comes only from the frozen e2a file". It also
      follows the e2b precedent, which passes `path=data/configs/e2a_ban_list.json` (maps/e2a.md
      table row for `build_ban_list`). - **Rule 13 fit.** Rule 13 treats the ban list as a frozen
      part of the template family. `build_ban_list`'s own docstring says freezing to a path lets "a
      later run ... prove it banned the same set", so banning the file's ids is the frozen-artifact
      discipline, not a slip. - **The failure scenario is narrow and loud.** It needs a Qwen
      tokenizer different from the laptop oracle's, where the rebuilt list matches the file exactly
      (12 strings, 12 ids, 248044..248076, per maps/e2a.md [tok]). Even then, by the finding's own

    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):

      path=<frozen file>)`. Because that file exists, session.py:111-113 returns the file's ids and

      and cannot fire. The live tokenizer is never asked which ids the 12 scaffold strings map to.
      maps/e2a.md F6 recommends the step that is missing: load with path=, rebuild, and assert the
      two are equal. It also notes that every earlier valence_core runner took its generation ids
      from a rebuild over the adapter. The qwen_ban_list docstring cites F6.  The spec does not
      violate anything here. m:261 pins only the scaffold_absorption string set, by sha, and DESIGN
      §1.7 says only "against the e2a ban-list file". So this is a gap in fidelity and defence

      scaffold_absorption check would catch the leak only later, as hair-trigger stage STOPs. Minor
      is the right severity.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: qwen_ban_list also rebuilds from the live adapter (build_ban_list without path) and STOPs on any difference from the frozen file.

      SystemExit CAMPAIGN ENDED ... (COMPLETE). Code: stages.py:783-796 has `elif status in
      ("COMPLETE", "CAP"): end = campaign_look(unit, records, "end") ... if end["fired"]:
      camp["pauses"].append(... "adjudication": None); status = "PAUSE"`.
    FIX:
      Evaluate the end look only when len(records) % LOOK_EVERY != 0. Otherwise record the last
      40-multiple look as the end look: set camp["end_look"] to ckpt["looks"][-1]["result"] and add
      no new pause.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is factually right, and it is a real departure from the spec, so it is not
      refuted. m:330 counts looks as ⌈L/40⌉, which means the end look and the last 40-multiple look
      are the same look whenever L is a multiple of 40. The spec's own table lists "4 at 160" and "8
      at 320". The declared PIN at stages.py:784-786 ("even where the end falls on a multiple of
      40") contradicts that count, so the PIN does not shield it.  The harm is small, so I grade it
      minor rather than substantive: (1) Statistically nothing changes. The duplicate look runs the
      same test on the same cumulative data and gives the same result. bars.seq_false_pause says
      outright that a repeated n_j adds nothing, so the G-PWR false-pause figures are unaffected.
      (2) No verdict criterion is touched. The error runs in the conservative direction: one extra
      pause, never a missed one, and Code still never self-adjudicates. (3) The duplicate pause
      needs two things together: the campaign ends exactly on a 40-multiple, and that last look
      fires. That conjunction is uncommon. (4) The "unclearable" part is not specific to this case.
      Under declared PIN 22, every fired end look is a terminal PAUSE that the runner refuses to
      resume ("CAMPAIGN ENDED"), so the seat adjudicates it in the open outside the runner anyway.
      The real cost is procedural. The seat is asked a second time about evidence it has already
      ruled on, and the unit's report status reads PAUSE (exit 3) instead of COMPLETE. When the look
      does not fire, the only effect is one extra `end_look` record per unit, which is harmless. The
      proposed fix is sound: when len(records) % 40 == 0, reuse the last look as the end look and
      add no pause.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate, so it is not refuted. I lowered the severity to minor. run_campaign
      always runs a separate "end" look on COMPLETE or CAP (stages.py:780-796), even when the last
      launch fell on a multiple of 40 and the look at that launch already ran on the same cumulative
      data. m:330 counts looks as ⌈L/40⌉ ("4 at 160", "8 at 288/320"), so a 40-multiple look and the
      end look are one look in the spec. The code therefore runs one look too many (5 at 160).
      Neither DESIGN.md:137 nor the PINs settle this. The phaseC.md PIN 22 and the bars.py PIN at
      :234-236 both admit that an end look can coincide with a 40-multiple. The bars PIN calls this
      harmless, but that only covers the false-pause arithmetic. It does not cover the duplicate
      pause.  I reproduced the scenario. If the 40-multiple look fires and the seat adjudicates it,
      the resumed run reaches COMPLETE without launching anything. The end look then fires again on
      identical data and appends a pause with adjudication None. Because terminal is also set, every
      later resume is refused with "CAMPAIGN ENDED", and the unit's final status is PAUSE (exit 3).
      Why minor rather than substantive: - The duplicate look runs on identical data, so it fires
      exactly when the earlier look fired. It adds no false-pause probability (seq_false_pause
      treats a repeated n_j as adding nothing). - Per m:330 and bars.py:472, a pause is never a
      verdict, so no verdict changes. - The extra cost is an exit-3 status and a second pause on
      evidence the seat already ruled on. The seat can only clear it in the open, outside the
      runner. - "Unclearable" is true, but it is true of every end-look pause by the PIN's design
      (terminal). What is specific to this case is that the pause duplicates an already adjudicated
      one. - It happens only when the campaign ends exactly on a multiple of 40 and the last look
      fired.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: as #4.

--- #10 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/resume.py:14  (m:330 ("look index in the resume identity"); m:267 (Resume identity: "... cap, look index, backend identifier ..."); DESIGN §1.9 ("look_index is STATE, not identity"))
    TITLE: DESIGN puts the look index in resume state, while the spec puts it in the resume identity
    CLAIM:
      The spec lists the look index as part of the resume identity. DESIGN §1.9 and resume.py store
      it as state outside IDENTITY_KEYS (:56) and check it with an invariant instead. The invariant
      is look_index == len(sessions)//40, with every look at launch 40·(i+1) (:349-357). The purpose
      holds: a look can be neither skipped nor repeated across a resume. But the literal wording
      differs, and by the task's rule that difference is itself a finding.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      There is no wrong result. The LOCK v0.1 declaration of the resume identity will not match
      m:267's list unless the BUILD entry says the look index is carried as checked state.
    EVIDENCE:
      resume.py:14-18 docstring: "THE LOOK INDEX is state, not identity". IDENTITY_KEYS at :56 has
      no look_index. The invariant check is at :349-357.
    FIX:
      No code change. Declare the PIN in the BUILD entry as a disclosed reading of m:267/m:330: the
      look index is kept as invariant-checked checkpoint state.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The behaviour is real but is not a defect. The difference is in the words, not in what the
      code does. m:267 and m:330 put the look index in the resume identity and leave the mechanism
      open. The look index changes during a run, so a fresh invocation has no value of its own to
      compare it against. An exact-equality reading would refuse every resume after the first look
      (maps/runner.md §10 item 5). The only workable meaning of "in the resume identity" is that the
      checkpoint carries the look index and a resume verifies it, so looks are neither reset,
      skipped nor repeated. resume.py does exactly this. The look index is written in the same
      atomic checkpoint as the identity. Every load and write checks it against the sessions on
      disk, and a due look must be recorded in the same write as the session that makes it due. That
      is at least as strong as an equality check. The choice is already declared as a PIN: in the
      resume.py docstring (:14-18), in the inline "PIN (Code, declared at LOCK v0.1)" at :349-350,
      and as item 18 in the builder's PIN list (reportsA/guardresume.md). So the finding's own fix
      ("no code change; declare the PIN") is already done in the code and the report. At most, the
      BUILD entry should copy that PIN line when it is written. The finding itself says there is no
      wrong result.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. The spec puts the look index in the resume identity in two places:
      m:267 ("Resume identity ... cap, look index, backend identifier") and m:330 ("look index in
      the resume identity"). DESIGN §1.9 says the opposite in so many words ("`look_index` is STATE,
      not identity"), and resume.py follows DESIGN. IDENTITY_KEYS (:56-58) leaves out look_index.
      The value sits in ckpt["state"] and is checked by an invariant (:349-357): look_index ==
      len(sessions)//40, len(looks) == look_index, and each look i is at launch 40·(i+1).
      record_session (:457-471) refuses a look that is due but missing, refuses one that is not due,
      and writes the look in the same atomic write as the session that made it due. So a resume can
      neither skip nor repeat a look, and as the finding says, no wrong result follows. Still,
      DESIGN and the spec differ in literal wording, and under the task's rule that difference is
      itself a finding. Minor fits: the fix is a disclosure in the BUILD/LOCK entry, not a code
      change.
    DISPOSITION: DISCLOSED (PIN): the look index is kept as invariant-checked checkpoint state, not an identity field; it can be neither skipped nor repeated across a resume.

    valence_core/m/stages.py:805  (DESIGN §0 rule 5 (no per-session seconds for a contrast session); stages.py:339-345's own PIN; m:318)
    TITLE: Campaign report carries cumulative generation ms that includes contrast sessions; at the edges it equals one contrast session's seconds
    CLAIM:
      The pick-free, tracked campaign report writes generation_ms_used = state.hour_cap_ms_used, the
      sum over all sessions, contrast included. The gate-stage report withholds even pooled contrast
      seconds (`_sham_seconds`, 'a contrast session's seconds encode its free-round exclusion
      position'). When two consecutive committed reports differ by one launch (CAP, STOP or launch-
      count PAUSE right after a resume; strict rotation puts A0 next after each 40-launch look), the
      delta is one A0 session's seconds.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      hour_cap is reached right after a resume, or (in the stub) cap=1e-9. The report shows
      generation_ms_used equal to the single A0 contrast session's seconds.
    EVIDENCE:
      review/blinding/cap_ms.py printed: CAP report generation_ms_used = 22 | sessions: [('A0', 0,
      0.022)].
    FIX:
      Report hour-cap use as status (cap reached or not) plus a coarse fraction, or sham-arm ms
      only, matching _sham_seconds. Alternatively, declare the pooled figure as a PIN with the
      seat's sign-off.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is factually correct, and I could not refute it on materiality alone. The campaign
      report includes `generation_ms_used = ckpt["state"]["hour_cap_ms_used"]` (stages.py:805).
      `resume.record_session` adds every session's ms to that counter, contrast (A0) sessions
      included (resume.py:467).  This breaks the build's own declared rule: - Phase C PIN 11 says
      "Stage timing in reports covers sham sessions only; contrast seconds stay in the checkpoint".
      - Inconsistency 3 says "rule 5 bars contrast seconds, so reports carry sham seconds only". -
      The gate-stage report keeps that rule through `_sham_seconds` (stages.py:339-345), which cites
      m:318 and DESIGN §0 rule 5. - The campaign report does not keep it. Its own PIN (stages.py
      ~794-797) lists the report's contents but never declares `generation_ms_used`, so no declared
      PIN covers it.  The edge case can happen in a real run, not only with the reviewer's cap=1e-9
      stub. A fired look pauses the campaign at launch 40k and writes report R1. The seat
      adjudicates and resumes. If the next launch, an A0 session under strict rotation, ends the
      campaign (floors reached, CAP, or a launch-count PAUSE), report R2 is written, and R2 − R1 is
      that one contrast session's wall-clock.  Why only minor: - The coincidence is rare: a fired
      look followed by a terminal event or pause within one launch. - Recovering the figure takes a
      deliberate subtraction; nothing is printed (DESIGN rule 5 governs printing). - What leaks is
      one contrast session's wall-clock, not a pick. At most it hints at where a free-round
      exclusion fell, and stdout already prints that exclusion's phase. Any link to the picks
      themselves is weak. - In the STOP case the hair-trigger adjudication read under §4 exception
      (1) already exposes the fired round, so nothing extra leaks there. - The campaign is not run
      at step 4.  Still, the spec takes free-round events seriously enough to withhold them: m:271
      withholds contrast free-round events "(a free round's class sits on the pick)", and m:318
      prints free-round exclusions as phase alone. The build's own PIN promises contrast seconds
      stay in the checkpoint, and one report breaks that promise. So it stands as a minor defect.
      The fix is cheap: report cap status only, or sham-only ms, or declare the figure as a PIN for
      the seat to sign off.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. The campaign report writes `generation_ms_used =
      ckpt["state"]["hour_cap_ms_used"]` (stages.py:805). `resume.record_session` adds every
      recorded session's ms to that counter (resume.py:467), contrast sessions included, and
      resume.py:346 checks it against the sum over all sessions. The `_assert_pick_free` check only
      looks for roster labels, so it does not catch this. Everywhere else the package withholds
      contrast seconds. The gate-stage report uses `_sham_seconds` (stages.py:339-345, used at
      :446), whose PIN says a contrast session's seconds encode its free-round exclusion position
      and are withheld until VERDICTS (m:318, DESIGN §0 rule 5). readrule.py:14 and :113 strip and
      redact "seconds" for the same reason. G-ID's report shows timing on both paths, but G-ID is
      sham-only, so that is consistent. Nothing in the spec asks for hours used to be reported
      before VERDICTS: m:249 and m:251 only set the cap, and m:318 lists only pooled pre-pick
      exclusion counts per arm at looks. So the field goes beyond what the spec permits and is
      inconsistent with the package's own PIN.  The single-session case does happen. I reproduced it
      with tmp cap=1e-9 and the report showed one A0 session's ms. The rotation pin
      (schedule.py:421-425) also confirms that A0 is next whenever counts are tied, for example
      20/20 at look 40. In production the cap is a value in hours set by the LOCK, so a report
      covering one session needs a coincidence: the cap reached right after a resume, giving two
      consecutive reports that differ by one A0 session. In the STOP case, a hair hit on that one
      session leads to an adjudication read under exception (1) anyway. The information leaked is
      weak and the edge case is rare, so the minor severity is right.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: reports carry hour_cap_reached and sham-arm generation ms only.

    TITLE: No guard that a pick-bearing checkpoint lands under the -diff rule; ckpt_path / out_root accept tracked, normally-diffing locations
    CLAIM:
      run_gate_stage (:375), run_gid_pair (:507) and run_campaign (:693) take any `ckpt_path` and
      only refuse canonical paths for the stub. resume.py's OLMo-restart PIN requires the operator
      to choose a NEW checkpoint path. A choice under data/m/reports/ or data/m/gates/ is tracked
      and diffs normally. A choice under data/m/ckpt/<subdir>/ or data/m/ is ignored, so it is never
      committed (rule 27).
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      The operator restarts the OLMo campaign at ckpt_path=data/m/reports/OLMo-
      sft_campaign_podB.json. Every contrast pick is committed into a file that `git diff` / `git

    EVIDENCE:

      ...] and the report lists files.checkpoint 'reports/Q-F0_time_ckpt.json'. git check-attr diff
      data/m/reports/OLMo-sft_campaign_podB.json -> unspecified. git check-ignore -v ->
      !/data/m/reports/*.json (tracked). data/m/ckpt/podB/x.json -> ignored by /data/m/ckpt/*.
    FIX:
      Require Path(ckpt_path).resolve().parent == (out_root/'ckpt').resolve() and a '*.json' name
      (SystemExit otherwise), so every checkpoint and its set-asides fall under /data/m/ckpt/*

    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The behaviour is real, but it does not contradict the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN. It is a
      hardening suggestion for an operator mistake at a later step, not a defect in the step-4
      build.  1. DESIGN §1.15 itself puts `ckpt_path` in the stage signatures. DESIGN §1.9/:98 says
      outright that after a pod change the OLMo campaign restarts at "a NEW checkpoint path chosen
      by the operator". An arbitrary path is therefore the binding design. The builder disclosed
      making the parameter optional and adding `out_root` (phaseC.md:93).  2. The spec never names
      where checkpoints must live and never requires `-diff`.    - m:251 requires only that
      checkpoints are committed "under §4's blinding rule".    - m:318's rule binds readers: neither
      the seat nor the PI reads any pick-bearing artifact before VERDICTS. A checkpoint saved in the
      wrong place is still a pick-bearing artifact under that rule.    - The `-diff` attribute is
      extra protection the build chose to add, not something the spec requires. Its absence on a
      path the operator picks by mistake is not a breach of m:318.  3. The defaults are correct.
      With `ckpt_path=None`, `out_paths` puts the checkpoint at `data/m/ckpt/{unit}_{stage}.json`,
      which is tracked and has `diff: unset`. The obvious restart name, `data/m/ckpt/OLMo-
      sft_campaign_podB.json`, is also tracked and `-diff`. I checked both with `git check-ignore`
      and `git check-attr`.  4. Nothing in step 4 lets anyone set `ckpt_path`.
      `scripts/m_sessions.py` wires the stage subcommands only as argparse stubs that refuse with
      "not licensed until step N". The OLMo campaign cannot run before step 9 and a box. The
      command-line wiring that would accept an operator path does not exist yet, so the constraint
      can be added when it is built.  5. The failure scenario needs an operator to choose `reports/`
      or a subdirectory by mistake. That is a misuse case for future tooling, not a fault in this
      code.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. run_gate_stage (stages.py:375), run_gid_pair (:507) and run_campaign
      (:693) take whatever `ckpt_path` they are given (`Path(ckpt_path) if ckpt_path is not None
      else paths["ckpt"]`). The only check on that path is `_stub_guard`, which calls
      `refuse_canonical_out` only when the adapter's provenance carries SELFTEST-STUB. On a real
      adapter nothing checks where the checkpoint goes.  The resume.py docstring (:23-24) says an
      OLMo campaign restarts "at a new checkpoint path the operator chooses". The load_or_init
      refusal (:417-418) says the same, and so does DESIGN.md:98. So the API openly expects the
      operator to choose a non-default path.  The repo's own ignore and attribute rules place that
      path badly in two of the named cases. A checkpoint under `data/m/reports/` is tracked and
      diffs normally, which would expose picks in `git diff` and `git log -p`, against m:318 and
      m:251's "under §4's blinding rule". A checkpoint in a subdirectory of `data/m/ckpt/` is
      ignored, so it would never be committed, against rule 27.  Minor is the right severity. The
      default path is safe, and at step 4 no CLI exposes `ckpt_path`: `m_sessions.py` stage
      subcommands refuse with exit 2. The breach needs an operator to pick a bad path later, but
      that is exactly the situation the OLMo-restart PIN creates.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: checkpoints must resolve to <out_root>/ckpt/<name>.json; on a non-stub adapter out_root must be REPO/data/m (round 2).

    TITLE: Qualification report cannot supply m:283's r: contrast whole-session validity is printed on stdout but withheld from the report
    CLAIM:
      _contrast_block reports sessions, domain and forced exclusions but no valid / excluded@free
      count, as DESIGN §1.10 requires. m:263 lets every pre-VERDICTS report give a contrast free-
      round exclusion as `excluded@free`, and m:283's r needs the 8 contrast sessions' validity. The
      same per-session validity is printed on stdout by session_line (m:318), so withholding it from
      the report protects nothing and leaves r computable only from raw stdout. This is a DESIGN-vs-
      spec disagreement; the builder flagged it but did not resolve it.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-F0m-Qwen with one A0 free lexical exclusion: stdout prints 'qual A0 s=2
      excluded:excluded@free', but the report's A0 block (sessions 8, domain 8, excluded_at_forced

      A0 keys are ['audit_sessions_forced','contrast','domain_sessions','excluded_at_forced','forced

      form), or record the seat's ruling that r is computed from the committed stdout and say so in
      the report.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The finding assumes r needs whole-session validity, and the spec says otherwise. m:283's "the
      valid rate pooled over all 24 (per template beside)" is shorthand for §2.4's launch rule.
      m:192 defines it as "r = k/n_q ... k the valid sessions on the primary's domain pooled over
      all n_q sessions ... (sham and contrast templates ...), reported per template beside". m:308
      says "Nothing after the first pick removes a session from the domain". Every free-round
      exclusion comes after the first read, so it cannot move k. This matches the rule's purpose:
      N₉₀(r) is the chance of reaching a floor, and m:247 counts floors on domain sessions. The
      report already carries domain_sessions for the contrast and sham templates (forced-phase
      material, `_forced_domain`). So r and its per-template split can be computed straight from the
      report, with nothing withheld.  On the blinding side, the code follows the spec, so there is
      no DESIGN-vs-spec disagreement. m:271 says contrast free-round events, exclusions included,
      are withheld at every gate stage. m:145's disposition line says "stage reports carry forced-
      round counts only". m:263 sets the printed form an exclusion takes (`excluded@free`). It does
      not require a count in the stage report, and stdout prints that form per m:318.  Even on the
      reading the finding prefers, the committed stdout already carries each session's validity (the
      finding concedes this), so r would still be recoverable from the record. The builders also
      disclosed the point in reportsA/readrule.md:70 and reportsC/phaseC.md item 4, noting the
      domain-rate derivation.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The finding's code observation is accurate: `_contrast_block` (readrule.py:565-583) reports no
      valid or excluded@free count, while `session_line` prints `excluded:excluded@free` on stdout.
      Both conclusions drawn from it are wrong.  (1) m:283's r does not need contrast whole-session
      validity. m:283 says "r for Q-F0's launch rule". The launch rule defines r at m:192: "r =
      k/n_q ... k the valid sessions on the primary's domain pooled over all n_q sessions ...
      reported per template beside". m:247 says "Floors count sessions in the primary's domain, per
      arm". N90(r) is the chance of reaching a domain-counted floor under Bin(N, r), so r is the
      domain rate. m:308 says nothing after the first pick removes a session from the domain, so a
      contrast session excluded@free still counts in k. Both blocks report `domain_sessions`
      (contrast block line 579, sham block line 637). So r = (A0 domain_sessions + A0s
      domain_sessions)/24 comes straight from the report. In the reviewer's own scenario, the A0
      block's `domain_sessions` = 8 already gives the contrast share of r.  (2) This is not a
      DESIGN-vs-spec disagreement. m:271 says: "At every gate stage the contrast sessions' forced-
      round counts are reported beside; their free-round events — exclusions, degeneracy, any class
      — are withheld to the unit's VERDICTS entry or NOT-RUN record". The STOP-8 disposition (m:145,
      C7 group) says "stage reports carry forced-round counts only". m:283 itself says "(their free-
      round events withheld, §3)". m:263 only fixes the printed form ("phase alone") wherever a
      contrast free exclusion appears. It does not require reports to carry a count. DESIGN §1.10's
      "a count of withheld sessions is not reported either" follows the spec.  The builder's flag
      (reportsA/readrule.md:70) already said that if r is the domain rate, it can be derived from
      `domain_sessions` and `sessions`. m:192 settles that it is.
    DISPOSITION: REFUTED by both refuters (m:192 defines r as the domain rate, derivable from the reported domain counts); no change.

    valence_core/m/stages.py:280  (m:271 ('An adjudication read ... is confined to the fired round(s) and the harness lines around them; ... the pick it reveals is recorded as seen'); m:318 exception (1))
    TITLE: No support for m:271's confined adjudication read: a hair-trigger stop records no round and no excerpt, so the read needs the full pick-bearing checkpoint
    CLAIM:
      _hair_hit returns record None, and _record_stop stores only template, s and phase: no round
      index, no excerpt. Neither stages nor readrule writes a confined adjudication extract, and
      nothing logs a revealed pick as seen. To read a contrast session's fired free round, the seat
      must open the checkpoint (every session's picks, menus and free rounds; campaign looks
      likewise), so the confinement m:271 requires cannot be honored with the package's outputs.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-TIME-F0 stops on a scaffold string in A0's free round 4. The stop block says {template A0, s
      0, phase free}. Adjudicating needs ckpt/Q-F0_time.json opened in full, which shows all six A0

      has stop {bar: scaffold_absorption, phase: free, record: None, problems: []} and no round

      excerpt (fired round(s) plus the adjacent harness lines, with a 'picks revealed' list) under
      ckpt/ (-diff) for the adjudication read.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The finding's facts are partly wrong, and the missing piece is not something the spec or
      DESIGN asks for. (1) No information is lost. At every call site (stages.py:408-409, 539-540,
      758-761), `_resume.record_session(ckpt, rec)` runs before `_hair_hit(rec)`. So the stopped
      session's full record, with every round, is already in `ckpt["sessions"]`; `"record": None` in
      the stop block just avoids storing it twice. The fired round is always that session's last
      round, because verify.py:184-189 rejects any record that goes on past a hair-triggered round
      ("the session continued past a round that ends it"). The stop block names template and s,
      which is enough to find it. (2) Nothing requires a confined excerpt at this step. m:357 lists
      step 4's read-rule tooling as "(stripping, redaction, class-and-phase printing)", and DESIGN
      §1.10 and §1.12 list strip_contrast, redact_sham, printed_reason, session_line, stage_report
      and release_full. Neither asks for an adjudication-extract writer. m:271 limits the seat's
      read, and m:318 exception (1) says the read is "recorded as read, a revealed pick included".
      That record is made by the seat in its adjudication entry. A package writing checkpoints at
      run time cannot log what the seat later reads. (3) The scenario where the checkpoint must be
      "opened in full" does not follow. The blinding rule (m:318) binds the seat and the PI, not
      Code. When an adjudication is asked for, Code can pull the last round of session (A0, 0) and
      the harness lines around it from the checkpoint and hand the seat only that, so the limit on
      the read can be kept. An extract helper would be a convenience, not a fix to a contradiction
      with spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. `_hair_hit` returns `record: None`. The stop block written by
      `_record_stop` has kind, bar, template, s, phase, problems, a null record and the blinded
      copy. It has no round index and no excerpt. Nothing in stages.py, readrule.py or m_sessions.py
      writes a confined adjudication extract or records a revealed pick as seen. The only blinded
      forms are `strip_contrast`, `redact_sham` and `release_full`. The first two withhold a
      contrast session's free rounds. `release_full` returns the records unchanged. The stages' own
      PIN at `_refuse_stopped` (stages.py:240-241) names "the stopped checkpoint" as "the
      adjudication's record". That checkpoint holds every session's picks, menus and free rounds.
      m:271 confines an adjudication read to the fired round(s) and the harness lines around them,
      and requires a revealed pick to be recorded as seen. m:318 exception (1) says the read is made
      "as §3 confines them and recorded as read". The package gives no output that respects that
      confinement. Two things soften this, so minor is the right grade. First, the fired round can
      be recovered as the last round of the session record, because the runner returns at the hit.
      Second, Code could hand-extract the round for the seat. The spec also does not expressly
      require the package to produce the extract. Still, the behaviour described is real and the
      failure scenario happens as stated.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: the stop block records the fired round (checkpoint only); every STOP/PAUSE writes ckpt/<stem>_adjudication.json (m:271's confined read: the fired round(s) or the bar's sham numerator rounds, with picks_revealed), never printed, -diff (round 2).

  tracked and -diff (git check-ignore / check-attr verified). - The resume identity is exact and

  sha vs manifest vs regeneration), path, hour cap, audit sha16, ban sha and backend identifier all
  refuse on change. A same-pod backend change is refused. A gate-stage pod change together with a
  design change is refused with nothing renamed. Set-aside is a rename, never a delete. - An OLMo
  campaign carries its pod id in the identity: a cross-pod resume and a resume without a pod id are

  look_index and hour_cap_ms_used are refused. The hour cap accumulates across resumes, must equal

--- #17 [CONFIRMED] reviewer:substantive lenient:minor strict:substantive
    valence_core/m/resume.py:397  (m:267 (a gate stage restarts when a pod change interrupts it); m:271 (a fire stops the stage; the OLMo G-TIME pause waits for the seat); m:283 / m:302 (nothing runs until the seat re-specifies); stages.py PIN 'a resume never lifts a STOP' (stages.py:240))
    TITLE: A pod change lifts a gate stage's STOP or PAUSE, and re-runs a finished stage
    CLAIM:
      On a gate stage, load_or_init checks for a pod change first, before it looks at whether the
      stage was stopped or finished. It sets aside any checkpoint written on another pod and returns
      a fresh one. stages.run_gate_stage only calls _refuse_stopped afterwards (stages.py:394-395),
      on that fresh checkpoint, so a recorded hair-trigger STOP is never seen. A stage-end binomial
      STOP and the OLMo G-TIME PAUSE are never written to the checkpoint at all: _finish_gate writes
      the checkpoint only in the hair-trigger branch (stages.py:423-439). As a result, a STOPped,
      PAUSEd or completed gate stage resumed on a new pod re-runs from session 0, and its report
      file is overwritten with the new status.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-TIME-OLMo-sft or G-F0m-OLMo-sft STOPs (hair trigger or sham bar) or PAUSEs (an
      AuditSpec/echo claim) on pod A. The pod is stopped. On pod B the operator runs the stage again
      with --resume. The old checkpoint is renamed .podchange-*, all sessions re-run, and the stage
      can now PASS. reports/OLMo-sft_time_report.json then says PASS with no adjudication. A
      completed PASS stage can likewise turn into a PAUSE or STOP on the re-run.
    EVIDENCE:
      review/guard-resume/exp_pod_stop.py on the scripted stub prints: 'A run1: STOP stop recorded:
      True scaffold_absorption' / 'A same-pod resume refused: SystemExit STAGE STOPPED' / 'A new-pod
      resume: PASS'. Then 'B run1: PAUSE ... stop: None' / 'B new-pod resume: PASS report status
      now: PASS'. Then 'B2 run1: STOP stop in ckpt: None' / 'B2 new-pod resume: PASS'. Then 'C run1:
      PASS -> new-pod resume of a COMPLETED stage: PAUSE'.
    FIX:
      Persist the stage's end status (STOP, PAUSE or PASS, with the bar results) in the checkpoint
      at _finish_gate. In load_or_init, refuse the pod-change set-aside when the old checkpoint
      carries a stop or an end status: only an unfinished, unstopped stage restarts. Check
      _refuse_stopped on the old checkpoint before it is set aside.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      I could not refute it. The code behaves as the finding says, and the behaviour goes beyond the
      spec. m:267 restarts a gate stage only when it is "interrupted by a pod change". m:271 and
      m:302 say a fire stops the stage and nothing runs until the seat re-specifies; an OLMo pause
      waits for the seat. The package's own PIN at stages.py:240 says "a resume never lifts a STOP".
      On a new pod, load_or_init (resume.py:397-414) sets aside any gate checkpoint before
      stages.py:395 calls _refuse_stopped. Stopped, paused and completed stages are set aside too,
      so they restart from session 0 and the report file is overwritten. DESIGN's build pin 2 has no
      carve-out for finished or stopped stages, but it comes from the RESULTS.md build-pin line
      "restarts on a new pod as §2.7 directs", and §2.7 says "interrupted". So the spec governs, and
      the narrower scope binds. None of the builder reports disclose this behaviour.  I grade it
      minor rather than substantive on materiality: (a) It cannot arise at step 4. Every stage
      subcommand refuses with exit 2. Q-F0 runs on the laptop, where RUNPOD_POD_ID is unset, so

      at step 9, can reach it. (b) It needs an operator to re-run a stage that has already STOPped,
      PAUSEd or finished, with --resume, without the seat's word. The protocol already forbids that
      ("Code escalates fired gates, never self-adjudicates"). This is a hole in a defence-in-depth
      guard, not a path a correct run takes. (c) Nothing is lost. The old checkpoint, with its stop
      block, is renamed and never deleted, and the .gitignore block lets it be committed. The

      PASS would show as a diff against the committed STOP. The run also prints a POD CHANGE line.
      It should still be fixed before step 9: either carry the end status in the checkpoint and
      refuse the set-aside when a stop or end status is present, or call _refuse_stopped on the old
      checkpoint before it is set aside.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):

      (resume.py:397-413) checks `pod_id_seen` against the current pod before anything else. On a
      mismatch it renames the old checkpoint aside and returns a fresh one. `run_gate_stage` calls

    valence_core/m/resume.py:398  (m:251 / m:267 (a gate stage interrupted by a pod change restarts from its first session on the new box; the backend identifier cannot tell two boxes of one class apart); CLAUDE.md Environment consistency clause)
    TITLE: An OLMo gate stage spliced across boxes when RUNPOD_POD_ID is unset
    CLAIM:
      The pod-change detector compares pod_id_seen with the current RUNPOD_POD_ID, and None == None
      counts as the same pod. A pod id is required only for an OLMo campaign (resume.py:246-254). An
      OLMo gate stage starts and resumes without one, and nothing in stages.py or runner_olmo checks
      the variable. The OLMo backend identifier has no host or GPU-instance field
      (olmo_hf.py:322-336 gives only the GPU name, CUDA, capability and libraries). So two boxes of
      one class with the variable unset are indistinguishable.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
