      A replacement box of the same GPU class resumes it. The 13 remaining sessions are appended to
      box A's 11 in one checkpoint and one stage-end bar, with nothing set aside. This is the splice
      m:267 forbids.
    EVIDENCE:
      exp_pod_stop.py case D, run with env_pod(None) on both 'boxes', prints 'D: interrupted with 11
      sessions on box A; resumed on box B: PASS sessions 24 set aside: [] pod_id_seen: None'. grep
      -n pod over stages.py, runner_olmo.py and verify.py finds nothing.
    FIX:
      Require a pod id for every OLMo stage, not only the campaign: refuse at start and on resume
      when RUNPOD_POD_ID is absent on an OLMo unit. Optionally add a per-instance discriminator
      (e.g. torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).uuid) to the OLMo backend identifier, or to
      pod_id_seen.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      I could not refute the finding. I confirmed the behaviour by running the code.
      resume.py:396-398 compares the stored pod_id_seen with _env_pod(), so None == None skips the
      pod-change branch. The identity diff is then empty and load_or_init returns the old
      checkpoint, so the stage resumes. An OLMo campaign fails closed without a pod id
      (resume.py:246-254, "POD ID ABSENT"). An OLMo gate stage does not, and stages.py,
      runner_olmo.py and m_sessions.py never check the variable for gate stages.
      OLMoHFAdapter.provenance() (olmo_hf.py:311-336) has no field that identifies a single

    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The finding is accurate. In resume.py, `_env_pod()` (lines 160-162) returns None when
      RUNPOD_POD_ID is empty or unset. `_fresh` stores that value as `pod_id_seen`, and
      `write_checkpoint` stamps it again. `load_or_init` treats a gate stage as having changed pods
      only when `seen != now` (lines 397-399), so two boxes that both lack the variable compare None
      == None and count as the same pod. A pod id is required only when `olmo_campaign` is true
      (lines 246-254). Every other identity, OLMo gate stages included, must have pod_id None. The
      OLMo provenance (olmo_hf.py:311-336) has no host or instance field, and the resume.py PIN at
      lines 61-65 itself says the backend identifier "cannot tell two boxes of one class apart".
      Grepping stages.py, runner_olmo.py, verify.py and m_sessions.py finds no check for the
      variable. The only safeguard is a docstring PIN on `pod_id()` that says "verify on the box",
      and the code does not enforce it. So when the variable is absent, the one mechanism that
      carries out m:267's rule (a gate stage interrupted by a pod change restarts from its first
      session on the new box) fails silently and joins the two boxes' sessions into one stage.
      DESIGN §1.9 line 98 allows `pod_id_seen` to be None and does not cover this case. The scenario
      is realistic. The checkpoints sit on the persistent network volume, so a replacement pod finds
      them, and the repo record shows Code drives the box through non-interactive ssh. My unverified
      understanding (no network here) is that RunPod's container env reaches ssh sessions through
      ~/.bashrc, which stock images skip for non-interactive shells. If so, the variable would be
      unset in that launch mode. Gate stages run before the campaign, so the campaign's POD ID
      ABSENT refusal would only appear after the gates had already run unprotected. The severity
      stays substantive: a spec rule is silently disabled under a plausible operating condition.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: every OLMo stage refuses without RUNPOD_POD_ID.

    valence_core/m/guard.py:152  (CLAUDE.md rule 2 (gates re-validated per backend; the annotation 'T-step identity is its own gate'); m:267 ('G-ID is a whole-generation identity check per family'); m:251 backend of record)

      G-ID-F0m PASSes on backend d21ab386dd82282e. After an OS or mlx update the backend becomes

      refuting the finding as a substantive or blocking defect.  1. **The code does what the finding
      says.** `guard._gid_pass` (guard.py:152-165) reads only unit, criterion, verdict, n_pairs and
      pairs_identical. `cached_path_admitted` and the runner's re-check (runner_qwen.py:123) call it
      without passing a backend. The artifact's `identity.backend_identifier` (stages.py:611,

      m:267 says the guard "permits `cached=True` only when the unit's G-ID artifact is on disk with
      PASS". The spec reads rule 2's annotation as "G-ID is a whole-generation identity check **per
      family**" (scoping it with "Q-F0's G-ID licenses nothing for F3"), not per backend. m:249 says

      field read the code implements. So the code matches the spec and DESIGN, and the two do not

      "Path; projections for Q-F0 under its path", and the floors come from that path's column. The

      not declared as a PIN. A dated note or PIN at LOCK v0.1 should say so. If the seat wants
      hardening, the fix should STOP on a backend mismatch rather than fall back to uncached. This
      is a hardening and disclosure item, not a rule violation.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The finding is accurate. guard.cached_path_admitted(unit, stage) has no backend parameter.

--- #20 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/guard.py:397  (m:267 (the source guard permits cached=True only on a G-ID PASS and exempts only the G-ID stage); DESIGN §1.8)
    TITLE: The call-site guard trusts spellings: a shadowed cached_path_admitted and rebinding by comprehension, except or match go unflagged
    CLAIM:
      _source_ok accepts any call whose callee is named cached_path_admitted: a local def, a module-
      level lambda, or an attribute on any object. _name_ok treats a keyword-only parameter as
      licensed and does not see a later rebinding of that name by a comprehension target, an except-
      as or a match capture. The run_gid_pair exemption (in_gid, guard.py:284-285) is inherited by
      any scope nested inside a function of that name, and is granted to a nested def of that name
      inside campaign code. The runtime re-check in runner_qwen covers runner calls outside stage
      'id', but a direct adapter.generate call, or a stage-'id' scaffold, depends on this static
      check alone.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      Each of these sources returns [] from check_call_sites, yet each can pass True to generate
      outside G-ID: `def cached_path_admitted(*a): return True`; `[a.generate(..., cached=cached)
      for cached in (True,)]` inside a function with `*, cached=False`; a nested `def
      run_gid_pair(): a.generate(..., cached=True)` inside run_campaign.
    EVIDENCE:
      review/guard-resume/exp_callsites.py: 'MISSED' on 9 of 11 crafted sources (comprehension x2,
      except-as, match capture, local def / lambda / attribute named cached_path_admitted, nested
      run_gid_pair, renamed-function stage-id run). Only the import-as and the getattr cases were
      flagged.
    FIX:
      Resolve licensed sources by binding, not spelling: accept only `guard.cached_path_admitted` /
      `guard.gid_paths` as attributes of a name imported from valence_core.m.guard, and flag any
      def, assign or import that binds those names. In _name_ok, also treat comprehension targets,
      ExceptHandler.name, Match* captures and Import aliases as rebindings. Grant the run_gid_pair
      exemption only to the module-level stages.run_gid_pair.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The finding is refuted on materiality. The behaviour it describes is real, but it is not a
      defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN.  1. DESIGN §1.8 itself specifies a check that
      works by name. It says a literal True "is allowed only inside a function named
      `run_gid_pair`", and that `cached=` must be "a Name bound from `cached_path_admitted(...)` /
      `gid_paths()`". So the nested-`run_gid_pair` exemption and the "callee named
      `cached_path_admitted`" acceptance follow DESIGN to the letter. 2. Accepting a keyword-only
      parameter is a declared PIN, and its callers are checked in turn. 3. m:267 asks the m
      package's guard to permit `cached=True` only on a G-ID PASS, exempting only G-ID. The built
      package meets that:    - check_call_sites returns [] over it.    - None of the bypass
      constructs exists anywhere in `valence_core/m` or `scripts/m_sessions.py` (no shadowing def,
      lambda or assign of `cached_path_admitted`, `run_gid_pair` or `gid_paths`; no comprehension,
      except-as or match binding of `cached`/`path_cached`).    - The only `adapter.generate` calls
      are in `runner_qwen` and `runner_olmo`. `runner_olmo` passes a literal `cached=False`.
      `runner_qwen` re-reads the licence at runtime for every cached session outside stage `"id"`,
      and the selftest checks that refusal.    - Only the spec-exempted `stages.run_gid_pair` builds
      stage-`"id"` scaffolds. 4. Every failure scenario needs deliberately contrived source, such as
      `for cached in (True,)`, `except ValueError as cached`, `match not False: case cached`, or a
      local def that shadows the guard function. These are cases that cannot arise in the code under
      review, not plausible accidental regressions. 5. No static lint over Python can be complete
      against such code. `exec`, `setattr(guard, ...)` and `globals()` would beat the proposed fix
      as well. The spec sets no adversarial-completeness bar; recon's precedent is a grep guard. 6.
      The reviewer's "9 of 11 missed" overcounts. The case labelled "renamed function stage-id run"
      is literally `def run_gid_pair(...)` passing `path_cached=True`, which is the licensed
      exemption working as DESIGN specifies, not a miss.  The one real residue is wording. The
      guard's docstring says "a name bound only from those", and that is slightly too strong for
      comprehension, except-as and match rebinding. It is a documentation-precision point only, with
      no verdict path behind it.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. In `guard._source_ok` (guard.py:397-399), a callee is accepted when
      its bare name is `cached_path_admitted`: `_callee` returns `f.id` or `f.attr`, so a local def,
      a lambda or any object's attribute with that name passes. `_name_ok` (guard.py:410-446) treats
      any parameter as bound. It only looks for rebindings of the following kinds: Assign,
      AnnAssign, NamedExpr, AugAssign, For, With and Global/Nonlocal. It does not look at
      comprehension generators, `ExceptHandler.name` or match captures. `in_gid` (guard.py:284-285)
      is `any(...)` over the whole scope stack, so a def named `run_gid_pair` passes the exemption
      to every scope nested inside it. That includes a nested def inside campaign code. The runtime
      re-check in runner_qwen.py:123 skips stage 'id' and covers only runner calls. A direct
      adapter.generate call gets no runtime licence check.  Two small points of imprecision, neither
      of which changes the finding. First, the reviewer's own except-as test case calls generate
      after the handler; Python deletes the name when the handler exits, so that exact source would
      raise UnboundLocalError rather than pass True. The variant that calls generate inside the
      handler is also missed by the guard and passes a truthy ValueError instance, not True. Second,
      DESIGN's wording ("inside a function named run_gid_pair") literally allows the nested-def
      case, but the spec (m:267) exempts only the G-ID stage. All three failure scenarios the
      finding states do pass True and are not flagged.  Minor is the right severity. No m code today
      exploits these gaps, and runner calls outside stage 'id' are re-checked at runtime. The gap is
      in how robust the static guard is.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: licensed sources resolved by binding (round 1); attribute rebinds flagged (round 2, N15; widened round 3, R7).

--- #21 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/resume.py:214  (m:267 (resume identity: the unit's lock hash ...); m:279 / m:282 / m:286 / m:302 (every stage stages.py runs follows its governing lock: G-TIME after v0.1, qual after v0.2, campaign after v1.0))
    TITLE: The resume identity binds no code: 'pre-lock' is accepted at every stage, and a changed renderer or audit wrapper resumes silently
    CLAIM:
      Identity accepts lock_sha256 == 'pre-lock' for any stage, campaign included, although by the
      spec's chain only G-CAL (not built here) precedes its lock. lock_sha256 is an operator-
      supplied string that nothing checks against the lock or the hashes it pins (renders, module
      homes, analyzer). Nothing else in the identity is computed from corr.renders_m or audit.py's
      claiming logic. So a stage interrupted and resumed after a change to the renderer or the audit
      wrapper keeps its identity, and the old and new sessions are spliced.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-F0m-Qwen is interrupted at 8 sessions. The corrupted renders change, for example after a
      corr.py edit. The stage is resumed with --resume and the same identity_extra. It completes
      with mixed renders in one stage-end bar: identity unchanged, no refusal. A campaign can also
      start and resume with lock_sha256='pre-lock'.
    EVIDENCE:
      review/guard-resume/exp_identity_code.py prints 'G: interrupted with 8 sessions; resume after
      a renderer change: PASS None | identity unchanged: True' and 'G2: a campaign identity with
      lock_sha256='pre-lock' is accepted: pre-lock'.
    FIX:
      Refuse 'pre-lock' for stages time, id, qual and campaign. Add to the identity a sha over the m
      package's sources and the renders of record (corr.renders_sha), or verify at startup that they
      equal the values the supplied lock pins.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The behaviour is real. I reran the reviewer's experiment from a copy in review/refute-
      identity-code/. It printed "PASS None | identity unchanged: True" after a monkeypatched
      renderer change, and a campaign identity built with 'pre-lock' was accepted. But neither half
      is a defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN.  (1) Code binding. m:267 lists what the
      resume identity holds: "the unit's lock hash, schedule hash, path (cached/uncached), cap, look
      index, backend identifier" and, for an OLMo campaign, the pod id. Its refusals are "any change
      of seed or n", plus skip-only. Nothing there requires a hash of the renderer, the m audit
      wrapper or the package sources. The build carries every listed field (look_index as verified
      state), plus extras the spec does not require (n, key templates, audit_v2_sha16, ban_sha,
      proposal_sha256). The locks are what bind code. LOCK v0.1 pins module homes (m:279). LOCK v1.0
      pins the schedules, pools, renders and analyzer by hash (m:286). The laptop checklist logs
      HEAD and analyzer hashes at each detached run (m:267). Nothing in the spec says the resume
      path must re-derive the lock's pinned hashes. Checking them at startup, as the fix proposes,
      is a design extension. Neither DESIGN §1.9 nor the spec asks for it. The renders of record
      that such a check would compare against do not exist yet: they are G-CAL's output (step 6,
      m:276/m:358) and are pinned only from LOCK v0.1 / v1.0 onward. CLAUDE.md's consistency clause
      and rule 27 bind the backend (library versions, determinism flags), and those are inside
      backend_identifier. They do not bind repo code.  (2) 'pre-lock'. DESIGN §1.9 sanctions it
      explicitly: lock_sha256 "(or \"pre-lock\" for stages before the governing lock)". The build
      declares it as a PIN in stages._extra. The value is not a loophole in the resume check,
      because resume equality is exact and two-way whichever placeholder or digest is used. Refusing
      'pre-lock' would also not make the field verified: an operator can pass any well-formed hex64
      just as freely, since there is no lock file or lock format yet to check against (LOCK v0.1 is
      step 9). The case also cannot arise at step 4. Every stage subcommand in scripts/m_sessions.py
      refuses with exit 2 ("not licensed until step N"). No CLI path builds identity_extra, and the
      only callers that pass 'pre-lock' are the self-tests on the stub. The real lock hash gets
      supplied when the stage wiring is written, at steps 10/12/14/16, after LOCK v0.1/v0.2/v1.0. At
      most, refusing 'pre-lock' for the campaign is optional hardening. The campaign identity
      already requires the hour cap, which comes from LOCK v1.0, so a campaign marked pre-lock is
      self-inconsistent. That is a hardening suggestion, not a contradiction of a governing text.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. resume.Identity (resume.py:214) accepts the literal 'pre-lock' at
      every stage it builds: time, id, qual and campaign. The only check on lock_sha256 is a regex.
      By the spec's chain, every one of these stages follows a governing lock: m:279 LOCK v0.1 comes
      before G-TIME (m:280) and G-ID (m:281), m:282 v0.2 before qual (m:283), m:286 v1.0 before the
      campaign, and m:302 gives the same order for OLMo. m:267 puts "the unit's lock hash" in the
      identity. DESIGN.md:95 allows "pre-lock" only "for stages before the governing lock", and none
      of the built stages is one, so accepting it everywhere departs from both. None of the other
      identity fields is computed from the m package's code or its renders. A renderer change
      therefore resumes with an unchanged identity and the old and new sessions are spliced, as the
      finding says. Minor fits: the CLI subcommands refuse every stage at step 4, and a real lock
      hash would not bind code at runtime either. The defect is in the library API's guard.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: stages refuse 'pre-lock'; the identity carries package_sha256, pool_sha16, renders_sha16. resume.Identity itself still accepts 'pre-lock' for non-stage callers (disclosed).

    valence_core/m/resume.py:414  (m:251 / CLAUDE.md Environment ('a mid-campaign box change aborts and restarts'); m:247 (COMPLETE and CAP are terminal); m:271)
    TITLE: An OLMo campaign that has ended or STOPped is told to restart on a new pod
    CLAIM:
      load_or_init raises the pod_id mismatch before stages.run_campaign can look at
      camp['terminal'] or ckpt['stop'] (stages.py:706-716). The refusal text tells the operator to
      restart the campaign at a NEW checkpoint path, even when the campaign on disk is COMPLETE, CAP
      or STOPped rather than interrupted mid-campaign. The restart shares the report path, so it
      overwrites the finished campaign's report.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      OLMo-sft's campaign completes on pod A. On pod B, a --resume (e.g. to regenerate a report) is
      refused with 'restarts under its lock on the new box at a NEW checkpoint path'. Following that
      message runs a second full campaign under the same keys, and reports/OLMo-
      sft_campaign_report.json now points at the restart.
    EVIDENCE:
      review/guard-resume/exp_camp_pod.py prints 'F run on podA: COMPLETE | same-pod resume:
      CAMPAIGN ENDED ...' / 'F podB resume of an ENDED campaign: ... restarts under its lock on the
      new box at a NEW checkpoint path' / 'F restart at a new path on podB: COMPLETE ... report now
      from the restart: {'checkpoint': 'ckpt/OLMo-sft_campaign.podB.json'}'.
    FIX:
      Before the identity diff, read the old checkpoint's campaign.terminal and stop. If either is
      set, refuse with that status and do not suggest a restart. Offer the restart message only for
      an unfinished, unstopped campaign.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The behaviour is real: I re-ran the reviewer's exp_camp_pod.py and got the same three lines.
      But it does not break the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN, and it does no harm by itself. (1) DESIGN
      §0 line 98 prescribes this exact path. OLMo campaign checkpoints carry pod_id in the identity,
      so a different pod is a hard refusal, with "the campaign restarts under its lock on the new
      box — a NEW checkpoint path chosen by the operator; never splice". DESIGN line 96 puts that
      identity check inside load_or_init, before any campaign state is read. m:271 uses the same
      wording: "a campaign resume on a different pod id is refused and the campaign restarts under
      its lock on the new box". The code matches both, word for word in substance. (2) The refusal
      is correct and keeps m:247's terminal state. Nothing is resumed and nothing is written to the
      old checkpoint. The finished campaign's checkpoint stays untouched on disk, and under rule 27
      it is committed before the pod stops. (3) The harm needs two separate operator mistakes, each
      refused or gated elsewhere. The first is resuming a finished campaign. That fails on the same
      pod too ("CAMPAIGN ENDED", stages.py:712), so "--resume to regenerate a report" is not a path
      the code offers on any pod. The second is launching a new full OLMo campaign at a hand-chosen
      ckpt_path. That is a long pod-budget run, which needs the PI's go (m_sessions OLMO_WORDS;
      CLAUDE.md workflow), and the operator would see the terminal status in the committed record
      first. (4) A restart at a new path writing the shared report path is how every restart works,
      the legitimate mid-campaign one included. That is not specific to this finding, and the
      overwrite shows in git against the committed report. (5) The OLMo campaign subcommand is
      refused at step 4 ("not licensed until step 9", m_sessions.refusal). This library path runs
      only on the stub, so the scenario cannot happen in the build under review. What is left is a
      wording point: the hint could check camp['terminal'] / stop first. That is a nice-to-have
      message improvement, not a defect.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate, so it is not refuted. In stages.run_campaign,
      _resume.load_or_init(cpath, ident, resume) runs at stages.py:706. The STOP check
      (_refuse_stopped, :707) and the terminal check (camp['terminal'], :716) only run after it
      returns. For an OLMo campaign, pod_id is part of the identity (resume.py:248). A resume on
      another pod therefore produces an identity diff that includes 'pod_id'. The refusal at
      resume.py:414-421 then adds 'it restarts under its lock on the new box at a NEW checkpoint
      path', and it never looks at camp.terminal or ckpt.stop. The spec's restart rule applies to a
      mid-campaign box change (m:251 and CLAUDE.md: 'a mid-campaign box change aborts and
      restarts'). m:247 calls the analysis endpoints terminal, and _refuse_stopped's own PIN says a
      STOP is re-specified by the seat, not restarted. The message is therefore wrong for a campaign
      that is COMPLETE, CAP or STOPped. The report path does not depend on ckpt_path (out_paths,
      stages.py:123-136), so a restart at a new checkpoint path overwrites reports/OLMo-
      sft_campaign_report.json. Minor is the right severity, for three reasons. Nothing is spliced,
      deleted or mis-scored. The old checkpoint survives, and rule 27 says it should already be
      committed. And at step 4 the CLI refuses every stage subcommand (m_sessions.py main), so only
      a direct API call can reach this path today.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: an ended or stopped campaign is refused with its status, never told to restart.

--- #23 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/guard.py:593  (m:267 (laptop checklist before a detached Qwen run: power, one process, disk); DESIGN §1.8 ('Run only by stage drivers on the laptop'))
    TITLE: The laptop preflight is called by no Qwen stage driver, and it flags shell wrappers as model processes
    CLAIM:
      run_gate_stage, run_gid_pair and run_campaign never call guard.preflight_laptop for Q-F0. The
      Phase-C builder report acknowledges this as unwired. _model_processes matches 'python' plus a
      marker anywhere in the full command line, so a `/bin/zsh -c ... jlens-qwen36/.venv/bin/python
      ...` wrapper, or any shell whose argument string names the venv, counts as a model process.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      At step 10+, a detached G-TIME-F0 or campaign launched through stages.py runs with no power,
      one-process or disk check. Once the preflight is wired, an unrelated shell whose -c string
      mentions the jlens python makes it refuse a healthy launch.
    EVIDENCE:
      stages.py:388 / :517 / :700 call _startup only; grep finds no preflight_laptop call in
      stages.py. A live run of guard.preflight_laptop() on this laptop listed '22271 /bin/zsh -c
      source ...snapshot-zsh... ' among the 'ONE PROCESS' hits.
    FIX:
      Call preflight_laptop() at the start of each Qwen stage driver and refuse on any problem. In
      _model_processes, match on the executable (argv[0] basename contains 'python') rather than on
      the whole command string.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      Both halves describe real behaviour. Neither is a defect at step 4 against the spec, CLAUDE.md
      or DESIGN.  (1) Not wired. It is true that no function in stages.py calls preflight_laptop.
      But DESIGN §1.16 requires every stage subcommand at step 4 to be an argparse stub that refuses
      with "not licensed until step N". scripts/m_sessions.py does exactly that: `refusal()` returns
      exit 2 before anything else runs. So no detached Qwen run can start through this package at
      step 4. phaseC.md ("Not done") states the gap and says the preflight must be wired when the
      subcommands are licensed. The spec's m:267 checklist is a pre-launch operator checklist that
      sits beside `nohup caffeinate -is … & disown`, display sleep and the logged gate line. It does
      not say the preflight lives inside run_gate_stage or run_campaign.  The proposed fix (call it
      at the start of each stage driver) would also be wrong: - run_gate_stage, run_gid_pair and
      run_campaign receive an adapter that is already loaded. A one-process check made there runs
      after the 27B model is in memory, which is too late. The check belongs in the CLI handler
      before the adapter is built, and that handler is the refusal stub. - The same drivers serve
      OLMo on the box, where pmset is absent (the result would be "POWER UNKNOWN"). - The stub-
      driven integration selftest would start depending on the machine's power and process state.
      (2) The shell-wrapper match is real: a non-ancestor `/bin/zsh -c ...jlens-
      qwen36/.venv/bin/python...` line is flagged. This is a fail-safe over-match, not a defect: -
      The spec gives no matching mechanism. - The heuristic flags every jlens-venv python by design,
      including a selftest run. Telling whether a process has actually loaded a model is out of
      reach for it. - macOS pgrep leaves out the caller and its ancestors by default, so the
      launch's own wrapper never appears. A foreign shell that names the venv python is normally
      wrapping a jlens python that is flagged anyway. - The only effect is a refused launch that
      names the pid and command for the operator. That is the safe direction for a "one model
      process at a time" rule.  What remains is a disclosed carry for the step that licenses the
      subcommands. It is not a step-4 build defect.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      Both halves of the finding hold. (1) No Qwen stage driver (run_gate_stage :364, run_gid_pair
      :494, run_campaign :670) calls guard.preflight_laptop. In stages.py they call only _startup
      and the guard's cached-path functions. DESIGN §1.8 says the preflight is to be "Run only by
      stage drivers on the laptop", and m:267 asks for the laptop checklist (power, one process,
      disk) before a detached Qwen run. phaseC.md:102 admits it is not wired. The gap is latent at
      step 4, because every stage subcommand of m_sessions.py exits 2 with "not licensed until step
      N". It becomes real only if the drivers are licensed without the preflight being added, so
      minor is the right grade. (2) _model_processes (guard.py:586-595) flags a process when
      "python" and any marker appear anywhere in its lowercased command string; it never looks at
      the executable. I fed it canned pgrep lines. Two /bin/zsh -c wrappers (an until-pgrep wait
      loop, and a tail command naming the jlens venv python) and a `less` on a file under jlens-
      qwen36 named *_python.txt all came back as model processes. preflight_laptop turns each hit
      into a "ONE PROCESS" problem. Ancestors are excluded, so a launch's own wrapper is not
      counted; an unrelated shell or viewer is. The failure scenario can therefore occur once the
      preflight is wired with refuse-on-any-problem.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: non-stub Qwen stages run guard.preflight_laptop and print m:267's gate line; process matching on argv[0].

--- #24 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:701  (m:282 (LOCK-m-F0 v0.2 fixes the path; projections are made under that path); m:267 (cached iff that unit's G-ID passes))
    TITLE: Nothing checks the path against the lock's declared path: a missing or malformed G-ID artifact silently runs uncached
    CLAIM:
      The qualification stage (stages.py:389) and the campaign (stages.py:701) take the path from
      the artifact on disk alone. The locked path is not an argument. Where G-ID passed and v0.2
      locked 'cached', an absent or unreadable artifact (fresh checkout, the file moved aside, a
      merge damaging it) starts the stage uncached with no stdout line. The path appears only in the
      report's identity summary. Launch counts and the hour cap projected on the cached column then
      govern an uncached run.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      LOCK v0.2 says cached. At step 16 the artifact is missing, so run_campaign starts with
      identity path 'uncached'. The campaign runs under the cached projection's launch counts and
      hour cap, and nothing announces the path change.
    EVIDENCE:
      stages.py:701 `path_cached = guard.cached_path_admitted(unit, "campaign")`. run_campaign's
      signature (stages.py:670-675) has no path or lock-path parameter. _finish_gate and
      run_campaign print only session lines, bar messages, the STAGE status and file paths.
    FIX:
      Pass the locked path through identity_extra and refuse when cached_path_admitted disagrees
      with it (in either direction). Print the path on the STAGE line.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The finding describes the code correctly but is not a defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or
      DESIGN.  What the code does: run_campaign (stages.py:701) and run_gate_stage (stages.py:389)
      take the path only from guard.cached_path_admitted. No locked-path argument exists
      (identity_extra is pinned to exactly lock_sha256 and proposal_sha256, stages.py:219-226). A
      missing or malformed artifact gives uncached, and the path is not printed on stdout.  Why that
      is not a defect: (1) DESIGN §1 (guard) requires exactly this. A missing, malformed or
      undeterminable artifact gives False ("an undetermined licence is not a granted one"). DESIGN
      §1 (stages) says sessions run "with path from guard". The builder followed the binding design.
      (2) The spec defines the guard by the artifact, not by the lock. m:267 says the guard "permits
      cached=True only when the unit's G-ID artifact is on disk with PASS". m:282 ("LOCK-m-F0 v0.2.
      Path; projections") says nothing about the package reading the lock's path or cross-checking
      it. The lock enters the identity by hash, which m:267 lists as an identity field. The spec
      asks for no stdout path line. (3) The fallback is fail-closed. Uncached needs no licence and
      is a valid instrument (f1's reference path, G-TIME's path). Cached is the path that needs
      G-ID. Q-F0's cached floors (96 for A0, m:182) are at least the uncached ones (80), so power is
      not undercut. A slower uncached run that reaches the PI's hour cap ends in CAP, a governed
      terminal state that the report records (m:48: "arms launch until floor, cap or PI stop"). (4)
      The change is not silent in the record. The path is in the checkpoint identity and in the
      pick-free report's identity summary (_ident_summary includes "path"). A resume across a path
      change is refused by the identity check. (5) The trigger needs repo damage that git shows.
      data/m/gates/*.json is tracked (`git check-ignore -v` matches the negation
      `!/data/m/gates/*.json`, .gitignore:231). A fresh checkout therefore carries the artifact, and
      moving or deleting it appears in `git status`. (6) Nothing can run it yet. At step 4 the CLI
      refuses the campaign subcommand until step 16 (m_sessions.py STEP_WORDS/refusal), and
      run_campaign is "built and self-tested on the stub only". How LOCK v1.0's values reach it is a
      later wiring step.  A path cross-check against the lock would be reasonable hardening, but the
      spec and DESIGN do not require it.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. At stages.py:389 and stages.py:701, the path comes only from
      guard.cached_path_admitted, which reads the artifact on disk. guard._gid_pass catches OSError,
      UnicodeDecodeError and ValueError and returns False, so a missing or malformed artifact
      quietly gives the uncached path. Neither run_gate_stage nor run_campaign takes a locked-path
      argument. _extra refuses any identity_extra key beyond lock_sha256 and proposal_sha256, and a
      self-test at stages.py:900 confirms that a 'path' key is refused. No code in the package reads
      a lock's contents: lock_sha256 is only format-checked in resume.Identity. Stdout carries only
      session_line ("{stage} {template} s={s} {status}"), bar messages, "STAGE name status" and file
      paths. The path appears only in the report's identity summary (_ident_summary) and in the
      checkpoint. The spec backs the premise. m:267 says Qwen rounds run "cached iff that unit's
      G-ID passes, else uncached". LOCK-m-F0 v0.2 fixes "Path; projections for Q-F0 under its path".
      LOCK v1.0 then sets the launch counts and the hour cap, which run_campaign receives as caller
      arguments. So if the lock says cached and the artifact is absent, the run is uncached under
      projections made for the cached path, and nothing on stdout announces it. One caveat:
      data/m/gates/*.json is un-ignored, so it travels once committed and a "fresh checkout" loses
      it only if it was never committed. Moving the file aside or a damaged merge still produce the
      scenario. The severity stays minor for three reasons: uncached is always a licensed path, a
      later resume refuses a path change through the identity, and the stage commands still refuse
      to run at step 4.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: identity_extra carries locked_path; Qwen qual and campaign refuse when the licence disagrees in either direction; G-TIME and OLMo require uncached.

LENS verifier-tests

COVERED (reviewer's own statement of what it checked clean):
  Method: everything ran on a scratch copy of the repo (review/verifier-tests/pkg, with the needed
  data copied). Nothing under the repo was touched and no model was loaded. In the copy the baseline
  gives 498/498, and the selftest writes nothing into the repo tree (find -newer is empty).
  Mutation campaign: 168 single-site mutations were listed (work/muts1-5.py, results in
  work/res1-5.json). One was not applied because its target string was not unique. Of the 167 run,
  121 were killed and 46 survived.  Survivors not reported, as equivalent or moot: - S16, S18, C04
  (k at 0.16 gives the same k on this pool), R10 and T17: equivalent on every reachable state. - T18
  and T19: each covers the other, because the stage-load refusal is tested at stages.py:969-1000. -
  R05 and R06: sham seed and property do not depend on the pick. - R04 (redacted sham keeps item):
  moot, because m:271's redacted Task line already shows the item.  Killed, so these areas are
  clean: - keys: 6/6. - banner: 7/7. This includes the contrast fill swap, clause, line order and
  the mirror. - corr: delivery, split, replacement and roster skip. - audit: claim order v2 -> echo
  -> content -> lexical, Qwen lenient and OLMo any-round end rules, the reasoning label, echo case.
  - bars: <= vs <, p0s, crits, DEGEN classes, one-claim threshold, sham-domain guard, abs binding. -
  readrule: the strip whitelist (strong differential test), printed contrast free class, free hair-
  trigger withholding. - guard: G-ID licence fields, time and OLMo refusal of the cached path, label
  ids. - resume: identity two-way equality, skip-only. - schedule: GATE_TABLE rows, permutation
  form, committed data and manifest (D01, D02). - runners: pick line, the cached pass-through, free-
  end rules, echo sets, the OLMo context rule. - stages: the gate-stage G-VER gate, G-ID
  identity/2-pair/completeness, status, end-bar stage filter, contrast free-phase announcement, look
  cadence, contrast reads stripped. - scaffold: rotation, menu index and n, menu order, start order,
  seeds, extra draws, header tag, sham delivery, gate item key, arm key, j, pole, strict-first, f1
  swap, boundary assertion, check_cell. These are caught by the independent recomputations in the
  scaffold and runner selftests even though the verifier shares the scaffold code path with the
  runner, as DESIGN §1.11 prescribes.  Record-level tests (work/rec_mut.py, pastend.py, fuzz.py,
  edge.py): verify_session flags each of the following on both substrates: - a harness byte and a
  generation byte; - seed, item, menu order and keys (arm and items); - banner and Note lines, cell,
  stage change, j; - domain, complete, strict, hair triggers; - picked role and pole, pole,
  property, template_index, menu_index, menu bytes, piece offsets; - pick vs read.pick, a truncated
  session, a session continuing past its end rule. It does not flag: - a pick inconsistent with
  p_a/p_b, or a nonzero verify_kl (reported above); - a cached path claimed without a licence (the
  identity carries the path); - a newline inside a generation, or a scaffold string the recorded
  audit missed. G-VER is scoped to harness bytes and trusts the recorded audit by design, and these
  are not reported.  G-VER ordering per m:284: verify_and_mark runs in _launch before record_session
  and write_checkpoint at all three call sites (stages.py:405, :536, :752). A failure quarantines
  the record in the stop block and prints no pick. Messages name no label or index.  DESIGN §0 rule
  7: every module exposes _selftest(chk) and is model-free with tempfile scratch. Coverage of
  individual clauses is uneven, as listed above.  Not covered: real-model and real-tokenizer
  behaviour (the OLMo accept path, boundary on box), and the frozen adapters' newline-stop edge,
  which is outside the m package.

      `read.verify_kl` is exactly 0.0. A record whose recorded pick contradicts its recorded

    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      (a) A record rebuilt consistently with its first pick flipped and the read probabilities left

      selftest: _read_dict swapping p_a/p_b (scaffold.py:358), and the read called with the labels
      reversed (runner_qwen.py:160, runner_olmo.py:157). The reversed call misattributes p_a/p_b to
      the menu labels and moves the tie-break to menu_b.

      'alternate history -- first pick flipped consistently' PASSES on both substrates. Mutants S22,
      Q11 and O06 SURVIVED 498/498. work/pick_consistency.py: on every stub read the pick equals
      argmax(p_a,p_b) over (menu_a,menu_b) (139/139) and verify_kl == 0.0 (139/139), so the added
      check would never stop a healthy run.
    FIX:
      At verify.py:154, also require `pick == (menu_a if read['p_a'] >= read['p_b'] else menu_b)`
      and `read.get('verify_kl') == 0.0`. Both are template_breakage.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The behaviour is real, but it is not a defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN. m:284
      limits G-VER to "every session's harness bytes reconstructed from its recorded fields, the
      committed schedule and the rng streams". m:271 defines template_breakage as "harness bytes
      fail G-VER". The read's p_a/p_b and verify_kl are readout fields, not harness bytes. The only
      harness byte that depends on the read is the pick line, and verify already rebuilds it from
      the recorded pick and requires that pick to equal read.pick and to be a menu label
      (verify.py:153-155). DESIGN §1.14 lists G-VER's checks, and the built verifier makes all of
      them; it does not ask G-VER to recheck the readout's argmax or its KL. The "KL exactly 0.0"
      requirement (m:172, DESIGN §1.10) is enforced where DESIGN puts it, at read time.
      guard.insulated_read (guard.py:95-109) halts with a CONSTRUCTION INCIDENT on any KL other than
      float 0.0, NaN included, before anything is recorded. A record with a nonzero verify_kl
      therefore cannot come from the runner; it needs a hand edit. Nor can a pick that contradicts
      p_a/p_b arise from the built code. Both runners record pick = cr.pick, which
      readout.choice_read computes itself (label_a if pa >= pb), and they call the read with
      (menu.a, menu.b) in order (runner_qwen.py:160, runner_olmo.py:157). The finding's scenarios
      are a forged record or hypothetical regressions of a correct runner. No m consumer reads
      p_a/p_b; they are descriptive. A reversed-label regression would move only the exact-float-tie
      case, and the arm rng counterbalances menu order. The proposed fix would file readout
      inconsistencies under template_breakage, a hair trigger the spec defines on harness bytes, so
      the fix itself stretches m:271. The surviving mutants S22/Q11/O06 show a selftest coverage gap
      in the runners. That is a separate test-coverage note, not a defect in verify.py.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate, so it is not refuted. At valence_core/m/verify.py:154, the only check
      on a choice's read is that `pick` is one of (menu_a, menu_b) and equals `read.pick`. Nothing
      in verify_session looks at `p_a`, `p_b` or `verify_kl`. I ran this against the real repo
      package: a record whose p_a and p_b are swapped, so the pick is no longer the read's argmax,
      verifies on both substrates, and so does one with `verify_kl` set to 0.5 or deleted. For real
      reads the impact is limited. `guard.insulated_read` (guard.py:101-108) already halts at run
      time unless verify_kl is exactly 0.0, so that half only matters for a mis-wired runner or an
      edited record. The pick-vs-probability half is still unchecked. A swapped `_read_dict`
      (scaffold.py:358) or a read called with the menu labels reversed would misattribute p_a/p_b
      and move the tie-break to menu_b. Neither the verifier nor the runner selftests check the pick
      against p_a/p_b; the selftests check only verify_kl and the scripted picks. The spec's G-VER
      (m:284) is scoped to "harness bytes", so this is extra protection, not a mandated check, and
      minor is the right grade. One citation is wrong: m:159 is §2.1 (keys and streams) and does not
      mention choice_read or KL. `choice_read(verify=True)` is at m:54, the tie-to-label_a rule is

      proposed fix is safe because readout.py normalises p_a and p_b by the same positive
      denominator, which preserves pa >= pb.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: verify ties the pick to p_a/p_b (strict comparison; an exact tie accepts either label, PIN) and requires verify KL exactly 0.0; template_breakage.

      The raises are real. I reproduced all four. One part of the evidence is wrong: a Qwen record
      carrying a constructible OLMo audit dict also raises ("qwen_ends on a non-Qwen round"). It

      SessionLog.add_round builds every round as a dict carrying ra.as_dict(). add_choice builds

      through JSON and then calls assert_native. Model output only fills in string values and a
      pick, and the pick has already gone through the insulated read. It cannot change a record's
      types or structure. A substrate-mismatched audit is ruled out twice. The runner type-checks
      its audit object (OLMoTaskRoundAudit or QwenTaskRoundAudit), and it calls _audit.olmo_ends or
      qwen_ends on every round itself (runner_olmo.py:144 and :171, runner_qwen.py:147 and :178).

      path loads records from disk into verify_session, since resume does not re-verify. Every one
      of these malformations would need a code edit, and the selftest would catch that edit. It
      verifies every produced record of both suites clean. Even if such a record did appear, the
      stage fails closed: nothing is written to sessions or the checkpoint. Because the defect would
      come from deterministic code, a resume would raise again rather than produce a clean record.
      DESIGN §1.14's "Returns [] iff verified" still holds, because these records never get [].
      m:271 and m:284 bind on harness bytes and schedule mismatches that a real session can produce,

      hardening, but nothing in the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN requires it.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. On the OLMo side, line 182 only flags a round whose audit substrate

    TITLE: Seed and menu-order departures are classed schedule_mismatch, outside m:271's definition
    CLAIM:
      PIN 15 (verify.py:39-43) includes `seed` in sched_fields (:133-138) and classes a menu-order
      departure (:149-150) as schedule_mismatch. Seeds and menu orders come from the arm rng and are
      not in m:271's schedule_mismatch list. Under the spec a G-VER failure on them is
      template_breakage. The selftests enshrine the PIN (verify.py:276-281; m_sessions.py:288-289
      expects gver_seed -> schedule_mismatch).
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      A seed or menu-order departure stops the stage under the wrong declared bar name. The
      checkpoint stop block and stdout say `schedule_mismatch`, which by m:271 asserts that the
      committed schedule (pair/map/start/clause/banner/item stream) was departed from when it was
      not. The outcome (a STOP) is the same.
    EVIDENCE:
      work/rec_mut.py: 'changed seed' -> schedule_mismatch; the verify selftest's 'changed menu
      order' case expects schedule_mismatch. Compare m:271's enumeration.

      flag PIN 15 in the BUILD entry as a departure from m:271, not a fill of an open choice.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The finding describes the code correctly, but it is wrong to call that a departure from the
      spec. Its key premise, "under the spec a G-VER failure on them is template_breakage", does not
      hold for seeds. m:271 defines template_breakage as "harness bytes fail G-VER". A generation
      seed is never a harness byte: it does not appear in the banner, headers, Task lines, menus or
      transcript. So the spec places a seed departure under neither name. m:271's two definitions do
      not cover every field G-VER (m:284) reconstructs from the record, the schedule and the rng
      streams, which leaves the build an open choice. It filled that choice with a declared PIN
      (verify.py:39-43; phaseB.md item 15). The choice also follows the spec's own pattern. m:271
      already puts "item stream" under schedule_mismatch, and the item stream is a key-derived rng
      stream (m-items|{s}, m:159), not a field of the schedule. So the spec already uses "committed
      schedule" loosely enough to cover the deterministic key streams. Seeds and menu order are
      draws from the arm rng keyed under the same m:159 rule (`int(rng.integers(2**31))`,
      `rng.integers(2)`), so grouping them with the item stream extends the spec's own usage rather
      than contradicting it. DESIGN §1.14 tells the verifier to assert seeds and menu (a,b) but does
      not say which class they belong to.  Menu order: a departure that actually changes the harness
      also produces template_breakage in the problem list, both from the menu-bytes check and from
      the transcript check. The checkpoint stop block keeps every problem (stages.py:321), so the
      adjudication read sees both classes and the exact field. Only the one-word bar label comes
      from the first problem, and that is itself a declared PIN (stages.py:270-273). Both names are
      hair triggers with the same unit, the same domain and the same result: the stage stops. No bar
      count, verdict or downstream branch depends on which name is shown. A departure can also only
      come from a runner or record bug, because the runner writes seed and menu order from the same
      scaffold the verifier rebuilds.  At most this is a wording point for the BUILD entry. The PIN
      could say it extends m:271's list from the item stream to the other m:159 arm-rng draws. That
      is not a defect.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. m:271 defines schedule_mismatch as a closed list: pair, map, start
      order, clause order, banner bytes or item stream differing from the committed schedule. m:159
      draws the per-round generation seeds and the free-choice menu order (`rng.integers(2)`) from
      the arm rng, not from the committed schedule. Start order in m:159 is the forced-round order
      of the P/Q labels, which is a different thing from menu order. So neither seeds nor menu order
      appear in m:271's list. verify.py nevertheless puts `seed` in `sched_fields` (:133) and tags
      any mismatch there as MISMATCH (:136-138, :159-160). It also tags a menu (a,b) departure as
      MISMATCH (:149-150). DESIGN.md:131 lists schedule_mismatch as "cell/banner/item stream" only,
      so the code goes beyond its own build design as well as the spec. stages._verify_hit (:274)
      names the STOP after the first problem's class, so a seed or menu-order departure stops the
      stage under `schedule_mismatch`. The outcome, a STOP, is the same either way, so minor is the
      right severity. The finding's suggestion that template_breakage is the spec class for these is
      a reading, not a quote, because m:271 defines template_breakage as "harness bytes fail G-VER".
      Its main point still holds: this classification is outside m:271's enumeration and should be
      recorded as a departure, not as a fill of an open choice.
    DISPOSITION: DISCLOSED (PIN): seed and menu-order departures are classed schedule_mismatch (arm-rng departures from the committed construction).

    TITLE: The stage driver's pre-session schedule_check cannot fire
    CLAIM:
      _launch builds the scaffold from `cell` through m_config/build_banner. schedule_check
      (verify.py:53-69) then compares that scaffold's cell and banner with the same `cell` and the
      same build_banner, so it is true by construction. It cannot fire. The docstrings at
      stages.py:16-17 and runner_qwen.py:27-28 present it as the pre-session schedule_mismatch

      session byte rebuild (verify.py:115, :172).

      schedule_check(sc, cell) at :254.

      Drop the call or document it as a tautology, and cite verify.py:115/:172 plus load_committed
      as m:253's run-time assertion. To make the pre-check meaningful, compare against an

      banner._spec_lines already does at selftest).
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The facts are right, but nothing here is a defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN. At
      stages.py:253-254 the scaffold is built from `cell` and then checked against that same `cell`,
      with `build_banner` called on the same arguments. I ran it over all 13,208 committed cells and
      it returned [] every time. But this is exactly what DESIGN §1.12 asks for:
      "`schedule_mismatch` checked before the session starts (banner bytes vs build_banner; cell vs
      committed schedule) by the stage driver". Phase B also disclosed `schedule_check` as the way
      it meets §1.12.  A check that cannot fire on correct code is an ordinary consistency
      assertion. It is not dead logic. It would fire if `m_config`'s handling of the cell changed
      (check_cell/_copy_cell normalising it) or if its banner call were miswired, for example P and
      Q swapped. The verify selftest already shows the function fires on a cell that differs
      (verify.py:318).  The spec's run-time requirements are met elsewhere, and the module says so:
      - m:271 schedule_mismatch "differing from the committed schedule": `load_committed`
      (stages.py:154-188) checks the directory is clean, each file's sha256 against the manifest,
      and the header and cells sha. - m:253 "byte-equality asserted at run time": `verify_session`
      rebuilds the transcript from banner + pieces and compares it byte for byte before the record
      is written (m:284).  The stages.py docstring at lines 13-15 already names the committed,
      manifest-verified schedule file as the source of the cell. Lines 16-17 only say
      `schedule_check` "runs before the session", which is true. They do not claim it is the only
      protection.  The finding's own failure scenario is a future BUILD or LOCK entry that might
      overclaim the check. That is hypothetical, and the finding concedes "No wrong result follows
      today."  The surviving mutants T20/T21 show only that no test makes `m_config` diverge from
      the committed cell. That is expected for a guard against a bug that does not exist.  One doc

      template_breakage after it. That is incomplete, because `verify_session` also returns
      schedule_mismatch (verify.py:101-113). It is wording only.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. In stages._launch the check compares the scaffold against the same
      `cell` it was just built from, and verify.schedule_check rebuilds the banner with the same
      pure, deterministic build_banner on the same arguments that m_config used. So the call returns
      [] by construction and cannot fire. If the cell were malformed, m_config's check_cell or
      build_banner would raise first, so schedule_check's own except branch is unreachable from this
      call site too. The docstrings at stages.py:16-17 and runner_qwen.py:27-28 still present this
      call as the pre-session schedule_mismatch check. m:253 does require the banner bytes to be

      no result, so minor is the right grade.

      survives: with `_scaffold_hits` monkeypatched in-process, `run_selftest()` still gives

      spec nor CLAUDE.md asks for selftest coverage; rule 26 is about reproducing published numbers.

--- #38 [CONTESTED] reviewer:minor lenient:not-a-defect (refuted) strict:minor
    valence_core/m/guard.py:106  (m:159 / rule-26 (iii) (KL exactly 0.0))
    TITLE: No test covers insulated_read's halt on a negative verify KL
    CLAIM:
      guard.py:104-105 states that the exact-zero test exists because assert_construction_stable
      lets NaN and negative values through. Only the NaN case is tested (guard.py:906-916).
      Loosening the test to `kl <= 1e-6` passes, and that loosening would admit negative KLs.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      After such a regression, a negative symmetric KL, which is not 0.0 exactly, would be accepted
      as a stable construction without any test failing.
    EVIDENCE:
      Mutant G05 (`kl <= 1e-6` at guard.py:106) SURVIVED 498/498.
    FIX:
      Add a _Drift variant that returns a negative verify KL (or monkeypatch symmetric_kl) and
      assert that insulated_read halts.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The facts are right, but nothing in the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN is broken. The production
      guard behaves exactly as the spec requires: insulated_read halts on any verify KL that is not
      exactly 0.0, negative values included. I confirmed this by running it. The missing negative-
      value test is a hypothetical regression guard, not a defect. First, the spec (m:172,
      "symmetric KL exactly 0.0") states a runtime requirement that the code meets. It sets no unit-
      test or mutation-kill requirement. Second, DESIGN §0 rule 7 asks each selftest to cover "a
      case that must fire AND one that must not". insulated_read has both: it accepts a reproducible
      read and halts on positive drift and on NaN. DESIGN sets no per-branch or mutation-coverage
      bar. Third, a negative value is practically unreachable. symmetric_kl takes the max of the two
      KL directions. With monotone log and product rounding, the two directions sum to a nonnegative
      total term by term, so only float summation rounding could push both below zero. I ran 23,000
      adversarial ulp-level perturbations of logits and of probabilities and got no negative value
      (minimum 0.0). The finding's spec_ref is also misplaced: m:159 is the keys/streams clause, and
      the read clause is at m:172. If kept at all, this is an optional test-hardening note.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The claim holds. The selftest checks insulated_read's halt only for a positive KL (_Drift) and
      a NaN KL (_Drift(nan=True)), never for a negative one. A positive KL is already stopped by the
      frozen assert_construction_stable (VERIFY_KL_BAR = 0.0), so loosening the guard to `kl <=
      1e-6` changes behaviour only for negative values. Neither existing test reaches that case, so
      the loosened version passes the whole suite. I reproduced it: the mutant gives 498/498, and it
      accepts a negative verify KL that the original rejects. verify.py does not check verify_kl
      after the fact, so nothing downstream catches it either. One detail is wrong: the spec_ref
      "m:159" does not point at KL text, and the spec has no line about KL = 0.0 in that area. The
      exact-zero requirement does exist, though, in CLAUDE.md rule 26 (iii) ("KL = 0.0 exactly") and
      in readout.py:34-38 (the "operative form" is exact equality). This is a test-coverage gap with
      no wrong behaviour today, so minor is the right severity.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: insulated_read halts on negative, tiny-positive and infinite KLs.

--- #40 [CONFIRMED] reviewer:substantive lenient:minor strict:substantive
    valence_core/m/stages.py:235  (CLAUDE.md rule 27 (verdict-relevant runs pin seeds, library versions and determinism flags in the logged entry); m:251 (the backend identifier is the adapter's provenance record); precedents format_recon.py:762, k1_stage1.py:291, box_b3_sessions.py:360)
    TITLE: Rule 27: stage artifacts record only a hash of the adapter's provenance; library versions, determinism flags and seeds are recorded nowhere
    CLAIM:
      _identity turns adapter.provenance() into resume.backend_identifier, a sha16
      (stages.py:229-236, resume.py:151-156), and stores only that hash. The checkpoint
      (resume._fresh, resume.py:287-290), the stage reports (stages.py:441-450, 583-591, 801-811 via
      _ident_summary, stages.py:358-360) and stdout never contain the provenance dict itself. The
      hash cannot be inverted, so a stage's artifacts cannot show which library versions, CUDA,
      determinism flags or seed produced them. Every precedent runner writes adapter.provenance()
      verbatim into its artifact.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      An OLMo gate stage runs on a box that is then stopped, which is the normal case under
      ephemeral pods. Its committed checkpoint and report carry backend_identifier '3f…' but no
      torch, transformers or CUDA version and no deterministic flag. A gate-stage pod change
      (resume.py tolerates a backend_identifier change) leaves no record of what changed. The logged
      entry cannot pin what rule 27 requires from the repo alone.
    EVIDENCE:
      exp3.py runs G-TIME-F0 on the ScriptedAdapter into a temp dir. Checkpoint keys:
      ['identity','looks','schema','sessions','state']; identity keys have no provenance; report
      keys: [...,'identity',...]; 'library_versions present: False | provenance present: False'.
      Stdout carries only session lines, the bar, the STAGE line and paths.
    FIX:
      Write adapter.provenance() verbatim into the checkpoint at init and append it on every resume,
      as a 'provenance' list beside state, kept out of the identity. Copy it into each stage report
      and print one PROVENANCE line at stage start, as format_recon and b3 do.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is partly wrong and partly right.  **Where it is right.** The provenance dict
      never reaches any m artifact or stdout. `_identity` stores only `backend_identifier =
      sha16(provenance minus n_encodes_clipped/path/seed)` (stages.py:235, resume.py:151-156).
      `_fresh` has no provenance field (resume.py:287-290), and the reports carry only
      `_ident_summary` (stages.py:358-360, :441-450, :583-591). The stage prints session lines, the
      bar messages, STAGE and the paths. So library versions and the adapter's own determinism flags
      (deterministic, CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG) are not in the stage's committed files. That departs
      from a practice every precedent runner follows: format_recon.py:762, k1_stage1.py:291,
      box_b3_sessions.py:360 and box_determinism.py:60 all write `adapter.provenance()` verbatim.
      m:251 also calls the backend identifier "the adapter's provenance record". Under the build's
      hash pin, the record itself is never kept, so the identifier written into an OLMo lock cannot
      be audited from the repo.  **Where it overstates.** 1. "Seeds recorded nowhere" is false.
      Every round's generation seed is in the checkpoint's session record (scaffold.py:389 `"seed":
      spec.seed`). The key templates that derive them are in the identity (`key_templates`,
      resume.py IDENTITY_KEYS). The only seed dropped is the adapter's construction seed, and on
      OLMo the per-call seed overrides it (olmo_hf.py:221 `t.manual_seed(seed)`). 2. Rule 27 binds
      "the logged entry", not the package's JSONs. For OLMo, the spec requires a `BACKEND —
      characterization` entry on every new box before any experimental forward (m:251, m:302). Its
      tool, box_determinism.py:60-61, saves and prints `adapter.provenance()`. The checkpoint's
      `state.pod_id_seen` ties a stage to its pod. So in the gate-stage pod-change scenario, each
      box's versions and flags are on record in that box's BACKEND entry. "Leaves no record of what
      changed" is largely mitigated. 3. Nothing can run at step 4. Every stage subcommand in
      m_sessions.py refuses with exit 2 ("not licensed until step N"), and the code that will build
      the adapter and capture stdout to results/raw is wired at the stage's own step. A PROVENANCE
      line there would meet rule 27. 4. DESIGN §1.9 itself pins `backend_identifier` as a sha16 and
      nowhere requires the dict in artifacts, so the builders followed the binding design.
      **Residual defect.** The stage artifacts do not record the run's own determinism flags or, on
      the laptop, the mlx/numpy versions. Rule 27 says verdict-relevant runs pin these; for laptop
      Q-F0 there is no per-run BACKEND entry to fall back on. It is a record-keeping gap against
      rule 27's intent and the repo's universal precedent. It changes no gate decision or verdict,
      and the fix is a few lines before the first stage runs. That makes it minor, not substantive.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The main claim holds. A stage keeps the adapter's provenance only as a 16-character hash, in
      identity.backend_identifier, and writes none of it to the checkpoint, the report, the reads or
      stdout. So the artifacts cannot show which library versions, CUDA version, GPU or determinism
      flags produced them. One part of the finding is overstated: seeds are not "recorded nowhere".
      Every round in the checkpoint carries its generation seed, and the identity carries
      key_templates. What is missing is the adapter-level 'seed' field, which BACKEND_DROP removes
      from the hash. That correction does not save the finding. The repo's own adapter contract says
      provenance is "Written verbatim into result artifacts" (base.py:354-357). All three cited
      precedents write adapter.provenance() verbatim. The builder's own schedule.py:22-23 notes that
      numpy versions differ between the laptop and the box, so the versions matter here. A Q-F0
      campaign on the laptop has no BACKEND entry to fall back on, so nothing in the repo would pin
      its MLX or numpy versions. On a gate-stage pod change, the set-aside checkpoint and the new
      one differ only by an opaque hash. Small, cheap to fix, and a real gap under rule 27 and the
      adapter contract, so it stays substantive.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 2): adapter.provenance() verbatim in the checkpoint (appended per invocation, outside the identity), copied to the report, one PROVENANCE line at stage start (ledger 27).

      attractor ("What is prism?", m:271 / rule 28(c)), so the trigger is realistic. Pauses persist
      in the checkpoint, so every later report write fails the same way.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      The campaign pauses at a look, and the seat's adjudication is passed as "... the sham's free-
      round claims quote 'What is prism?' ...". The resumed campaign runs to its floors and records
      terminal COMPLETE in the checkpoint, then raises ValueError. No STAGE line is printed.
      reports/Q-F0_campaign_report.json keeps the earlier status PAUSE. A further resume is refused
      with 'CAMPAIGN ENDED', so the pick-free end report is never produced.
    EVIDENCE:
      exp2.py (scratchpad review/hygiene): first run 'PAUSE'; resume with that adjudication prints
      'resume RAISED ValueError campaign-Q-F0: a roster label reached a pick-free artifact; refused
      | sessions in ckpt: 44 | report status: PAUSE'.
    FIX:
      Keep the adjudication text in the checkpoint only. Put a sha16 or 'adjudicated: true' in the
      report's pauses. Alternatively, check the adjudication string for roster words when it is
      passed in, and refuse it before any session runs.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      I could not refute the finding. The code behaves as claimed. `run_campaign` stores the seat's
      adjudication text verbatim in the pause entry (stages.py:723). It then copies `camp["pauses"]`
      into the pick-free report (stages.py:810). `_assert_pick_free` (stages.py:333-336) runs a
      case-insensitive whole-word match for 12 roster words over the whole report (amber, copper,
      granite, maple, plaza, prism, quartz, ridge, saddle, slate, velvet, walnut). It raises
      ValueError at stages.py:812, which comes after the session loop and after every checkpoint
      write. The trigger is realistic. Besides quoting the attractor ("What is prism?", m:271 / rule
      28(c)), several roster words are ordinary English: an adjudication saying "start from a clean
      slate" crashes the same way.  The harm is worse than the finding states. The end-look BAR
      lines are printed only after the report write (stages.py:815-816). On the crash path, the end
      look's results are lost from both stdout and the report. In my run the end look FIRED and
      recorded an end_look pause, so the guard's per-arm pre-pick counts at that look were lost too.
      That pause then exists only in the pick-bearing checkpoint, which m:318 bars the seat and the
      PI from reading before VERDICTS. That works against m:318 (per-arm pre-pick exclusion counts
      reported at each look) and m:330 (a fired bar pauses and the seat adjudicates in the open).
      There is no recovery inside the package. A later resume raises SystemExit "CAMPAIGN ENDED"
      (stages.py:715) before any report is built, and no other code path writes the campaign report.
      No PIN or builder report covers this: phaseC.md item 21 says only that the text "is recorded
      with the pause". It is not an off-box limitation either, because the stub adapter reproduces
      it.  Mitigating points, which keep this from being blocking: - It fails closed and loudly (a
      traceback, no pick leak). - The checkpoint keeps all session data, and no verdict changes. -
      The campaign CLI is not wired until step 16, so a caller could pass a label-free reference.
      Even so, the API accepts any string, nothing warns the caller, and the fix is cheap: validate
      the adjudication against the roster words when it is passed in, or keep only a hash or flag in
      the report.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=substantive):
      The finding is accurate, so I am not refuting it. stages.py:723 stores the seat's adjudication
      text, unchanged apart from stripping whitespace, in camp["pauses"][-1]. The report built at
      stages.py:800-811 copies camp["pauses"] in whole. _assert_pick_free (stages.py:333-336) runs a
      case-insensitive whole-word regex over json.dumps of the whole report. The regex is
      \b(?:amber|copper|granite|maple|plaza|prism|quartz|ridge|saddle|slate|velvet|walnut)\b, and it
      matches "What is prism?". It runs at stages.py:812, after the checkpoint (with terminal or the
      new pause) has been written at :796. No check is made on the adjudication string when it is
      passed in.  I copied the reviewer's experiment and ran it. The first run returns PAUSE (a

    .gitignore:220  (m:318 exceptions (2)-(4); DESIGN §0 rule 5)
    TITLE: The .gitignore comment and the stages docstring call reports/ 'pick-free', but G-ID's reads file there carries full sham records with picks, and it is diffable
    CLAIM:
      The appended .gitignore block says '... pick-free stage reports ... so no diff shows a pick
      before the unit's VERDICTS' (.gitignore:219-222). stages.py:38 says 'reports and reads are
      pick-free'. But _finish_gid writes readrule.release_full(records) (full A0s records,
      choices[].pick included) to reports/Q-F0_id_reads.json (stages.py:596-598). That path is
      whitelisted and not -diff (git check-attr: unspecified). The redacted sham reads also let the
      sham pick be recovered (readrule report). These are sham picks, which exceptions (2)-(4)
      license, so nothing leaks from a contrast session. Only the comments' claim is false.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      A reader trusts the comment that reports/ is pick-free and that no diff shows a pick. git log
      -p over data/m/reports/Q-F0_id_reads.json renders sham picks.
    EVIDENCE:
      git check-attr diff -- data/m/reports/Q-F0_qual_reads.json → 'diff: unspecified'.
      stages.py:596-598: reads = {..., "sessions": copy.deepcopy(readrule.release_full(records))}.
    FIX:
      Reword both comments: checkpoints are the only contrast-pick-bearing files; reports/ may carry
      sham picks under §4 exceptions (2)-(4). Alternatively, mark /data/m/reports/*_reads.json -diff
      as well.
    LENIENT REFUTER (refuted=True, severity=not-a-defect):
      The facts check out, but nothing here is a defect against the spec, CLAUDE.md or DESIGN.
      G-ID's reads file does hold full A0s records with choices[].pick in them. It sits under
      reports/, which the .gitignore whitelist admits. Its diff attribute is unspecified, so it is
      not marked -diff. All of that is behaviour the spec requires. m:271 says "G-ID's sham-only
      sessions are read in full at G-ID (nothing to blind; §4 exception (4))", and m:318 exception
      (4) licenses those full reads before VERDICTS. The code guarantees that only sham records can
      reach the file: run_gid_pair raises unless template == "A0s", and keys.is_contrast('A0s') is
      False. So no contrast pick can reach reports/, and the finding admits nothing leaks. What
      remains is wording. In this project, "pick-free" means free of contrast picks. DESIGN uses it
      that way itself: its stage_report is a "pick-free report" that includes the sham first-pick
      share (DESIGN.md:108). m:318 likewise says a sham session "carries no contrast information".
      Read that way, the .gitignore comment is accurate. The stages.py module docstring also states
      in its OUTPUTS paragraph (lines 42-44) that the reads file holds "G-ID's sham-only sessions in
      full, §4 exception (4)". The PIN comment at stages.py:593-595 says the same. So the build
      discloses the behaviour and does not hide it. The finding's failure scenario does not hold
      either. Someone who reads sham picks through git log -p is doing exactly what exception (4)
      allows, so no blinding rule is broken. At most, "pick-free" at stages.py:38 and :128 could say
      "contrast-pick-free". That is a cosmetic edit, not a defect.
    STRICT REFUTER (refuted=False, severity=minor):
      The finding is accurate. The new .gitignore block says "pick-free stage reports" and claims
      the -diff marking on checkpoints means "no diff shows a pick before the unit's VERDICTS". The
      stages.py docstring (line 38) says "reports and reads are pick-free", and the out_paths
      docstring (line 128) labels reports/{unit}_{stage}_reads.json "(pick-free)". But _finish_gid
      writes readrule.release_full(records) to that reads path. release_full returns its records
      unchanged, so the file holds full G-ID A0s session records with choices[].pick. The reads file
      is whitelisted and has no -diff attribute, so git diff and log -p would show those picks. The
      stages.py docstring even contradicts itself: line 43 says the reads file carries "G-ID's sham-
      only sessions in full". These are sham picks, which m:318 exceptions (2)-(4) allow, so no
      contrast information leaks. The defect is only that the comments are false, so minor is the
      right severity.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: the .gitignore/.gitattributes comments (Code) and stages' docstring/PIN (round 2) say reports carry no contrast pick, may carry sham picks under m:318 (2)-(4).
