--- N2 [dose-delivery] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:221  TITLE: The startup banner check and the gate line read a hard-coded v1_11 proposal file that is never compared with the identity's proposal_sha256
    CLAIM:
      Since the fixes, every stage start runs banner.spec_problems(proposal_path=PROPOSAL_PATH), and
      m:267's gate line hashes the same file. PROPOSAL_PATH is the literal REPO/'PROPOSAL-m-gating-
      campaign-v1_11.md' (stages.py:88). identity_extra['proposal_sha256'], 'this document's' hash
      as the lock pins it, is only checked as 64-hex (_extra, stages.py:459-462) and stored. Nothing
      compares it with the file's sha256. So the run-time banner check validates the banner against
      a document the run's identity does not name, and if that file moves, every stage is refused at
      startup.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      (1) A later revision, say v1.12, is committed and v1_11 is moved to archive/, as commit
      05c4636 did for v1.2-v1.10. Every stage, including the resume of an in-flight campaign, then
      refuses with 'STARTUP CHECKS FAIL ... banner spec: cannot read' until the code is re-pointed.
      Re-pointing changes package_sha256 and so refuses the resume anyway. (2) If v1_11 stays at
      root while the lock pins v1.12's hash, the banner check passes against the superseded
      document. The gate line then logs v1_11's hash while the checkpoint's identity records
      v1.12's, and nothing flags the disagreement.
    EVIDENCE:
      confirm/dose-delivery/proposal_bind.py runs G-TIME-F0 on the stub with the committed schedules
      copied in.
      - With the real sha256 (42ab36ecc814c9c4...), the run PASSES.
      - With proposal_sha256 = 'ab'*32, it also PASSES, and the checkpoint's identity records
      abababababababab, while spec_problems read v1_11.
      - With the real sha but PROPOSAL_PATH pointed at a copy whose TOLD-OBJ literal differs, it
      fails 'STARTUP CHECKS FAIL: banner spec: TOLD_OBJ differs'.
      - With PROPOSAL_PATH absent, it fails 'STARTUP CHECKS FAIL: banner spec: cannot read'.
      
      grep shows that proposal_sha256 in stages.py is only validated and passed to _resume.Identity
      (stages.py:453-462, 504). The pre-fix stages.py (scratchpad f1_stages_orig.py) had no
      PROPOSAL_PATH and no run-time proposal read.
    FIX:
      In _begin or right after _extra, refuse with a SystemExit unless sha256(PROPOSAL_PATH bytes)
      == extra['proposal_sha256'], or resolve the proposal file from the lock's recorded hash or
      path rather than a literal filename. Then spec_problems and the gate line read the document
      the identity names. The selftest's xtra() would pass the file's real sha.
    REFUTER:
      The finding is correct, and I reproduced it.
      
      **Code reading:**
      - `stages.py:88` sets the proposal path to a fixed file, `PROPOSAL_PATH = REPO / "PROPOSAL-m-
      gating-campaign-v1_11.md"`.
      - Every stage start reaches `_startup_checks` through `_begin`. That calls
      `startup_problems()`, which runs `_banner.spec_problems(proposal_path=PROPOSAL_PATH)`
      (`stages.py:221`).
      - `gate_line()` at `stages.py:269` hashes the same `PROPOSAL_PATH`.
      - `_extra()` at `stages.py:459-462` only checks that `proposal_sha256` is 64 lower-case hex.
      `_identity()` at `stages.py:504` then passes it into `_resume.Identity` unchanged.
      - `grep` finds no place where the sha256 of `PROPOSAL_PATH`'s bytes is compared with
      `identity_extra['proposal_sha256']`.
      - `banner.spec_problems` returns "banner spec: cannot read ..." when the file is missing, so
      every stage would refuse at startup.
      - The earlier copy of stages.py (`scratchpad/step4/f1_stages_orig.py`) has no `PROPOSAL_PATH`
      and no `spec_problems` call, so this coupling arrived with the fixes.
      
      **Reproduction:** I copied `proposal_bind.py` to `confirm/refute-proposal-bind/` and ran it
      against the real package on the stub.
      - With the real sha `42ab36ecc814c9c4`, G-TIME-F0 returns PASS.
      - With `proposal_sha256 = 'ab'*32`, it also returns PASS, and the checkpoint identity records
      `abababababababab`, while the banner check read v1_11.
      - With the real sha but a copy whose TOLD-OBJ literal differs, it stops with "STARTUP CHECKS
      FAIL: TOLD_OBJ differs / DECLARED_HARNESS_STRINGS differs".
      - With the file absent, it stops with "STARTUP CHECKS FAIL ... banner spec: cannot read".
      
      **Why it matters, and why it stays minor:**
      - m:267 says G-CAL's identity includes "this document's hash", and the gate line logs the
      proposal hash. The run's identity hash and the document the checks actually read can disagree,
      and nothing flags it.
      - Moving v1_11 to `archive/` (what 05c4636 did for v1.2–v1.10) would make every stage refuse
      at startup.
      - The failure is fail-closed or a provenance mismatch, not a wrong verdict.
      - The stage CLI paths are not licensed yet. `proposal_sha256` will be supplied externally at
      lock time.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (rounds 2-3): the proposal file is tracked at HEAD with sha256 == the identity's; round 3 takes it from identity_extra['proposal_file'] (required on non-stub).

--- N3 [schedule] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:1152  TITLE: Residual of #3 at k = 1: the committed schedule is now the hard bound and equals L there, so a healthy arm can still be stranded below floor, and only a PI stop ends it
    CLAIM:
      F1 replaced the L bound with the committed schedule's n (`if s >= n_sched[arm]` gives PAUSE
      schedule_exhausted). After that pause a resume carrying only an adjudication is refused
      (SCHEDULE EXHAUSTED); only pi_stop ends the campaign. The files are materialized to exactly
      the k = 1 L (schedule.py:98-108 L_MAX_K1: Q-F0 A0 2624, OLMo A0 2208, every sham 960). At k =
      1 there is therefore no headroom past L for Q-F0 cached A0, OLMo A0, or any sham. At that row,
      m:192's 'until each reaches its floor, the unit's cap, or a PI stop' is still cut short by the
      file length, exactly as #3 described for L. Below k = 1 there is headroom, except that Q-F0
      uncached A0 has 2624 cells against an L of 2208.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      Take a unit qualified at k = 1 (r = 1/24) whose locked hour cap allows the k = 1 plan (OLMo
      total 3,168 sessions, about 17-20 h at the OLMo basis; Q-F0 cached 3,584 launches). By exact
      binomial at r = 1/24, the arm runs out of committed cells below its floor with probability
      .086 (Q-F0 cached A0: 2624 cells, floor 96), .089 (OLMo A0: 2208, floor 80) and .081 (any
      sham: 960, floor 32). Q-F0 uncached A0 (2624 cells, floor 80) gives .0012. When that happens
      the unit PAUSEs and the PI cannot choose to continue to floor: extending the file changes the
      schedule hash in the identity, and the resume is refused.
    EVIDENCE:
      stages.py:1152-1159 (schedule_exhausted pause) and :1113-1120 (the SCHEDULE EXHAUSTED refusal
      without pi_stop). schedule.py:98-108 (materialized to 'the largest L the launch rule yields').
      The m_sessions selftest camp_exhaust case shows the PAUSE, then the refusal, then PI-STOP.
      Probabilities are from my exact-Fraction binomial computation (confirm/schedule, inline). F1
      discloses that exhaustion can only be PI-stopped (fix_F1.md:33) but does not say that at k = 1
      the file equals L.
    FIX:
      Either materialize headroom past the k = 1 L (for example, size MAX_UNITS so that P(shortfall
      at r = 1/24) is negligible, about +25% cells, regenerating data/m/schedules before any lock),
      or state the k = 1 limit and these probabilities in the PIN as a seat question, so that LOCK
      v1.0's cap is set knowing it.
    REFUTER:
      I checked every claim against the code and none is wrong, so the finding stands.
      
      1. The hard bound is the committed file. In stages.py:1079-1081, n_sched is read from the
      committed schedule files. The loop then pauses with schedule_exhausted when `s >=
      n_sched[arm]` (:1152-1159).
      
      2. Only a PI stop ends that pause. When the last pause is schedule_exhausted and no pi_stop is
      given, the resume is refused with SCHEDULE EXHAUSTED (:1113-1120). An adjudication alone does
      not resume.
      
      3. Extending the file is refused. The identity carries both schedule_sha256 and n
      (`_identity`, :495-506), and load_committed refuses any file whose hash does not match the
      manifest (:397-400).
      
      4. At k = 1 the files equal L. The committed sizes under data/m/schedules are: Q-F0 campaign
      A0 2624, A0s 960; every OLMo A0 2208 and A0s 960. These are exactly the k = 1 L values.
      Recomputing the launch rule at r = 1/24 with exact Fractions gives N90 = 2604, 2194 and 943,
      which ceil_unit to L = 2624, 2208 and 960. So for Q-F0 cached A0, OLMo A0 and every sham, the
      file has no cells past L. Q-F0 uncached A0 has 2624 cells against L = 2208.
      
      5. The probabilities reproduce exactly. P(Bin(cells, 1/24) < floor) is .0861 (2624 cells,
      floor 96), .0893 (2208, 80), .0810 (960, 32) and .0012 (2624, 80).
      
      6. This is a residual conflict with the spec. m:192 says "L is a projection, not a bound",
      with arms launching until floor, cap or PI stop. At the k = 1 row, about 8-9% of healthy arms
      run out of cells first, under the planning rate. The PI then cannot continue to floor and can
      only stop.
      
      Severity stays minor. The behaviour is declared in a code comment (PIN), and DESIGN.md:56
      materializes the files to the k = 1 L, so it is not hidden. Only the k = 1 row is affected,
      the PI stop ends the campaign as analyzed-as-obtained, and the fix is a regeneration or a seat
      question before any lock.
    DISPOSITION: DISCLOSED: at k = 1 the committed schedule (built to the k = 1 launch row) equals L and is the hard bound; an arm still short of its floor at the schedule's end PAUSEs for the PI.

      well (scen.py, run against the real package and both mirrors). On the real code, resuming with
      a changed projection is refused with CHECKPOINT MISMATCH and 0 adapter calls. L = 3000 against
      a committed n of 2624 raises ValueError, also with 0 calls. Under Mg, the resume with L
      {A0:32} runs silently to COMPLETE with 40 adapter calls. The checkpoint then records the
      projection {A0:6} while the report carries {A0:32}, exactly as the finding describes. Under
      Mk, L 3000 > 2624 runs to COMPLETE with 80 calls. Behaviour is correct today and the gap is
      only in the tests. The Mk consequence is mild, because L is a projection rather than a bound,
      so minor is the right severity.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 2): tests for the projection resume check and the L > committed-n guard.

--- N6 [audit-runners] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/audit.py:133  TITLE: Finding-5 PIN extends the period-less echo form to Qwen, where the stop-token mechanism does not apply, and changes Qwen session validity
    CLAIM:
      The fix applies the stem-at-end echo rule on both substrates ('applied on both substrates (one
      echo definition)', audit.py:124). Its only stated rationale is OLMo-specific: OLMo's newline
      stop drops the fused '.\n' token. According to the finding and both refuters, Qwen's
      vocabulary has no punctuation+newline tokens, so a Qwen runner never strips a final period. On
      Qwen, a stored period-less Note line is therefore a genuinely incomplete reproduction by the
      model. m:261 (and DESIGN §1.7 line 72) defines Qwen echo as a 'verbatim ... complete "Note:"
      line'. Under that literal, a period-less TOLD copy is an own-pair lexical hit, and a period-
      less COMMON copy is unclaimed. The PIN reclassifies both, and on Qwen that moves the valid-
      session rule of record (f1's free-round lexical exclusion, m:261) in the direction of keeping
      sessions.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      A Qwen contrast or sham session copies its TOLD line without the final period in free round 1.
      - Current code: the claim is echo, which counts as post_choice_degeneracy. The session is not
      excluded, runs all 6 reads and stays valid.
      - Spec literal (the pre-fix definition): the claim is lexical, excluded@free. The session ends
      after 1 read and is invalid.
      
      On a Qwen forced round:
      - A period-less TOLD copy moves from lexical@forced (outside round_degeneracy) to echo@forced
      (inside the numerator of the G-TIME-F0 and G-F0m-Qwen binomial bars).
      - A period-less COMMON copy moves from an unclaimed, valid, in-domain session to echo@forced,
      excluded and out of the domain.
      
      Qwen does end responses without terminal punctuation: 552 of the 1,894 e2a responses, per the
      finding's own tally. So this case can arise from real model output, not only from a storage
      artifact.
    EVIDENCE:
      confirm/audit-runners/c5q2.py runs real runner_qwen.run_session_qwen on the scripted stub,
      with the current harness_echo and with the pre-fix complete-line definition monkeypatched in:
      
      | Case | Current | Pre-fix |
      |---|---|---|
      | Free TOLD_np, sham s12 | claims [None x4, 'echo', None x5]; excl None; valid True; n_choices
      6 | claims [None x4, 'lexical']; excl ('lexical','free'); valid False; n_choices 1 |
      | Free TOLD_np, contrast s1 | same as sham | same as sham |
      | Forced COMMON_np, sham s11 | ('echo','forced'); valid False; domain False | no claim; valid
      True; domain True |
      
      c5q.py: a forced TOLD_np gives degen_rounds 1 now and 0 pre-fix.
      
      The selftest case testing.py:338 ('qwen free echoes without the final period (TOLD, COMMON)
      end nothing') and the fix report ('OLMo ends the session; a Qwen free round does not') show
      the Qwen extension is intended. No authority or mechanism is cited for the Qwen half. The
      finding's own text says 'Qwen is not affected'.
      
      Related wording residue: bars.py _JUST_PAUSE still calls echo 'a verbatim reproduction of a
      harness line'.
    FIX:
      Either scope the stem clause to OLMo, with a substrate argument to harness_echo passed from
      OLMoTaskRoundAudit only, and keep Qwen on m:261's complete-line form; or keep one definition
      and declare the Qwen half explicitly as a departure from m:261's 'complete' for the seat at
      LOCK v0.1, noting that it converts a would-be free-round lexical exclusion into a valid
      session. If scoped, update the Qwen selftest cases s10-s12 to expect lexical@forced, no claim,
      and lexical@free respectively.
    REFUTER:
      I could not refute any factual part of the finding. I checked each claim by reading and
      running the real package, imported read-only.
      
      1. **The rule applies on both substrates.** `harness_echo` (valence_core/m/audit.py:113-136)
      has no substrate argument. Its stem-at-end clause (`t.endswith(stem)` when the line ends in
      `.`, `!` or `?`) therefore runs on Qwen as well as OLMo. The PIN says so itself ("applied on
      both substrates (one echo definition)"). The fix_F2 report confirms it was intended: "OLMo
      ends the session; a Qwen free round does not". Selftest cases s10-s12 in testing.py:331-340
      encode the Qwen half.
      
      2. **The stated rationale only covers OLMo.**
         - The Qwen adapter has the same drop-the-stop-token loop (qwen_mlx.py:292-300, 317-321). On
      Qwen, though, the period survives: of the 1,894 e2a Qwen responses (data/e2a/sessions.jsonl),
      958 end in `.` and 1,342 in `.`, `!` or `?`. On OLMo it is lost (8 of 3,462 b3 responses end
      in punctuation, per the finding-5 evidence).
         - The other 552 Qwen responses end without terminal punctuation: titles, quotes, emoji,
      truncations. So the model does produce such endings, and a Qwen Note copy with no period is
      the model's own text, not a storage artifact.
      
      3. **The spec's wording supports the finding.** m:261 defines Qwen echo as a "verbatim,
      whitespace-normalized reproduction of a complete 'Note:' line". maps/olmo.md §7 item 7 raised
      "whether complete requires the trailing period" only as an open OLMo question. maps/audit.md
      D1 (Qwen) asked only containment versus equality.
      
      4. **The behaviour reproduces.** I ran c5q2.py and c5q.py from the repo root on the real
      `run_session_qwen` with the scripted adapter:
      
         | Case | Current code | Complete-line form (pre-fix) |
         |---|---|---|
         | Free round, period-less TOLD (sham s12 and contrast s1) | echo; not excluded; valid; 6
      choices | `('lexical','free')` exclusion; invalid; 1 choice |
         | Forced round, period-less COMMON | `('echo','forced')`; excluded; out of the domain | no
      claim; valid; in the domain |
         | Forced round, period-less TOLD | `degen_rounds` 1 | `degen_rounds` 0 (lexical@forced) |
      
         So on Qwen the PIN changes f1's valid-session rule of record in the direction the finding
      says.
      
      **Why the severity stays minor.**
      - The PIN is declared at LOCK v0.1, not silent.
      - m:261's claiming order already turns a complete TOLD copy on a free round into a non-
      excluding echo (maps/audit.md §5 item 6). The PIN widens that existing effect to period-less
      copies; it does not create it.
      - Such copies need the model to reproduce a Note line almost verbatim, so they should be rare.
      
      The defect that remains is real but small. The Qwen half has no stated mechanism or authority.
      It departs from m:261's "verbatim ... complete" wording, yet the PIN presents it as consistent
      with that line. The PIN also does not disclose the effect on Qwen session validity. A further
      wording leftover is stale: bars.py:493-496 `_JUST_PAUSE` still describes echo as "a verbatim
      reproduction of a harness line".
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 2): the period-less echo is OLMo-only; Qwen keeps the complete-line rule.

--- N7 [bars] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:908  TITLE: A gate stage's end is persisted before its report, reads and G-ID artifact, and the ended stage can never be re-finished; an interrupted G-ID strands Q-F0 with no lawful runner path
    CLAIM:
      _finish_gate (:762) and _finish_gid (:908) write stage_end to the checkpoint before they write
      the pick-free report, the reads file and (G-ID) the licence artifact. Since #17, _begin ->
      resume.refuse_ended refuses any re-invocation of a checkpoint that carries stage_end, with or
      without --resume. So a failure between the checkpoint write and the output writes (ENOSPC,
      kill, power loss) leaves a stage that is ENDED and has no outputs, and the runner cannot
      regenerate them. Before the fixes this was recoverable: a finished stage could be resumed,
      every session was skipped, and the pure _finish_* re-ran. For G-ID the next stage is blocked
      as well. A G-ID ended PASS with no artifact is refused 'STAGE ENDED (PASS)', and G-F0m-Qwen is
      refused 'G-ID ARTIFACT ABSENT'. The only ways out are setting aside an ended checkpoint, which
      the code's own PIN forbids, or hand-writing the licence artifact that the cached-path guard
      trusts.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-ID-F0m reaches its verdict. write_checkpoint persists stage_end PASS, then the artifact
      write fails (simulated ENOSPC). Re-running G-ID, with or without resume, gives 'STAGE ENDED
      ... (PASS)'. Qualification with locked_path cached or uncached gives 'G-ID ARTIFACT ABSENT'.
      Q-F0 cannot proceed. The same applies to G-TIME-F0: if the report write fails after stage_end
      PASS, no report, reads or STAGE line ever exists, re-runs are refused 'STAGE ENDED', yet
      prior_gate('Q-F0','id', that checkpoint) returns PASS. G-ID is then licensed without the reads
      file the seat's §3 read uses.
    EVIDENCE:
      confirm/bars/gid_ended_before_artifact.py prints:
      - 'G-ID run1: OSError ... | stage_end {... status: PASS}'
      - 'artifact exists: False'
      - both reruns: 'STAGE ENDED at ...'
      - 'qual locked cached -> G-ID ARTIFACT ABSENT', and the same for uncached.
      
      confirm/bars/ended_before_report.py prints:
      - stage_end PASS, report and reads absent, no STAGE line on stdout
      - reruns '{}' and '{resume: True}' both refused 'STAGE ENDED'
      - prior_gate(G-ID) returns status PASS.
      
      Code: stages.py:762-763 and :908-909 write the checkpoint before :775-776 and :933-947.
      resume.py:522-539 and :563 refuse any stage_end. Pre-fix (scratchpad
      f1_stages_orig.py:559-613) wrote the checkpoint only on a hit and had no refuse_ended.
    FIX:
      Write the pick-free outputs first (report, reads and, for G-ID, the artifact; all are pure
      functions of ckpt['sessions'] and the decision), then persist stage_end. A crash before
      stage_end then re-derives them on resume. For G-ID, the 'G-ID ARTIFACT EXISTS' refusal (:838)
      must also accept, on a resume, an existing artifact byte-equal to the recomputed one.
      Alternatively, let refuse_ended allow a finish-only pass that launches nothing and re-derives
      missing pick-free outputs from an ended checkpoint.
    REFUTER:
      I could not refute this finding: the code does what it describes and both failure scenarios
      reproduce.
      
      **Ordering, from the code.** `_finish_gate` persists `stage_end` before any output file:
      - It calls `_record_stage_end` and then `_resume.write_checkpoint` (stages.py:762-763).
      - Only after that does it build and write the report and reads (`_write_json` at :775-776).
      
      `_finish_gid` has the same order:
      - `_record_stage_end` and `write_checkpoint` come first (:908-909).
      - Report and reads follow (:933-934), then the G-ID artifact (:947).
      
      **Refusal of an ended stage.** `_begin` calls `resume.refuse_ended(cpath)` (stages.py:516).
      `load_or_init` calls it again (resume.py:563). `refuse_ended` raises `STAGE ENDED (status)`
      for any checkpoint with a `stage_end` status (resume.py:522-539), with or without `resume`. No
      finish-only or regenerate path exists in stages.py or in `scripts/m_sessions.py` (its CLI has
      only `--selftest`, `--build-schedules` and the stage subcommands).
      
      **Before the fixes this was recoverable.** In the pre-fix `f1_stages_orig.py`, the PASS path
      never wrote `stage_end`; the checkpoint was written only on a hit (:426). The pre-fix resume
      had no `refuse_ended`.
      
      **Reproduction.** I copied both evidence scripts to `confirm/bars/refute908/` and ran them
      with the venv Python. They import the real package read-only; `GATES_DIR` and `out_root` are
      redirected into the work directory. `git status` was unchanged.
      
      - **gid_ended_before_artifact.py:**
        - G-TIME ended PASS.
        - The G-ID run hit the simulated ENOSPC (OSError 28) with `stage_end` already saved as PASS,
      and the artifact does not exist.
        - Both re-runs, with `{}` and with `{resume: True}`, were refused `STAGE ENDED ... finished
      gate stage is never resumed`.
        - Qualification with `locked_path` cached and with uncached both gave `G-ID ARTIFACT
      ABSENT`, so Q-F0 is stranded.
      - **ended_before_report.py:**
        - G-TIME's checkpoint holds `stage_end` PASS, but the report and reads are absent and stdout
      has no STAGE line.
        - Both re-runs were refused `STAGE ENDED`, and the report is still absent afterwards.
        - `prior_gate('Q-F0', 'id', ckpt)` still returns status PASS, so G-ID is licensed without
      the reads file.
      
      **Severity.** Minor. The failure needs a crash or I/O error in the narrow window between two
      atomic writes. When it happens, though, nothing in the runner can recover the stage: the
      operator must set aside an ended checkpoint or hand-write the licence artifact. No verdict is
      corrupted, but the next stage can be blocked (G-ID) or licensed without its reads file
      (G-TIME).
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 2 write order; round 3 end_pending, re-finish-only).

--- N9 [blinding] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/resume.py:510  TITLE: Re-invoking a STOPped stage prints the hair-trigger class the STOP announcement withheld
    CLAIM:
      _ended_message is new with the #17/#22 fix; the pre-fix _refuse_stopped printed no bar. It
      builds the STOP refusal from ck['stop']['bar'], the unblinded class, instead of
      ck['stop']['blinded']['bar']. refuse_ended runs at every stage start (_begin, stages.py:516)
      and inside load_or_init. So any later invocation of a stage or campaign stopped by a contrast
      session's free-phase hair trigger prints the class, although the stop announcement said 'its
      class is withheld (m:318 exception (1))'. This contradicts m:263/m:271's form and stages' own
      PIN at :586-591. Today it carries no information: scaffold_absorption is the only hair trigger
      with a free phase, since G-VER stops have phase None. It would leak once another free-phase
      hair class exists.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-TIME-F0 stops on '<|im_end|>' in A0's free round 4. Stdout correctly says the class is
      withheld. The operator re-invokes the stage, with or without resume, and gets SystemExit
      'STAGE ENDED at .../ckpt/Q-F0_time.json (STOP, scaffold_absorption): ...'. A Q-F0 campaign
      stopped on A0 s=3's free round shows the same thing on its next invocation: '... (STOP,
      scaffold_absorption)'.
    EVIDENCE:
      confirm/blinding runs on the current package. In the gate-stage run (fstopmsg), the
      announcement reads 'STOP G-TIME-F0: a hair trigger fired in the free phase of a contrast
      session (A0 s=0); its class is withheld ...'. The resume refusal and the plain re-run refusal
      both end '(STOP, scaffold_absorption)'. The checkpoint's stop.bar is 'scaffold_absorption',
      while its blinded.bar is 'withheld to the unit's VERDICTS entry...'. The campaign run (fcstop)
      has a report stop.bar that is withheld and no 'scaffold' on stdout, but its next invocation
      prints '(STOP, scaffold_absorption)'. resume.py:509-511: `bar = (ck.get("stop") or
      {}).get("bar")`. The pre-fix copy scratchpad/step4/f1_stages_orig.py:243 printed only 'STAGE
      STOPPED: ... records a STOP'. The m_sessions check asserts only '(STOP' in the refusal.
    FIX:
      In _ended_message, print ck['stop']['blinded']['bar'] or no bar. Extend the m_sessions
      time_hair refusal check to assert that 'scaffold' is absent from the refusal text.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds. I reproduced it on the current package in scratchpad/step4/confirm/refute-
      ended-blind/t.py, which imports the real package read-only.
      
      **Gate stage.** A G-TIME-F0 stage with a '<|im_end|>' in A0's free round (n=9):
      - The first run's stdout reads "STOP G-TIME-F0: a hair trigger fired in the free phase of a
      contrast session (A0 s=0); its class is withheld (m:318 exception (1))".
      - The checkpoint's stop.bar is 'scaffold_absorption', and stop.blinded.bar is the
      WITHHELD_CLASS text.
      - The report's stop block is blinded.
      - Re-invoking run_gate_stage, with resume=False and with resume=True, raises SystemExit "STAGE
      ENDED at .../Q-F0_time.json (STOP, scaffold_absorption): ...". 'scaffold' appears in the
      refusal text both times.
      
      **Campaign.** resume.refuse_ended on the blinding lens's stopped campaign checkpoint
      (out/fcstop, stop.bar scaffold_absorption, phase free, A0) gives the same refusal, ending
      "(STOP, scaffold_absorption)".
      
      **Cause.** resume.py:509-511 builds the message from (ck.get('stop') or {}).get('bar'), the
      unblinded field that _record_stop writes from hit['bar']. It does not use
      stop['blinded']['bar']. refuse_ended runs in _begin (stages.py:516) and in load_or_init
      (resume.py:563/570). That contradicts the code's own PIN at stages.py:586-591, which says the
      class goes to the checkpoint's stop block only.
      
      **Pre-fix behaviour.** The pre-fix _refuse_stopped (f1_stages_orig.py:239-244) printed no bar.
      fix_F1.md confirms that _ended_message / refuse_ended came in with the #17/#22 fixes.
      
      **Test gap.** The m_sessions check (scripts/m_sessions.py:328-330) asserts only 'STAGE ENDED'
      and '(STOP', so it does not catch this.
      
      **Severity.** The finding says itself that the leak carries no information today. Session
      hair_triggers come only from scaffold hits, so scaffold_absorption is the only free-phase
      class. A G-VER stop has phase None. The class can already be deduced from "a hair trigger
      fired in the free phase", and m:318 exception (1) lets the adjudication read the class anyway.
      It stays a real, minor departure from the declared blinded form, and it would become a real
      leak if a second free-phase hair class is ever added.

    valence_core/m/resume.py:510  TITLE: Refusing an ENDED stage prints a contrast free-phase hair trigger's withheld class

      I did not refute the finding: it is factually correct. In valence_core/m/resume.py:507-512,
      `_ended_message` reads `ck['stop']['bar']`. `stages._record_stop` (stages.py:609-613) stores
      the unblinded `hit['bar']` there. The blinded form, `WITHHELD_CLASS` from

--- N13 [guard-resume] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/resume.py:220  TITLE: package_sha256 binds every valence_core/m file, so any edit (OLMo-track code, a comment) blocks resuming a paused or interrupted campaign and blocks m:267's gate-stage restart
    CLAIM:
      The #21 fix put package_sha256 into the resume identity. It is a hash over ALL
      valence_core/m/*.py (resume.py:195-227; stages.py:505). The identity diff is exact, so any
      byte change anywhere in the package between invocations refuses a resume. That includes OLMo-
      only modules (runner_olmo.py), the stages.py code shared by both substrates, and comments.
      Spec step 16 runs the OLMo track 'in parallel' with the Q-F0 campaign in this same package.
      The step-9 box PINs (RUNPOD_POD_ID source, fsync on /workspace) invite code edits in that
      window. A Q-F0 campaign paused at a look waiting for the seat, or interrupted, then cannot be
      resumed unless the package is reverted byte-for-byte. A restart at a new path starts
      hour_cap_ms_used at 0, so the time spent before it no longer counts against the cap (m:251).
      On the box, an unfinished OLMo gate stage resumed on a new pod after a pull is refused as 'a
      design change, not a pod change' instead of being restarted as m:267 directs. The spec's
      identity list (m:267) and LOCK v0.1's 'module homes' (m:279) do not require binding the whole
      package. The refusal is fail-closed and loud, and no verdict figure moves.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      Q-F0 campaign (step 16) pauses at the look at launch 80. While the seat adjudicates, Code
      commits a fix to runner_olmo.py for the box. Resuming with the adjudication fails with
      'CHECKPOINT MISMATCH ... differs on [package_sha256]'. The campaign continues only if the
      operator checks out the old package bytes. Otherwise it restarts from launch 0 and the cap
      accounting resets.
    EVIDENCE:
      confirm/guard-resume/c_pkg_resume.py (c_pkg_resume.out): a stub Q-F0 campaign interrupted at 2
      launches (44 ms used). A resume with resume.package_sha256 standing in for an edited tree is
      refused 'CHECKPOINT MISMATCH ... differs on ['package_sha256']'. The same resume with the
      package unchanged completes (COMPLETE). c_identity_code.py: on a copy of the package, a one-
      line comment appended to runner_olmo.py moves package_sha256_of. c_pkg_pod.py: an OLMo qual
      stage interrupted on podA, resumed on podB with a changed package hash, is refused '... as
      well as the pod -- a design change, not a pod change; refusing.' (resume.py:576-580).
    FIX:
      Bind what the unit actually executes, not the whole directory. For example, hash only the
      modules on the unit's substrate path, or check at startup against the module hashes the
      governing lock pins. Alternatively, keep package_sha256 as a recorded, reported field and
      leave it out of the exact-equality set, so the change is logged rather than refused. Either
      way, declare the choice as a PIN for the seat.
    REFUTER:
      I checked the finding by reading the code and running it, and every factual claim holds. The
      real file is resume.py, but the hash function is at :195-216 and the cached per-process value
      at :220-227, not :195-227 as the finding says.
      
      **What the code does**
      - `package_sha256_of(PACKAGE_DIR)` hashes every `*.py` file under `valence_core/m`.
      `PACKAGE_DIR` is set at resume.py:78. The value is cached once per process.
      - `stages._identity` (stages.py:505) puts that hash into every identity.
      - `_identity_diff` compares the recorded and current identities exactly, in both directions.
      In `load_or_init`, any difference outside the pod-change branch raises "CHECKPOINT MISMATCH".
      - A paused campaign resumes in a new process. `run_campaign` calls `load_or_init`, which
      checks the identity, before it reads the adjudication at stages.py:1121-1131. So a resume
      carrying the seat's adjudication is refused after any edit to the package between the two
      invocations.
      - In the gate-stage pod-change branch (resume.py:572-580), only `backend_identifier` may
      differ. A changed `package_sha256` there gives "... as well as the pod -- a design change, not
      a pod change; refusing."
      
      **What I ran**
      My own test, `confirm/guard-resume/refuter/r_pkg_edit.py`, makes a real file edit instead of
      the reviewer's monkeypatch. It points `resume.PACKAGE_DIR` at a copy of the package and
      interrupts a stub Q-F0 campaign at 2 launches (41 ms used). It then appends a single comment
      line to `runner_olmo.py` in the copy and clears the cache.
      - The hash moved.
      - The resume was refused with "CHECKPOINT MISMATCH ... differs on ['package_sha256']".
      - After reverting the edit, the hash matched the original and the resume finished COMPLETE.
      
      I also checked c_pkg_pod.out, which matches the refusal text at resume.py:580.
      
      **Spec check**
      - m:358, step 16, does run the OLMo track "in parallel" with the Q-F0 campaign.
      - The identity list at m:267 contains no code hash.
      - m:279, LOCK v0.1, names only "module homes".
      
      The whole-package binding is a deliberate, declared choice: the resume.py docstring at :43-46
      says it is there "so a code or render change between invocations refuses a resume". That
      choice came from the fix for #21. Even so, the spec does not require it, and the operational
      friction the finding describes is real.
      
      **Why minor**
      - The refusal fails closed and says so loudly.
      - No verdict figure moves.
      - Workarounds exist. The operator can check the old package bytes back out. For an OLMo gate
      stage, they can also move the checkpoint aside and start fresh, which is the restart m:267
      calls for.
      - The cap-accounting reset happens only if the operator chooses to restart a Qwen campaign at
      a new path, which the spec never directs.
    DISPOSITION: DISCLOSED (by design): package_sha256 refuses a resume across any edit to the m package.

    valence_core/m/stages.py:762  TITLE: stage_end is persisted before the pick-free report and reads are written; a failure between the writes leaves a stage ENDED with no report and no way to regenerate it through the package

      built, at :766-775. The `_assert_pick_free` check runs at :775, and the report and reads files

      :908-909, the report and reads at :932-933, and the G-ID artifact at :947-948. `_begin` (:516)
      calls `resume.refuse_ended`. Once `stage_end` has a status, `ended_status` (resume.py:490-504)
      returns it, so every later invocation is refused with "STAGE ENDED", with or without --resume.
      Nothing in stages or m_sessions.py writes a report or reads file from a checkpoint outside
      `_finish_gate`, `_finish_gid` and `run_campaign`, and those are reached only by running the
      stage. I re-ran confirm/guard-resume/c_end_window.py against the current package and got the

      {'status': 'PASS'}`, and neither Q-F0_time_report.json nor the reads file exists. The resume

      :948 never happens after a PASS, `prior_gate` (:352-353) refuses the Qwen qualification with
      "G-ID ARTIFACT ABSENT". The window can be hit in real use: a process kill, ENOSPC, or a
      deterministic `ValueError` from `_assert_pick_free` all land between the checkpoint write and
      the report write. Severity stays minor. The window is narrow, and the checkpoint keeps every
      session record, so nothing is lost and the outputs could be rebuilt by hand. The cost is that

--- N15 [guard-resume] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/guard.py:357  TITLE: The guard's docstring says any other binding of the licensed names is flagged, but an attribute assignment that monkeypatches guard.cached_path_admitted passes check_call_sites
    CLAIM:
      The #20 fix resolves licensed sources by binding, and the docstring (guard.py:29-32) claims
      'any other binding of either name outside this file is itself flagged'. _bindings collects
      Assign targets through _target_names, which ignores ast.Attribute (guard.py:357-361). So
      `guard.cached_path_admitted = lambda *a, **k: True` in a scanned file is not seen. A later
      `guard.cached_path_admitted(...)` still counts as licensed because `guard` is bound only by
      import. runner_qwen's runtime re-check calls the same patched module attribute, so the defence
      in depth is defeated as well. The setattr form cannot be caught by any static check; the
      literal assignment can be, and is not.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      A scanned file containing `from . import guard` / `guard.cached_path_admitted = lambda *a,
      **k: True` / `a.generate(..., cached=guard.cached_path_admitted('Q-F0','qual',
      backend_identifier='x'))` returns [] from check_call_sites. At run time the call yields True
      with no G-ID PASS on disk.
    EVIDENCE:
      confirm/guard-resume/c_callsites2.py (c_callsites2.out): 'MISSED  attribute assignment
      monkeypatches the guard's function []'. In the same run, the nested-def, method and parameter-
      shadowing variants are FLAGGED.
    FIX:
      In _bindings (or a visit_Assign check), flag any Assign, AugAssign, AnnAssign or Delete whose
      target is an Attribute named cached_path_admitted or gid_paths outside guard.py.
      Alternatively, soften the docstring to state that attribute and dynamic rebinding are out of
      the check's reach.
    REFUTER:
      The finding is correct. I reproduced it with the real package, imported read-only. My script
      and its output are in confirm/refute-guard-attr/t.py and t.out.
      
      Cause: `_target_names` (guard.py:260-267) returns [] for an `ast.Attribute` target, so
      `_bindings` (guard.py:318-380) never records `guard.cached_path_admitted = ...` as a binding.
      As a result `_flag_licensed_bindings` (guard.py:426-436) never sees it. `_is_guard_alias`
      still treats `guard` as bound only by the import, so `_licensed` accepts the later
      `guard.cached_path_admitted(...)` call. The only rule that looks at Attribute targets is
      `_true_into`, and it applies only to a literal True assigned to `cached` or `path_cached`.
      
      `check_call_sites` returns [] for every attribute-rebinding form I tried:
      - `from . import guard` followed by the attribute assignment and a `generate(...,
      cached=guard.cached_path_admitted(...))` call
      - the same with `from valence_core.m import guard`
      - the dotted form after `import valence_core.m.guard`
      - `guard.gid_paths = lambda: (True, True)`
      - an annotated attribute assignment
      
      Plain-name controls are flagged as the docstring promises: `cached_path_admitted = lambda ...`
      and `def cached_path_admitted`.
      
      The runtime re-check is defeated too. `runner_qwen.guard is guard` is True. With no G-ID
      artifact for the backend, `cached_path_admitted("Q-F0","qual",backend_identifier=...)` returns
      False before the patch. After the patch it returns True when called through

--- R1 [round2-stages] finder:substantive strict refuter: refuted=False severity=substantive
    valence_core/m/stages.py:1048  TITLE: A STOP's outputs are written before the fired session and the stop are on disk; re-invoking re-launches the session, can lift the STOP, and leaves a stale excerpt
    CLAIM:
      The round-2 PIN (stages.py:1092-1096, module docstring :58-63, resume.py:39-40) says an end on
      disk always has its outputs, and that a crash before it leaves a stage that re-finishes from
      the sessions on disk, byte-identically. That is false for a hair-trigger or G-VER STOP. The
      fired session's record (or the quarantined G-VER record) and the stop block live only in
      memory: :1048-1049 writes the checkpoint only `if hit is None`, the same at :1226-1227 for
      G-ID and :1565-1570 for the campaign. Round 2 now writes the report (status STOP), the reads
      and the adjudication excerpt (which lists the revealed contrast pick) first, at :1125-1128 and
      :1658-1660, and persists the end last. A kill in that window leaves those outputs on disk
      beside a checkpoint that has no stop and no fired session. The re-invocation is then not a re-
      finish: it launches the fired session again. If the fired round does not recur, the stage runs
      on, and an announced-to-disk STOP is lifted. The stale excerpt is never removed, because
      `apath` is only written when events exist (:1102, :1651-1652). It stays beside the PASS or
      COMPLETE checkpoint, names that checkpoint in its `checkpoint` field, is not listed in the
      report, and still lists the contrast pick as seen.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-TIME-F0: A0 emits '<|im_end|>' at round 6, a contrast free-phase trigger. The process dies
      after writing the report, reads and excerpt and before the checkpoint write. On disk: the
      report says STOP, the excerpt lists pick 'ridge', and the checkpoint has sessions [] and no
      stop. (a) A resume with the same deterministic script makes 6 generate calls to re-run A0.
      Provenance on disk becomes [0, 1], and the report is no longer byte-identical to the pre-kill
      one. (b) A resume where the round does not recur (any residual nondeterminism) ends PASS,
      persists stage_end PASS, overwrites the report to PASS, and leaves the STOP excerpt in place.
      The campaign shows the same thing: a contrast hair trigger at A0 s=3 round 7 is killed before
      its stop write. The report says STOP and the checkpoint has no stop. The resume makes 380
      generate calls and ends COMPLETE, with the stale hair_trigger event and its pick still in
      Q-F0_campaign_adjudication.json.
    EVIDENCE:
      check2/round2-stages/e1_hair_refinish.py (e1.out), e1b_hair_refinish_sleep.py (e1b.out:
      'generate calls on resume: 6 | report identical to the pre-kill one: False'), e7_campaign.py
      (e7.out (b): 'report STOP | stop on disk False ... resume: COMPLETE ... generate calls 380 ...
      stale STOP event still in the excerpt True'). Each kill was simulated by wrapping
      resume.write_checkpoint to raise on the first write carrying an end, the selftest's own
      cut_at_end form. The selftest's re-finish check (stages.py:2501-2531, m_sessions cut_at_end
      uses) only covers PASS, COMPLETE and PI-STOP ends, where every session is already on disk. All
      under /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jasondwyer-Documents-code-jspace-
      lab/adb70afc-5886-490b-a675-abb30f8c425f/scratchpad/step4/check2/round2-stages/.
    FIX:
      Before writing any output, persist the fired record (record_session, or the quarantined record
      in the stop block) together with the stop as an 'end pending' state. ended_status/refuse_ended
      should treat that state as re-finish-only: no launch, no pod set-aside, outputs re-derived
      from disk. Then persist stage_end, or the stop's final form, last. When a finish has no
      events, remove any excerpt at adjudication_path, or key excerpts to the invocation that wrote
      them. Add a selftest for a hair-trigger STOP killed in the window.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds. I confirmed it by reading the code and by running independent reproductions
      under check2/refute-stopwindow/ (r1.py and r7b.py, with copies of h.py and bh.py).
      
      **What the code does.** In run_gate_stage (stages.py:1048-1049), run_gid_pair (:1226-1227) and
      the campaign loop (:1565-1570), the fired session is recorded only in memory when `hit` is not
      None. No checkpoint write follows. A G-VER hit is never recorded in `sessions`; its record
      goes only to the in-memory stop block (_verify_hit :777-788).
      
      **Write order.** Round 2 moved the checkpoint write to the end of _finish_gate (:1125-1129)
      and the campaign finish (:1658-1662). The stages.diff hunks show the old `_record_stop` plus
      `write_checkpoint`, which ran first, being removed. A traced clean run of a contrast free-
      phase hair trigger at G-TIME-F0 shows this order:
      1. ckpt (no stop, 0 sessions)
      2. report
      3. reads
      4. adjudication excerpt
      5. ckpt (stop, 1 session, stage_end STOP)
      
      **Killed in the window.** I killed the run on the first checkpoint write carrying `stop`.
      Results:
      - The STOP never reached stdout. The only lines were PROVENANCE and 'time A0 s=0 valid'.
      - On disk, the report says status STOP with a blinded stop block.
      - The checkpoint has stop None, stage_end None and sessions [].
      - resume.ended_status on that checkpoint returns None.
      - The excerpt (picks_revealed ['ridge']) names ckpt/Q-F0_time.json.
      
      **Resume where the fired round does not recur.** The run made 20 generate calls and ended
      PASS. It persisted stage_end PASS and overwrote the report to PASS; the report's files list no
      adjudication. The stale excerpt with pick 'ridge' was still on disk. It is never removed,
      because `apath` is set only when `events` is non-empty (:1102, :1651).
      
      **Resume where the fired round recurs (deterministic).** The run made 6 generate calls and re-
      ran the fired session, not a re-finish. It reached STOP again, but the report bytes differed:
      the only differing key was `provenance`, a new stamp. Provenance launched was [0, 1].
      
      **Campaign.** Rerunning e7 part (b) in my own directory reproduced it: report STOP, no stop on
      disk, 6 sessions on disk. The resume made 380 generate calls and ended COMPLETE, and the stale
      hair_trigger event was still in the excerpt.
      
      **False claims and test gap.** The PIN at :1092-1096 and the module docstring at :58-63 say an
      end on disk always has its outputs and a crash re-finishes byte-identically from the sessions
      on disk. That is false for hair-trigger and G-VER STOPs: the fired record and the stop are not
      on disk, so re-invocation relaunches rather than re-finishes. resume.py:39-40 claims the same.
      The selftest's cut-at-end checks (stages.py:2501-2531; m_sessions.py:600/623/884/943) cover
      only PASS, G-ID verdict, COMPLETE and PI-stop ends, where every session is already on disk.
      
      **Why substantive.** The harmful branch, a STOP lifted, needs a kill in a narrow write window
      plus non-recurrence. Non-recurrence comes from residual nondeterminism, or from an OLMo pod
      change, where the unstopped checkpoint is set aside and the stage restarted. Even so, it is a
      fail-open path that this round introduced against the build's own declared PIN and 'a resume
      never lifts a STOP' (:740-745). The PIN is wrong and the stale excerpt is a real artifact
      defect, so this is more than wording or a pure test gap.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 3): every finish first persists every session (the fired record, or the G-VER-quarantined one) with an end_pending block, then writes report, reads, excerpt and artifact, then persists the end; a checkpoint carrying end_pending is re-finish-only on any pod and at any invocation. The round-3 verifier ran 104 kill-point combinations (two kill points x four re-invocations x 13 stage/end kinds): 0 generate calls and byte-identical outputs in every one.

--- R2 [round2-stages] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/resume.py:675  TITLE: A pod change after an interrupted finish sets aside a sessions-complete gate stage and restarts it, discarding an evaluated STOP or PAUSE
    CLAIM:
      Round 2 moved the end write to the end of the finish (stages.py:1129), after the report, reads
      and excerpt are written. If the process dies in that window, every session is on disk but no
      end is. A re-invocation on another pod takes load_or_init's pod-change branch
      (resume.py:673-689): the checkpoint is set aside and the stage restarts from its first
      session. The 'no pod change lifts it' guarantee (stages.py:57; resume.py:41-42) therefore does
      not hold for an end that was evaluated and partly written. In round 1 the end was persisted
      first, so the new pod would have refused it 'STAGE ENDED'. The killed run's excerpt also stays
      beside the new checkpoint (same stem).
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-TIME-F0 on pod A: 3 v2 claims in the sham session fire round_degeneracy (STOP). The report
      (STOP) and the excerpt are written, then the box dies before the checkpoint write. On pod B, a
      resume prints 'POD CHANGE ... the stage restarts from its first session', makes 20 new
      generate calls and ends PASS (the claims do not recur). The final report is PASS, and the STOP
      excerpt is still at ckpt/Q-F0_time_adjudication.json, unreferenced. On OLMo, where pods are
      ephemeral by assumption, a G-TIME-OLMo PAUSE lost this way means the qualification runs
      without the seat's adjudication.
    EVIDENCE:
      check2/round2-stages/e5_podchange.py (e5.out): 'killed: _Interrupt | sessions on disk 2 | end
      on disk None | report on disk STOP | excerpt on disk True' then 'new pod: PASS ... generate
      calls 20 | set aside [...podchange-...] | final end {... PASS} | stale STOP excerpt beside the
      PASS checkpoint: True | report lists it: False'.
    FIX:
      In load_or_init's pod-change branch, treat a gate-stage checkpoint whose every planned session
      is on disk (or which carries an end-pending marker, as in the previous finding) as re-finish-
      only on the new pod, re-deriving the end from the sessions, rather than setting it aside. Or
      persist an end-pending marker before writing any output and refuse to set aside a checkpoint
      that carries one.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds. I reproduced it and confirmed it in code. Minor is the right severity.
      
      **Code path**
      - `_finish_gate` (stages.py:1097-1129) computes the end in memory first. It then writes the
      report, the reads and the excerpt (1122-1127), and persists the end last with
      `write_checkpoint` at 1129.
      - The STOP or PAUSE line and the STAGE line are printed only after that persist (1130-1132). A
      process killed in the window therefore announces nothing on stdout.
      - On re-invocation, `load_or_init` (resume.py:666-689) runs as follows:
        - `refuse_ended` passes, because no `stage_end` was persisted.
        - For a gate stage with `pod_id_seen != now`, it sets the checkpoint aside and returns a
      fresh one. It does not check whether every session is already on disk.
      - On the same pod, the stage re-finishes instead.
      
      **Runs** (my copies are in `check2/refute-podchange/`)
      - `e5_podchange.py` reproduces exactly:
        - Killed on podA: 2 sessions on disk, no end, report = STOP, excerpt present.
        - On podB: POD CHANGE, 20 generate calls, PASS, the report overwritten to PASS, the stale
      STOP excerpt still beside the checkpoint and not listed in the report.
      - `olmo_pause.py`, the OLMo-sft G-TIME round-3 echo:
        - Killed on podA: report = PAUSE, excerpt present, and no PAUSE or STAGE line printed.
        - Same-pod resume: re-finishes to PAUSE with 0 generate calls.
        - New pod where the echo recurs: PAUSE again (13 calls).
        - New pod where it does not recur: PASS with 20 calls. The PAUSE checkpoint is set aside and
      the stale excerpt remains.
        - The OLMo qualification then runs to PASS with `prior_ckpt` set to the new PASS checkpoint
      and no `prior_adjudication`. So a PAUSE that was evaluated and partly written is lost, and the
      qualification skips the seat.
      
      **What this contradicts**
      - The code's own docs:
        - resume.py:39-40: "a crash before it leaves a stage that re-finishes"
        - stages.py:61-63: "a re-invocation that finds every session on disk and no end re-finishes
      idempotently"
      - Both are true only on the same pod.
      
      **Why minor, not higher**
      - m:267's text ("interrupted by a pod change, it restarts from its first session") arguably
      covers this case. The end was never persisted or announced.
      - Round 1 already had a small version of this window: the time between the last session's
      write and the end persist.
      - The window is a few fsynced writes, so a box dying inside it is rare.
      - With deterministic keys, a restart on a deterministic backend reproduces the same end.
      - The set-aside checkpoint is kept and named in `state.set_aside`, so the evidence survives,
      even though no step acts on it.
      - The Q-F0 version of the scenario is synthetic, since Qwen runs on the laptop with no pod id.
      The OLMo version is realistic.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED: as R1 (a pending end is never set aside on a pod change).

    valence_core/m/stages.py:536  TITLE: prior_gate's new proposal (and stub) rules bind the prior checkpoint only; the G-ID artifact that licenses the qualification and sets its path is accepted under any proposal
    CLAIM:
      The round-2 rule (docstring :26-27, prior_gate :509-515) refuses a prior under another
      proposal or written on the stub. The qualification has two licences: the G-TIME checkpoint and
      the unit's G-ID artifact. For the artifact, prior_gate checks only is_file() (:536-538), and
      guard._gid_pass (guard.py:189-195) compares only unit, criterion, verdict, pairs and backend.
      The artifact's identity.proposal_sha256 (and its renders/package) is never compared with this
      run's. So a qualification, and then a campaign, can run on the cached path licensed by a G-ID
      that ran under another proposal. The proposal rule refuses that same G-TIME-under-another-
      proposal as a checkpoint.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      Under proposal B, G-TIME and then G-ID-F0m end PASS, writing the artifact at gates/Q-F0_G-
      ID.json (identity proposal 7777...). Under proposal A, G-TIME runs, then G-F0m-Qwen with
      locked_path 'cached' and prior = A's G-TIME. It ends PASS on the cached path (240 of 240
      generate calls cached), identity proposal 2222.... The same qualification given B's G-TIME
      checkpoint is refused 'PRIOR STAGE UNDER ANOTHER PROPOSAL'. The only defence is the PIN's
      operator duty to move an old artifact aside after a re-specification.
    EVIDENCE:
      check2/round2-stages/e3_proposal.py (e3.out): 'proposal A qualification (prior G-TIME under A,
      G-ID artifact under B): PASS None | identity path cached | cached generate calls 240 of 240 |
      identity proposal 22222222'; 'same, prior G-TIME under B: PRIOR STAGE UNDER ANOTHER PROPOSAL
      ...'.
    FIX:
      In prior_gate for qual, read the artifact and refuse when identity.proposal_sha256 differs
      from this run's (and, symmetrically with N17, check the artifact's provenance or backend for
      the stub). Apply the same check where the campaign takes its path, e.g. pass proposal_sha256
      into the licence read, or have run_campaign re-check the artifact's proposal.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds, at minor severity. I re-ran the evidence script from my own copy
      (check2/refute-gidprop/e3_proposal.py, with GATES_DIR patched to a temp dir) and got the same
      output. A G-ID artifact written under proposal 7777... licensed a qualification under proposal
      2222... on the cached path: PASS, identity path cached, 240 of 240 generate calls cached. The
      same qualification given B's G-TIME checkpoint as its prior was refused with "PRIOR STAGE
      UNDER ANOTHER PROPOSAL".
      
      The code confirms why. In stages.py, prior_gate compares proposal_sha256, and checks for a
      stub writer, only on the prior checkpoint (:509-515). For the artifact it checks only that the
      file exists (:536-538). guard._gid_pass (guard.py:171-195) compares only unit, criterion,
      verdict, n_pairs, pairs_identical and backend_identifier. It never compares the artifact's
      identity.proposal_sha256, package_sha256 or renders_sha16. run_gate_stage (:1023) and
      run_campaign (:1465) take the path from that check alone. The campaign also runs, because its
      prior is the qualification, which is under A.
      
      The scenario can happen in a real run. PROPOSAL_PATH is fixed to v1_11, so "another proposal"
      means v1_11 amended in place, or a package change. Either way the old data/m/gates/Q-F0_G-
      ID.json stays on disk. The only defence is the operator duty in the PIN at :1186-1187: move
      the old artifact aside after a re-specification. That same PIN shows an old artifact is not
      meant to license a re-specified run.
      
      Two things keep this at minor. First, LOCK v0.2 under the new proposal would itself have to
      declare "cached" without a G-ID run under that proposal, so a second process error is needed.
      Second, the stub half of the suggested fix is mostly moot: a stub cannot write the canonical
      artifact (_stub_guard / refuse_canonical_out), and the cached licence already requires the
      run's backend_identifier. So no stub-written artifact can license the cached path. It can
      still satisfy the any-verdict existence check, but the run then goes uncached. The proposal
      and package half is a real residual of the N17 class.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 3): the G-ID artifact's proposal is checked (this run's, or one the lock re-adopts) in prior_gate and where the campaign takes its path; a non-stub run refuses a stub-written artifact.

--- R4 [round2-stages] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:409  TITLE: Git licence: 'tracked and clean at HEAD' passes a tracked symlink or an assume-unchanged file whose hashed bytes are not HEAD's, and the 'read-only' git diff rewrites .git/index
    CLAIM:
      _tracked_clean (:405-413) proves cleanliness with `git ls-files --error-unmatch` plus `git
      diff --quiet HEAD -- rel`. licence_problems (:433-440) then hashes the working-tree bytes. git
      compares a tracked symlink's target string, not the target's bytes, and reports an assume-
      unchanged (or skip-worktree) path as clean whatever its bytes are. So the bytes whose sha256
      must equal the identity's hash can be an uncommitted file while the licence prints 'tracked,
      clean at HEAD' (:452-453). Separately, the docstring at :401 calls this 'A READ-ONLY git
      query', but `git diff` refreshes the index stat cache: .git/index is rewritten and index.lock
      is taken when stat data is stale. GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 does not prevent this on git 2.39.5.
      The k1_stage1.proposal_gate precedent has the same calls.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      In a scratch repo, locks/Lsym.md is a committed symlink to drafts/L-draft.md, which is
      untracked. The draft is edited after the commit. licence_problems returns [] although the
      hashed bytes ('draft lock B -- edited, never committed') are in no commit; `git show
      HEAD:locks/Lsym.md` is only the link text. Likewise for `git update-index --assume-unchanged
      locks/Lau.md` followed by an edit: licence_problems returns [] while HEAD's blob is 'lock
      committed' and the hashed bytes are 'lock EDITED after commit'. Control cases (an untracked
      file, a plain edit, a staged-only new file) are refused. The staged-only case is refused with
      the misleading text 'working-tree edits not at HEAD'.
    EVIDENCE:
      check2/round2-stages/e4_git.py (e4.out), run in check2/round2-stages/gitwork, a throwaway
      repo; the real repo was not touched. Index rewrite: after `touch locks/L.md`, `git diff
      --quiet HEAD -- locks/L.md` changed the .git/index mtime (1791278084 -> 1791278085), and again
      with GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 (-> 1791278086).
    FIX:
      Hash the committed blob: take `git cat-file blob HEAD:<rel>` (or `git show HEAD:<rel>`),
      refuse if HEAD has no such path or the entry is a symlink (mode 120000 from `git ls-tree HEAD
      -- <rel>`), and require sha256(blob) == sha256(working-tree bytes) == the identity's hash.
      These are pure object reads; `git diff` is then unnecessary, so the index is never written.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds. I reproduced it on my own in check2/refute-r2s-git/t.py, in a throwaway
      repo at check2/refute-r2s-git/gw, importing the real stages module read-only.
      stages.py:405-413 (_tracked_clean) checks only `git ls-files --error-unmatch` and `git diff
      --quiet HEAD -- rel`, and licence_problems at :433-440 then hashes the working-tree bytes. I
      passed lock_sha256 = sha256 of those working-tree bytes in each case below, and
      licence_problems returned [] every time: (1) a committed symlink whose untracked target was
      edited after the commit (`git status` shows only `?? d/`); (2) a file marked assume-unchanged
      and then edited; (3) a file marked skip-worktree and then edited. A clean tracked lock also
      returned [], as it should. In each of the three cases the hashed bytes are in no commit, yet
      _licence (:452-453) would print 'tracked, clean at HEAD'. The prior e4 run also confirms the
      controls: a plain edit and a staged-only new file are both refused, the staged one with the
      misleading message 'working-tree edits not at HEAD'. On the index write: after touching the
      lock, `git diff --quiet HEAD -- locks/L.md` changed the .git/index mtime, and it changed it
      again with GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 (git 2.39.5 Apple). So the docstring at :401 calling this 'A
      READ-ONLY git query' is inaccurate. That write is only a stat-cache refresh, though, not a
      content change. scripts/k1_stage1.py:72-76 (proposal_gate) makes the same calls. Severity
      stays minor. Each bypass needs a deliberate, unusual setup: committing the lock as a symlink,
      or setting assume-unchanged/skip-worktree on it. The real repo currently has neither (`git ls-
      files -v` shows only H entries and `git ls-files -s` shows no mode-120000 entries). The index
      write changes no content. No verdict-relevant result is affected in a healthy run. Still, the
      licence does not fully deliver what it documents ('the document read is the document the
      identity names' at HEAD). The proposed fix is sound: hash `git cat-file blob HEAD:rel`, refuse
      mode 120000, and require that hash to equal both the working-tree hash and the identity's
      hash.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 3): the licence hashes the committed blob (cat-file / ls-tree only; symlinks, tree entries and absent paths refused) and requires blob == working tree == the identity's hash; git diff dropped (it writes .git/index; the verifier saw the index unchanged).

--- R5 [round2-stages] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/resume.py:245  TITLE: Provenance 'launched' never counts a session whose invocation died inside it, contrary to its PIN; the selftest bakes in the undercount
    CLAIM:
      note_launch increments the in-memory counter before each launch (stages.py:1040, :1218,
      :1552). The count reaches disk only with the next checkpoint write, and a kill inside the
      session prevents that write. The PIN at resume.py:528-529 ('a G-VER stop or an interrupt
      launches without recording') and the docstring at :50-51 ('the number of sessions it
      launched') both say an interrupted launch is counted. It never is. The provenance entry, which
      the report copies (report_provenance) as the record of what each backend ran (ledger 27),
      undercounts every interrupted session's generations. An invocation whose only launch was
      interrupted shows launched 0 and is dropped from the report entirely.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      G-TIME-F0 is killed in A0s round 3. Invocation 1 made 13 generate calls over two sessions; its
      entry says launched 1. The resume launches A0s again: entries [1, 1], 2 launches recorded
      against 3 actually made. m_sessions.py:851-855 asserts [30, 14] for a campaign interrupted
      inside A0 s=15. That is 31 + 14 launches, recorded as 44.
    EVIDENCE:
      check2/round2-stages/e6_launched.py (e6.out): 'invocation 1: _Interrupt | generate calls 13 |
      sessions recorded ['A0'] | provenance launched [1]' and 'invocation 2: PASS | generate calls
      10 | provenance launched [1, 1]'.
    FIX:
      Persist the counter before generating, e.g. write the checkpoint after note_launch (one extra
      atomic write per session). Or redefine 'launched' in the PIN and docstring as 'launched and
      recorded or quarantined', and stop citing interrupts there.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds, and minor is the right grade.
      
      What the code does: `note_launch` (resume.py:245-251) adds 1 to `prov[-1]["launched"]` in
      memory only. At all three launch sites (stages.py:1040 gate, :1218 G-ID, :1552 campaign), the
      count reaches disk only through the next `write_checkpoint`, which comes after `_launch` and
      `record_session`, or through `_finish_gate` on a G-VER or hair stop. Nothing in stages.py or
      resume.py catches `KeyboardInterrupt` or `BaseException` to save the checkpoint first (grep).
      So a kill during a session always loses that launch from the count. A G-VER stop does reach
      disk through `_finish_gate`.
      
      Why it contradicts the text: the PIN at resume.py:528-529 explains the `>=` slack as "a G-VER
      stop or an interrupt launches without recording". But an interrupt can never add slack,
      because its launch is never counted. The docstring at :50-51 ("the number of sessions it
      launched") is also wrong for any interrupted invocation.
      
      What I ran: I copied the finder's h.py and e6_launched.py to check2/refute-r2stages-launched/
      and ran them with the jlens venv python. Output matched the finding exactly:
      - invocation 1: `_Interrupt`, 13 generate calls, sessions ['A0'], launched [1]
      - invocation 2: PASS, 10 calls, launched [1, 1], report [1, 1]
      
      Three sessions generated across the two invocations; two launches are recorded.
      
      The selftest bakes this in. m_sessions.py:832-855 has a cut at A0 s=15 round 3, asserts
      `n_before == 30`, then asserts `[30, 14]`. That is 31 launches in invocation 1 (the 31st
      interrupted mid-generation) plus 14 in the resume, recorded as 44.
      
      Why minor, not higher:
      - `launched` is Code's own field (#40); PROPOSAL-m v1.11 does not define a launch count for
      provenance.
      - An interrupted session leaves no record, so no verdict-relevant number moves.
      - The report_provenance PIN defines the report's entries as "the backends that produced the
      stage's records and stops". Dropping an invocation whose only launch was interrupted is
      therefore consistent with that PIN's stated purpose.
      - The checkpoint list still keeps that invocation's entry.
      
      That leaves a mismatch between the docstring/PIN and the code, and a selftest that asserts the
      code's undercount, with no verdict impact.
    DISPOSITION: WORDING (round 3): provenance 'launched' counts sessions launched and recorded (or quarantined); a session interrupted mid-generation is counted by neither invocation.

--- R6 [round2-stages] finder:minor strict refuter: refuted=False severity=minor
    valence_core/m/stages.py:1570  TITLE: A campaign look pause is persisted before its report and excerpt; killed in between, it can only be re-derived after the seat adjudicates a look it has no lawful material for
    CLAIM:
      A fired look's pause is written with its session (:1566-1570), and the schedule-exhausted
      pause likewise (:1545-1547). The pick-free report and the counts-only excerpt the seat
      adjudicates from are written only after that, at the end of the invocation (:1658-1660). If
      the process dies in between, every re-invocation without an adjudication is refused 'CAMPAIGN
      PAUSED' (:1512-1523) before anything is written. The look's results then exist only in the
      pick-bearing checkpoint, which the seat may not read before VERDICTS. Code cannot self-
      adjudicate, so the pause can only be released by adjudicating it blind or by the PI's stop.
      Disclosed as 'Not done' in fix2_G.md; recorded here because it is reachable and has no lawful
      exit.
    FAILURE_SCENARIO:
      Q-F0 campaign: the look at 40 launches fires on the sham arm. The report write fails or the
      process is killed right after the checkpoint write. Checkpoint pauses = [('look', 40)], no
      report, no excerpt. A resume says 'CAMPAIGN PAUSED ... a resume needs the seat's
      adjudication'; a plain run says 'CHECKPOINT EXISTS'. The report still does not exist.
    EVIDENCE:
      check2/round2-stages/e7_campaign.py (e7.out (a)): 'pauses on disk [('look', 40)] | report
      exists False | excerpt exists False' followed by both refusals with 'report exists now False'.
    FIX:
      When the last pause is unadjudicated and neither an adjudication nor a PI stop is given, re-
      derive the report and the excerpt from the checkpoint and write them (no launch, no checkpoint
      change), then exit with the PAUSE status. Alternatively, write the look's report and excerpt
      before the write that persists the pause.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds. All cited lines match `valence_core/m/stages.py`:
      - When a look fires, its pause is persisted with its session by `_resume.write_checkpoint` at
      :1570. The schedule-exhausted pause is persisted the same way at :1545-1547.
      - The pick-free report (`_write_json` at :1658) and the excerpt (:1660) are written only at
      the end of the invocation.
      - The refusal at :1512-1523 ("CAMPAIGN PAUSED") fires before any write.
      - There is no handler for interrupts or signals, and the CLI has no command that re-derives
      the report from the checkpoint.
      - The code's own PIN at :1584-1586 says a stop or terminal state is written to the report and
      excerpt first and persisted after (findings N7/N10/N14). The look pause does not follow that
      order.
      
      I reproduced it independently in `check2/refute-r2s-lookpause/r1.py`, using a copy of the
      finder's harness that imports the real package read-only. I let the real checkpoint write
      finish, then raised an interrupt as soon as a pause was on disk:
      - **On disk:** pauses = [look 1 at 40 launches, bar `round_degeneracy@campaign-Q-F0`,
      adjudication None], 40 sessions, no report, no excerpt. The look's bar messages had not been
      printed to stdout either.
      - **`resume=True`:** refused with "CAMPAIGN PAUSED ... a resume needs the seat's adjudication
      (m:330) or the PI's stop". Still no report.
      - **Plain run:** refused with "CHECKPOINT EXISTS ... pass --resume". Still no report.
      - **Resume with `adjudication="seat: continue"`:** it launched new sessions (40 to 44 on disk,
      40 generate calls on a fresh adapter) before writing any report. Releasing the pause therefore
      means adjudicating blind. The seat's only other material is the pick-bearing checkpoint, which
      it may not read before VERDICTS (m:318, DESIGN §stage_report).
      
      One small overstatement: "no lawful exit" is too strong. The PI's stop is a lawful exit, as
      the claim's own body says. But that exit ends the campaign rather than releasing the pause.
      
      Minor is the right severity. It needs a process death in a short window between two writes, it
      changes no verdict, and it leaks no pick. It is real and reachable, and `fix2_G.md` lists it
      as "Not done".
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 3): a paused campaign re-invoked with no ruling re-derives and writes its report and excerpt, launches nothing, changes nothing, exits PAUSE.

    valence_core/m/guard.py:463  TITLE: N15 residue: several static attribute-rebinding forms of the licensed names still pass check_call_sites, and the new PIN wrongly says the runtime licence still reads the artifact
    CLAIM:

      `setattr`/`delattr`/`__setattr__`/`__delattr__`/`patch.object` when the name sits in argument

      reads the artifact on disk".

      guard.cached_path_admitted in (...)`, `with ... as guard.cached_path_admitted` and `[0 for

      - `_CallSiteScan` calls `_flag_attr_rebind` only from four visitors: `visit_Assign` (:617),
      `visit_AnnAssign` (:622), `visit_AugAssign` (:627) and `visit_Delete` (:631).
      - No For, With or comprehension visitor checks attribute targets. `_bindings` and `_name_ok`
      look only at Name targets via `_target_names`, which returns [] for an Attribute.
      - `_flag_attr_rebind_call` (:477-492) reads only argument slot 1 for the setattr family, and
      handles `patch` only with a dotted string in slot 0.
      
      **Static probe (probe.py).** Each case was a temp file with `from valence_core.m import
      guard`, one rebinding line, and a `generate(..., cached=guard.cached_path_admitted(...))`
      call.
      - The two controls are flagged: plain assignment and `setattr(guard, 'cached_path_admitted',
      f)`.
      - `check_call_sites` returned [] for all 10 other forms:
        - a for-loop target, a with-as target and a comprehension target
        - the bound `guard.__setattr__('cached_path_admitted', f)`
        - `guard.__dict__.update(cached_path_admitted=f)` and `vars(guard).update({...})`
        - `mock.patch.multiple(guard, ...)` and `mock.patch.dict(guard.__dict__, {...})`
        - pytest's `monkeypatch.setattr('valence_core.m.guard.cached_path_admitted', f)`
        - `guard.__dict__.__setitem__('gid_paths', f)`
      
      **Runtime probe (runner_probe.py, a copy of the finder's script).** `GATES_DIR` pointed at an
      empty temp dir and I ran `run_session_qwen` on a Q-F0 'qual' cached scaffold with
      `ScriptedAdapter`.
      - Unpatched, it was refused: "CACHED PATH NOT LICENSED".
      - After `for guard.cached_path_admitted in (lambda *a, **k: True,): pass`, it ran all 10
      rounds with `cached=True` on every call.
      
      The runner's re-check at runner_qwen.py:143 calls the module attribute
      `guard.cached_path_admitted`. Any rebinding therefore defeats the runtime half too, including
      the run-time-computed `setattr` form the PIN concedes. The PIN's phrase "the runtime licence
      still reads the artifact on disk" (guard.py:469-470) is false as a backstop claim. The
      docstring (:31-35) also says attribute rebinding is flagged "in any file", which is broader
      than what the code flags.
      
      **Why only minor.** `check_call_sites` over valence_core/m plus scripts/m_sessions.py returns
      [], so the real package has no such code and there is no live unlicensed path. The defect is
      an overstated disclosure and a gap in the static proof's coverage.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED (round 3): attribute rebinds of the licensed names flagged in for/with/comprehension targets, bound and unbound __setattr__/__delattr__/__setitem__/__delitem__, patch.multiple/patch.dict, __dict__.update and vars().update, dotted setattr strings; the docstring and PIN list exactly these. Residual (disclosed; the verifier's informational list): operator.setitem(vars(g), ...), monkeypatch.setitem(g.__dict__, ...), g.__dict__ |= {...}, dict.update(g.__dict__, ...), __dict__.update([(name, f)]).

    valence_core/m/stages.py:196  TITLE: A campaign restarted at a new checkpoint path overwrites the aborted campaign's report
    CLAIM:

      reports/OLMo-sft_campaign_report.json naming ckpt/OLMo-sft_campaign.json and its adjudication
      excerpt. The pod is lost while the pause awaits the seat. On pod B the campaign restarts at
      ckpt/OLMo-sft_campaign_podB.json and ends COMPLETE. Its report replaces pod A's in the working

      beside them.

      {checkpoint: ckpt/OLMo-sft_campaign.json, adjudication: …}. After the pod-B restart at the new

      adjudication_path already does. Or refuse to overwrite a report whose files.checkpoint names a
      different checkpoint.
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds; I'm not refuting it. I read the code and reproduced the scenario.
      
      **Code**
      - In stages.py:182-197, `out_paths` builds `reports/{unit}_{stage}_report.json` from the unit
      and stage alone. Only `adjudication_path` (stages.py:227) takes its name from the checkpoint
      stem.
      - `run_campaign` writes the report on every invocation that ends, a look PAUSE included:
      `_write_json(paths["report"], report)` in stages.py around :1657. The report's
      `files.checkpoint` names the current checkpoint.
      - A paused campaign has no `terminal`, so `resume.ended_status` returns None and it does not
      count as ended.
      - On another pod, `load_or_init` refuses the old path because the pod_id differs. Its message
      tells the operator to restart "at a NEW checkpoint path" (resume.py:690-694).
      - `checkpoint_path` accepts any `<out_root>/ckpt/<name>.json`.
      - `_begin` calls `refuse_ended` on the new path only. Nothing checks for, or refuses to
      overwrite, an existing report. The G-ID artifact has exactly that guard (stages.py:1186-1193);
      reports do not.
      
      **Run**
      - I copied step10-dryrun's emu.py, nomlx.py, probes.py and probes2.py to check2/refute-
      reportcollide/ and ran probes2.py with the venv Python. Output is in check2/refute-
      reportcollide/out.txt.
