      `{adjudication: ckpt/OLMo-sft_campaign_adjudication.json, checkpoint: ckpt/OLMo-
      sft_campaign.json}`.

      has sha16 6920b8dd1644d70d and lists only `{checkpoint: ckpt/OLMo-sft_campaign_podB.json}`.
      The probe prints `pod-A report overwritten: True`.
      - The pod-A checkpoint and its adjudication excerpt stay in ckpt/ with no report beside them.
      
      **Why minor**
      - No verdict-relevant number or contrast pick is affected.
      - Under m:251 / rule 27 the pod-A report should already be committed, so git history keeps it.
      The loss is the working-tree pointer, plus the file itself if the pod died before that commit.
      - The pod-A checkpoint survives and holds the state the report is derived from. Nothing
      regenerates the report from it, though, because the old path is refused on any other pod.
      - At step 4 the scripts/m_sessions.py stage subcommands are refused, so this can only be
      reached through the library API (`run_campaign(ckpt_path=...)`). It is a latent defect in the
      package.
      
      The suggested fix fits the existing pattern: derive the report name from the checkpoint stem,
      as `adjudication_path` does, or refuse to overwrite a report whose `files.checkpoint` names a
      different checkpoint.

      run's. It takes no parameter for an earlier proposal; its signature is unit, stage,
      prior_ckpt, prior_adjudication, proposal_sha256, require_real.
      - A non-stub run writes only under REPO/data/m (`_out_root_guard`, :255-268), so G-TIME's
      checkpoint path is fixed. `refuse_ended` refuses that ended checkpoint.
      
      **What I ran** (check2/refute-step10-amend/amend.py, output in amend_out.txt):
      - I made a copy of v1.11 with one dated status line appended, in place under the same name.
      This follows the PROPOSAL-g, -h, -j and -k precedents: `git log --diff-filter=M` shows each
      was edited after its commit.
      - On the copy, `spec_problems` returns []. A banner-neutral amendment passes startup but moves
      the sha (42ab36ec... to 5f8c3c74...).
      - I wrote a G-TIME PASS checkpoint with real-shaped provenance under the old sha.
      `prior_gate('Q-F0','id', ..., proposal_sha256=<old>)` returns PASS. Under the amended sha it
      raises "PRIOR STAGE UNDER ANOTHER PROPOSAL".
      - `refuse_ended` on that checkpoint says: "a finished gate stage is never resumed, re-run or
      set aside on another pod".
      - `spec_problems` on the archived (absent) v1_11 path returns "cannot read ...
      FileNotFoundError".
      - This agrees with the lens's probe9_2.txt.
      
      The scope is wider than the finding states. Any byte edit to the governing proposal after step
      10 refuses every later stage, not only a rename to v1.12. The licence forces the run's
      `proposal_sha256` to be the new file's, and `prior_gate` then refuses every completed prior.
      The stages.py selftest asserts the "another proposal" refusal (:2416), so the strictness is
      deliberate. It was added on a confirm-round finding (confirm_result.json). Neither the
      proposal nor the code declares a procedure for an amendment: §2.7's resume identity names the
      document's hash only for G-CAL.
      
      **Overstated parts:**
      1. The quoted text "never resumed, re-run or restarted on another pod or path" is the STOP and
      campaign branch (resume.py:611-616). A PASS gate stage gets the :617-620 text, which also says
      "never ... re-run", so the substance holds.
      2. Moving the G-ID artifact aside is not forbidden by the code's messages. The refusal says
      only "never overwritten" (:1193). The PIN at stages.py:1186-1187 declares that "a re-run after
      a re-specification starts with the old artifact moved aside by the operator". Only the G-TIME
      re-run conflicts with the code's own text.
      
      **Severity:** minor. The refusal fails closed and changes no verdict. It depends on an
      amendment that §8 does not plan; m's own practice carries residues as dated notes in RESULTS,
      not as proposal edits. The remedy is the seat's decision. If in-place amendments are expected
      mid-chain, the rubric's "a refusal that would STOP a healthy run at a later step" would make
      it substantive.
    DISPOSITION: FIXED by the code option (round 3): identity_extra['proposal_file'] (required on non-stub) replaces the hard-coded v1_11 path at run time; an optional identity_extra['prior_proposal_sha256s'] lets the governing lock re-adopt stages run under an earlier proposal. FLAG for the seat: that list is operator-supplied from the lock; its use needs the seat's sign-off.

  backend Y, gets "PATH MISMATCH" with 0 calls and the end still pending. Back on the original backend it re-

  docstring's "re-finish-only on any pod and at any invocation" (`stages.py:70`) does not hold for a cached-path
  stage after a backend change. On the laptop an OS update is a backend change and cannot be rolled back, so a
  re-finish there would need a new G-ID first.
  - **NOT APPLIED behaves as reported:** the re-finish completes, then exits; the ruling applies on the next
  invocation. It needs your decision.

# PART 5 -- ROUND 4 (Code's own edit to stages.py for the verifier's two new items and the PATH MISMATCH gap; tests and an
# independent review by one agent, verbatim report)
####################################################################################################
  Round 4. Code's own edit to valence_core/m/stages.py, made for the round-3 verifier's two new items (V1: a
  fired campaign look printed its bar messages before its pending end was on disk; V2: END PENDING MISMATCH's
  "nothing written" wording) and for the disclosed PATH MISMATCH gap (a cached-path stage with an end pending
  could not re-finish after a backend change).
  (a) A fired look's messages go to the finish's lines (_pause_lines) and print only after the end is persisted;
  a re-finish prints them again; a silent look prints at once.
  (b) _locked(name, extra, admitted, cpath): where the lock's path differs from this backend's licence and an
  end is pending at the checkpoint, the locked path is returned (a PATH line is printed) and the invocation only
  re-finishes; refinish_only refuses PATH MISMATCH if no end is pending once loaded.
  (c) The END PENDING MISMATCH wording.
  An independent agent reviewed the edit and added tests. Its findings:
  - (b) and (c) are correct.
  - (a) had one reachable defect. A terminal campaign whose end look IS its last recorded look re-announced that
  look's bar messages (FIRED included) on a re-finish, where the first finish printed the END LOOK line. Fixed:
  _pause_lines returns the END LOOK line when end_look carries end_is_look, and _campaign_end's terminal branch
  takes its lines from _pause_lines, so both finishes print from one source.
  - Minor: run_gate_stage's refinish_only refusal ran after a checkpoint write. It is moved straight after
  load_or_init, before any write, as in the campaign driver.
  - Left as is (cosmetic or unreachable): the PATH line prints before load; in a race where the pending end
  vanishes between the read and the load, load_or_init could still set a checkpoint aside with --resume;
  _pending_on_disk duplicates resume.pending_at.
  Checks added: 1 in stages._selftest and 8 in the m_sessions integration selftest:
  - a fired look killed at its pending write announces nothing and its re-finish announces it once;
  - a silent look prints at once;
  - every campaign end kind, killed with its end pending, re-finishes with 0 generate calls, the killed run's
  lines plus the re-finish's lines equalling the uninterrupted run's lines;
  - a fired look prints exactly once per finish;
  - END LOOK is announced at the finish and at the re-finish;
  - cached and uncached Qwen stages with an end pending re-finish across a licence mismatch;
  - the same mismatch without a pending end is still refused before any launch;
  - refinish_only with no end pending is refused with no write.
  Mutation pass: 14 mutants, all killed. The selftest is 628/628 on two runs; check_call_sites returns []; the
  forbidden-string grep is clean; the schedules are unchanged.
  Evidence: scratchpad step4/check4/ (exp1.out, stages.diff, m_sessions.diff, final_selftest_*.txt, mut/).
